# Isaf al-Talib fi Jami al-Matalib (The Student's Aid in All Demands)

## Introduction to the Book

Isaf al-Talib fi Jami al-Matalib is a work of religious instruction, written as a continuous treatise in the voice of its author, who addresses the reader directly as one who seeks and who speaks as one who has gathered. No name for that author is recorded here, and this edition does not supply one.

The book begins where belief begins, with the eight levels of faith, and with three questions put by the King of Rum. From there it turns to the conduct that faith requires: the greeting of peace and its grammar, the standing of guardians and the limits of praise, the claims of parents, of kin, and of the neighbor at one's side, and what a person must learn and what a person must conceal. The larger part of the book is then given to practice. Purification comes first — water, intention, the washing place, major ritual impurity, the cleanliness of bodies and things — and after it prayer, in its hours, its call, its place, its leader, its shape, its lapses, and its forms for the sick, the traveler, the eclipse, and the night vigil. Burial follows, then the wealth that is not yours, then fasting and the quarrel over the crescent.

The text cites earlier books by short names, and the reader should note that the same short name may serve more than one work: Mukhtasar stands for both Mukhtasar al-Musannaf and Mukhtasar al-Athar, and al-Daaim for Daaim al-Islam, which is not Tawil al-Daaim.

## 1. The Eight Levels of Faith

Through Him I seek help, upon Him I rely, and He is sufficient for me.

> وَبِهِ نَسْتَعِينُ وَعَلَيْهِ أَتَوَكَّلُ وَهُوَ حَسْبِي وَكَفَى
> وَمَا تَوْفِيقِي إِلَّا بِاللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ تَوَكَّلْتُ وَإِلَيْهِ أُنِيبُ وَأُفَوِّضُ أَمْرِي إِلَى اللَّهِ إِنَّ اللَّهَ بَصِيرٌ بِالْعِبَادِ وَلَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّةَ إِلَّا بِاللَّهِ الْعَلِيِّ الْعَظِيمِ

Praise belongs to God — who fulfills the hope of every seeker of truth, guidance, and certainty; who makes the features of religion clear to those He has guided; who has made His saints into ladders of salvation for all who aspire to climb toward true life. I praise Him and bear witness that there is no god but He, before whose greatness all creation stands humbled. And I bear witness that Muhammad is His servant and His messenger, sent with the finest of laws and creeds, whose noble character encompassed every means by which innovations are expelled.

I send blessings upon his brother, his appointed guardian, and the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali (عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ) — who descends from the line of the sacrifice, Ismail ibn Ibrahim al-Khalil — and who, through his interpretation, dispels the doubts that gather over the outward revelation.

And upon the niche of his light, the locus of his secret and his manifestation, Fatima al-Batul, daughter of Muhammad the Messenger. And upon her eldest son, Abu Muhammad al-Hasan al-Mutahhar; and upon her second son, Abu Abdullah al-Husayn — who grasps the meaning of what lies within the seven oft-repeated verses (السَّبْعُ الْمَثَانِي) of the great Quran — the foremost among those versed in the knowledge of the two paths.

And upon the pure Imams of his descendants and the righteous guides of his lineage — the Princes of the Faithful, the family of Taha and Yasin, virtuous and noble, God's trustees over His revelation through the turning of all ages and eras, the suns of souls and the seas of the great sciences. And upon the one who upholds their command and makes their secret plain — the Imam of Imams, the revealer of what is obscure, the Commander of the Faithful, the firm rope of God, heir of his forefathers and the one who raises up His saints. And upon their chosen callers — the full moons amid darkness — and their chosen trustees, those rivers of knowledge that can never be overcome.

And upon the caller of this age and time from among them — the one who, in his own hour, gave shape to the forms of the people of faith: Jafar (جَعْفَر) ibn Sulayman (سُلَيْمَان) ibn al-Hasan ibn Yusuf (يُوسُف) ibn Sulayman, who stands in the station of his fathers and of the illustrious callers before them, in the Tayyibi cycle, in service to his master Mawlana the Imam al-Tayyib and his pure sons, in the station of Ishaq and his children and of Ismail and his children, upon all of whom be the prayers of the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful. And upon his trusted successor, who was first among them, and upon his deputy, who was second — the dearest and most glorious father, the caller Muhammad ibn al-Fahd. And (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) until the Day of Judgment.

Now, after this praise of God, glorified be He, these thanks to Him, and these prayers upon His messenger, his family, and those who call to them and to Him: a guardian of religion's great boundaries and a firm pillar of the believers — steadfast in his loyalty to the radiant Imams and to those who call to them — did not cease to press his request upon me, day after day, without relenting. I belong to our lord, our master, our father, and our refuge: the most exalted and singular caller, the unmatched and glorious chief, the luminous Sheikh — the holy and dear soul who has no equal in his age, who labored morning and evening to revive the religion of God, glorified be He — the honor of this world and of religion, the successor of his trustworthy father, the proof of Mawlana the Commander of the Faithful, and his overseer over all the people of his dawa: our master Jafar, son of our master Sulayman. May God raise his sanctity in the high chambers of the gardens, grant us his mercy, his intercession, and his approval, extend to us his secret, and pour upon us his favors and his gifts.

And upon the servant of his faithful successor — second to him in the high and firm station, the sword of his father's dawa and that dawa's blade upon the crown of its opponent's head — our lord, our master, our father, and our deliverance: the illustrious caller and noble Sheikh, the reviver of religion and the destroyer of all its enemies, the shining Sheikh and the weighty form, our master the caller Muhammad ibn al-Fahd. May God raise his sanctity in the most glorious station and grant us his mercy, his approval, and his intercession with his guardian and his master.

His request was this: that I gather — for himself and for every believer who seeks religion and desires understanding in the school of the Household of the Messenger of the Lord of the Worlds — what lies scattered through the books of the saints of God (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) and of their learned and illustrious callers. Specifically:

- the paired and authoritative books
- the brief and abridged compositions on matters of the Sharia
- works of similar kind among the expansive treatments of juristic paths and legal opinions

so that what is transmitted in them might be brought near and those paths made easier to travel.

So I sought the good of God, glorified be He, the Most High, the All-Knowing, and of His guardian (ع) and His caller — the guardian of my favor named above, may God raise his sanctity in the chambers of light — and of the guardian of my favor who was his trusted successor and surety, may God raise his sanctity in the illustrious station. I answered his request, gathered what he sought, and began this work to the measure of my effort, my strength, my diligence, and my capacity, alongside the service I owe to those who have granted me their hands and their favors.

Whatever is correct in it comes from the substance of the guardian of my favor, who opens for me the doors that are closed against me, and from the substance of his successor, the guardian of my favor and the owner of my neck. I claim none of it as my own, and I pass each morning and evening in that anxiety. Whatever in it is a slip or an error comes from the shortness of my reach and from my own straying. I entrust my affair to them both and rely upon them in all things, for they are my sufficiency and my surety, and they are the ones who grant me healing from my spiritual ailments.

The composition began on the seventh day of the month of Rajab al-Asabb — which is likewise the seventh among the months of the year, the month that rouses from heedlessness and slumber — in the fifty-first year after the great thousand of the pure Prophetic migration, upon its author be the finest prayers and the purest greeting.

I named this book — which gathers all that has been described above — إِسْعَافُ الطَّالِبِ فِي جَمِيعِ الْمَطَالِبِ: The Student's Aid in All Demands, for that is its scope and its design, just as it is its name. And I plead with the guardian of my favor named above, and with his successor, the guardian of my gratitude, that they set right for me all my affairs, in this path and in every other; that they accept my work through their generosity; that they bring me by their nobility to the good I hope for; that they seal my life with their approval; and that they make my resting place with them good through their bounty and their kindness, by the right of our master Muhammad and his pure family, upon whom be the prayers of God.

> وَاللَّهَ اَعْبُدُ وَبِهِ اَسْتَعِينُ وَهُوَ سُبْحَانَهُ نِعْمَ الْمَوْلَى وَنِعْمَ النَّصِيرُ وَنِعْمَ الْمُعِينُ وَلَا حَوْلَ وَلَا قُوَّةَ إِلَّا بِاللَّهِ الْعَلِيِّ الْعَظِيمِ وَاللَّهُ يَهْدِي مَنْ يَشَاءُ إِلَى صِرَاطٍ مُسْتَقِيمٍ

God alone do I worship, and from Him do I seek help. He, glorified be He, is the best of masters, the best of defenders, and the best of helpers. There is no power and no strength except through God, the Most High, the Most Great, and God guides whomever He wills to a straight path.

I turn now to the mention of faith as it is set down in the Book of Guardianship (كِتَابُ الْوِلَايَةِ).

I find it stated in some of the noble books:

> اِعْلَمْ يَا أَخِي إِنَّ الْإِيمَانَ ثَمَانُ مَرَاتِبَ
>
> "Know, my brother, that faith has eight levels."

The first of those levels is named in these words:

> أَوَّلُهَا الْإِحْسَانُ بِالْوَالِدَيْنِ ، وَبِذِي الْقُرْبَى ، وَالْيَتَامَى ، وَالْمَسَاكِينِ ، وَالْجَارِ ذِي الْقُرْبَى ، وَالْجَارِ الْجُنُبِ ، وَالصَّاحِبِ ذِي الْجَنْبِ ، وَابْنِ السَّبِيلِ وَمَا مَلَكَتْ أَيْمَانُكُمْ
>
> "The first of them is charity toward parents, toward relatives, toward orphans and toward the needy, toward the neighbor who is a relative and the neighbor who is nearby, toward the companion at one's side, toward the traveler, and toward those whom your faith obligates you to care for."

This is established in the Book of God, the Exalted.

He then said:

> وَلَيْسَ مَنْ تَقَلَّدَ عِبَادَةَ اللهِ وَدَعْوَةَ أَهْلِ الْبَيْتِ عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ بِغَيْرِ عِلْمٍ وَلَا عَمَلٍ يَقَعُ عَلَيْهِ اسْمُ الْإِيمَانِ وَلَا لَهُ عِنْدَ اللهِ ذِمَامٌ وَلَا عِنْدَ وَلِيِّهِ عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ عَهْدٌ وَلَا إِكْرَامٌ لِأَنَّهُ قَدْ خَرَجَ مِنْهُ

Whoever takes upon himself the worship of God and the call of the People of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) without knowledge and without action cannot rightly bear the name of faith. He has no bond with God, and with His guardian (ع) he holds neither covenant nor honor, for he has departed from it.

I turn now to the distinction between faith and Islam — what each of them encompasses, and to a mention of what lies beyond.

Our lord and master, the trust of the Imam, the banner of Islam — may God exalt his sanctity and grant us his intercession and his company — said, in the fourth of his noble councils known as the Mustansiriyya Councils — designated the Councils of Exposition and the Councils of Wisdom, may the peace of God be upon their author:

> وَقَدْ تَقَدَّمَ الْقَوْلُ إِنَّ أَئِمَّةَ الدِّينِ عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ سَبْعَةٌ يَتَرَدَّدُونَ فِي الْعَالَمِ كَأَيَّامِ الْجُمُعَةِ إِلَى حِينِ قِيَامِ الْقَائِمِ وَالْحُجَجُ الْاِثْنَى عَشَرَ مُنْبَثَّةٌ فِي الْعَالَمِ فَهَذِهِ مَعْرِفَةُ جُمْلَةِ حُدُودِهَا

It has already been said that the Imams of religion (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) are seven, recurring in the world as the days of the week recur, until the rising of the one who will arise, while the twelve proofs are spread throughout the world. This is the knowledge of the sum of its limits.

By this he meant — may God exalt his sanctity — the limits of the testimony. As for fulfilling the rights owed to those limits, that consists of upholding seven obligations of religion and twelve practices incumbent upon the God-fearing.

The seven obligations are:

1. Guardianship (الْوِلَايَة)
2. Purity (اَلطَّهَارَة)
3. Prayer (الصَّلَاة)
4. Almsgiving (اَلزَّكَاة)
5. Fasting (اَلصَّوْم)
6. Pilgrimage (الْحَجّ)
7. Struggle (اَلْجِهَاد)

The twelve practices are:

1. Honoring parents
2. Maintaining the ties of kinship
3. Protecting the neighbor
4. Enjoining what is good
5. Forbidding what is wrong
6. Truthfulness in one's home
7. Good conduct with one's spouse
8. Kindness to those in one's care
9. Spreading the greeting of peace
10. Feeding others
11. Keeping the bond among the brethren
12. Visiting the sick

These twelve are not obligatory upon women.

Our master the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his four hundred precepts:

> اِفْتَرَقَ بَنُو إِسْرَائِيلَ عَلَى اثْنَتَيْنِ وَسَبْعِينَ فِرْقَةً وَسَتَفْتَرِقُ هَذِهِ الْأُمَّةُ عَلَى ثَلَاثٍ وَسَبْعِينَ فِرْقَةً وَاحِدَةٌ فِي الْجَنَّةِ وَسَايِرُهَا فِي النَّارِ
>
> "The Children of Israel divided into seventy-two sects, and this community will divide into seventy-three sects. One of them is in the Garden, and the rest of them are in the Fire."

## 2. Three Questions from the King of Rum

I turn now to the guardianship of the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), and to his love and his station.

Our master and Mawlana, the long-serving preacher Jafar ibn Mansur al-Yaman — may God exalt him, sanctify him, and grant us his intercession and his blessing — said in the Book of the Foster-Suckling that Muhammad is His prophet, and that God says of him:

> فَإِنَّ ٱللَّهَ هُوَ مَوْلَىٰهُ وَجِبْرِيلُ وَصَلِحُ ٱلْمُؤْمِنِينَ
>
> "Indeed God is his master, and Gabriel, and the righteous of the believers."

By this is meant the Imam of the believers, who is 'Ali ibn Abi Talib, the Imam of every believing man and every believing woman. And His words:

> وَكَانَ أَبُوهُمَا صَلِحًۭا
>
> "And their father was righteous."

By this is meant an Imam. And His words:

> أَنَّ ٱلْأَرْضَ يَرِثُهَا عِبَادِىَ ٱلصَّلِحُونَ
>
> "The earth is inherited by My righteous servants."

By this are meant the Imams from among His servants.

Mawlana the Imam al-Muizz li-Din Allah (ع) said in the Friday sermon, likening himself in the physical world to a pen in the spiritual world. He set four ranks beneath it, each one preceded by a spiritual rank that guides it toward the rank beyond. In this way he established the foundation facing the tablet, engraving into it what he derived from the spiritual world, just as a pen engraves upon a tablet.

I now record the answers given by the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), to questions he was asked, and what followed from them.

It is stated in the book Uns al-Afidah al-Muniba, in the account of some of the cases of the Commander of the Faithful 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) and his remarkable rulings:

The king of Rum sent a messenger to Medina with a sum of money and instructed him: "Go to Muhammad. If he does not agree with you, then ask about his successor, and put to him proof." The messenger was to ask three questions, and if he received answers, he was to hand over the money.

The man arrived in Medina and was brought before the Messenger of God (ع). He asked about his successor and was directed to Abu Bakr. Abu Bakr was brought forward, and the man asked him: "Tell me what is not God, what is not with God, and what God does not know." Abu Bakr grew angry and said to him: "Woe to you. By saying this you have added to your disbelief." The man left his presence. He was then directed to 'Umar, who responded in much the same way.

Then 'Abdullah ibn Abbas said: "What kind of men are these? They could not answer his questions, and yet they did not simply say, We do not know.' Instead they accused him of knowing falsehood. I know who knows the answer." He was asked, "Who is he?" He said, "'Ali ibn Abi Talib." They said, "Take us to him," and they all set out together with the man and knocked on his door.

The Commander of the Faithful (ع) came out to them with a pen behind his ear and his finger stained with ink. 'Abdullah ibn Abbas told him the story, and he (ع) said to the man: "Ask what you wish." The man said: "Tell me what is not God's." He said: "God has no partner." He said: "Tell me what is not with God." He said: "Injustice." He said: "Tell me what God does not know." He said: "That the Messiah is the son of God. For God Almighty, glorified and exalted be His name, has said:

> أَتُنَبِّـُٔونَ ٱللَّهَ بِمَا لَا يَعْلَمُ

'Do you inform God of what He does not know?'"

The meaning is that it does not exist, and God has no knowledge of what does not exist.

The man said: "I bear witness that there is no god but God, that Muhammad is the Messenger of God, and that you are his guardian." He then handed over the money, and 'Ali (ع) gave it to al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) and told them: "Go and distribute it among the Muslims," and they did as he commanded.

It is also narrated from Abu al-Jarud, from al-Harith al-Awar, that while the Commander of the Faithful (ع) was in al-Rahba with the people pressing around him — some seeking a ruling, others seeking help — a man stood and addressed him: "Peace be upon you, O Commander of the Faithful, and the mercy and blessings of God." The Commander of the Faithful (ع) looked at him and said: "And upon you peace, and the mercy and blessings of God. Who are you?" He said: "A man from your flock and from the people of your country."

He said to him, "Are you from my flock or from the people of my country? If you had greeted me for a single day, you would not be hidden from me." Then he said to those around him: "Do you know this man?" No one knew him. So he said to the man, "These are the people of your country, and they do not know you. Had I seen you for a single day, you would not be hidden from me." The man said, "Safety, O Commander of the Faithful." He said, "Have you committed any transgression in this land of mine since you entered it?" He said, "No." He said, "Perhaps during the days of war?" He said, "Yes." He said, "When war lays down its burdens, there is no harm in it."

The man said: "Muawiya sent me to you and commanded me to come to you unannounced, to ask you about things that Ibn al-Asfar sent to him to inquire about. He says to him: if you are the rightful guardian of this matter and the claimant of the caliphate after Muhammad, then answer me these things, for if you answer me I will follow you and send you the tribute. And when the messenger saw that he had no answer to them, he sent me to you to ask you about them."

He said, "And what are they?" The man said:

- How great is the distance between truth and falsehood?
- How great is the distance between heaven and earth?
- How great is the distance between east and west?
- What is this galaxy?
- What is the rainbow?
- What is the darkening in the moon?
- What is the first thing that appeared on the face of the earth?
- What is the first thing that stirred upon it?
- What is the spring to which the souls of the polytheists flee?
- What is the hermaphrodite?
- What are the ten things, each harder than the last?

The Commander of the Faithful (ع) said to him: "God has destroyed the son of the liver-eater. How astray he is, and how astray are those with him. By God, he freed his slave girl and did not do well to marry her. God judge between me and this nation, who severed my ties of kinship, wasted my days, denied me my right, diminished the greatness of my station, and joined together in contending against me."

Then he said, "Bring me al-Hasan, al-Husayn, and Muhammad." They came to him. He said to the man, "O brother of the people of Syria, these are the sons of the Messenger of God (ع)," and he gestured toward al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), "and this is my son," and he gestured toward Muhammad (رَضِيَ اللَّهُ عَنْه), "so ask whichever of them you wish." The Syrian said he wished to ask al-Hasan (ع). The Commander of the Faithful (ع) said to him, "Ask him," and he asked him all of those questions.

Al-Hasan (ع) said: "O brother of the people of Syria, the distance between truth and falsehood is four fingers. What your eyes see is truth, and with your ears you may hear a great deal of falsehood." The Syrian said, "You have spoken truly, may God set you aright."

He said: "Between heaven and earth lies the prayer of the oppressed and the reach of sight. Whoever says otherwise has lied." He said, "You have spoken truly."

He said: "Between east and west lies the sun's course in a single day. You look at it as it rises and you look at it as it sets. Whoever says otherwise has lied." He said, "You have spoken truly."

He said: "As for this galaxy, it is the lamps of the sky, and from it the pouring water descends. As for the rainbow, it is the bow of God and a safeguard from drowning. As for the darkening in the moon: the moon's light was once like the light of the sun, but God blotted it out. God, mighty and majestic, said regarding that:

> وَجَعَلْنَا اللَّيْلَ وَالنَّهَارَ آيَتَيْنِ فَمَحَوْنَا آيَةَ اللَّيْلِ وَجَعَلْنَا آيَةَ النَّهَارِ مُبْصِرَةً

'And We made the night and the day two signs, and We erased the sign of the night and made the sign of the day visible.'

As for the first thing that appeared on the face of the earth, it is the valley of Dab. As for the first thing that stirred upon the face of the earth, it is the palm tree. As for the spring to which the souls of the polytheists flee, it is a spring called Salma. As for the hermaphrodite, it is a human being of whom it is not known whether the person is a woman or a man; so the person is examined: if a man, he has the emission of a man; and if a woman, she menstruates or her breasts develop. Otherwise the person is told to urinate against a wall: if the urine strikes the wall, it is a man, and if it falls back as the urine of a camel falls back, it is a woman. As for the ten things, each harder than the last: the hardest thing God created is stone; harder than stone is iron, by which stone is cut; stronger than iron is fire; stronger than fire is water; stronger than water is cloud; stronger than cloud is wind; stronger than wind is the angel; stronger than the angel is the Angel of Death; more severe than the Angel of Death is death itself; and more severe than death is the command of God, Lord of all worlds."

Then the Syrian said, "I bear witness that you are the son of the Messenger of God (ع), and that 'Ali (ع) is the guardian of Muhammad (ع), and that he has more right to this affair than Muawiya." He wrote these things down and carried them to Muawiya, and Muawiya sent them to Ibn al-Asfar. Ibn al-Asfar said: "I bear witness that these are not from you, and that they come only from the mine of prophecy and the seat of the message. As for you, were you to ask me for a single dirham, I would not give it to you."

It is also narrated from him that he said: 'Umar ibn al-Khattab came to the Commander of the Faithful (ع) and said to him, "O Abu al-Hasan, there are matters I neglected to ask the Messenger of God (ع) about. Do you have anything concerning them?"

He said, "And what are they?" He said: "A man lies down and sees something in his sleep, and when he awakens it is as though he had taken it in his hand; yet he may see something that turns out to be nothing at all. And a man meets another man and loves him without knowing him. And a man meets another man and hates him without knowing him. And a man sees a thing with his eye or hears it and remembers it for a long age, then forgets it at the moment he needs it, then recalls it when he has no need of it."

The Commander of the Faithful (ع) said to him concerning that: "As for what you say about the thing a man sees in his sleep, God, blessed and exalted, said in His noble Book:

> اللَّهُ يَتَوَفَّى الْأَنفُسَ حِينَ مَوْتِهَا وَالَّتِي لَمْ تَمُتْ فِي مَنَامِهَا فَيُمْسِكُ الَّتِي قَضَىٰ عَلَيْهَا الْمَوْتَ وَيُرْسِلُ الْأُخْرَىٰ إِلَىٰ أَجَلٍ مُّسَمًّى

'God takes the souls at the time of their death, and those that have not died, during their sleep. He keeps those for whom He has decreed death and releases the others until an appointed term.'

Every servant who lies down to sleep carries within him a likeness of death. Whatever he sees while resting, with his soul loosened from his body, is true — he is in the kingdom. Whatever he sees as his soul returns is false; that is the delusion of Shaytan. As for what you say about a man seeing another and loving him without knowing him, or hating him without knowing him — God, mighty and majestic, created the souls two thousand years before the bodies, settled them in the air, and they would meet and scent one another as horses scent one another. Whatever souls came to know one another on that day are joined together today and love; whatever souls rejected one another on that day are divided today and hate. As for what you say about a man seeing something with his eye or hearing it, then forgetting it, then recalling it, then forgetting it once more — there is no heart that does not have a film over it, like the film of the moon. When that film passes through the heart, the servant forgets what he has seen and heard."

At that, 'Umar said, "You have spoken truly, O Abu al-Hasan. May God not keep me alive after you, and may I never be in a land where you are not."

## 3. The Grammar of Peace

I turn now to the guardianship of the Imams from the family of the Messenger of Allah (ع), and to the obligation of praying for Muhammad and the family of Muhammad (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) — together with what pertains to that prayer: the good proclaimers, the righteous borders, their stations, the command to return peace, and the reply to the greeting of the book.

In the twenty-third council of the Mustansiriya councils — may God's peace be upon the author of the book — he said: he had finished treating the Sunnah that we had set out to discuss, namely the revelation of peace. Above, it divides into seven sections — those that came from God and from His angels; below, it divides into twelve sections — those that came from human beings.

1. The first — the highest, the most particular, the most exalted, and the most sincere — is the word of God Almighty:

> ⟪سَلَامٌ عَلَيْكُمْ⟫
>
> "Peace be upon you."

2. The second, which carries a figurative quality, is His saying:

> ⟪يَا نُوحُ اهْبِطْ بِسَلَامٍ مِّنَّا⟫
>
> "O Noah, descend in peace from Us."

3. The third, which is more suggestive, is His saying:

> ⟪تَحِيَّتُهُمْ يَوْمَ يَلْقَوْنَهُۥ سَلَمٌۭۚ⟫
>
> "Their greeting on the day they meet Him is peace."

4. The fourth is His saying:

> ⟪وَيُلَقَّوْنَ فِيهَا تَحِيَّةًۭ وَسَلَمًا⟫
>
> "And they will be met therein with greeting and peace."

5. The fifth expresses that it is from the angels in particular:

> ⟪وَالْمَلَائِكَةُ يَدْخُلُونَ عَلَيْهِم مِّن كُلِّ بَابٍ سَلَامٌ عَلَيْكُم⟫
>
> "And the angels enter upon them from every gate: peace be upon you."

6. The sixth is that God and His angels pray for the Prophet (ع):

> ⟪يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا صَلُّوا عَلَيْهِ وَسَلِّمُوا تَسْلِيمًا⟫
>
> "O you who believe, pray for him and submit to him in full submission."

7. The seventh is the word of the Almighty:

> ⟪سَلَامٌ عَلَىٰ آلِ يَاسِينَ⟫
>
> "Peace be upon the family of Ya Sin."

And ياسين is our Prophet Muhammad (ع), and the family of ياسين is the family of Muhammad — آل على (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ).

He said in the twenty-fourth assembly of it:

Know that the revelation of peace is an obligatory Sunnah of Islam, one that speaks of affection and submission, and Salam is one of the names of the One possessed of majesty and honor. You have heard what we have read to you from what it contains regarding the seven sections, and this is followed by the twelve divisions, which concern the greeting exchanged between human beings.

The first of these is the greeting of the people of the right hand to the faithful prophet (ع) and his noble family. He says, and He is the most truthful of speakers:

> ⟪سَلَامٌ عَلَيْكُم بِمَا صَبَرْتُمْ⟫
>
> "Peace be upon you for your patience."

For if they are among the people of the right hand, then (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) from the people of the right hand.

The second is the greeting upon entering the mosque with peace, for the Almighty says:

> ⟪فَإِذَا دَخَلْتُم بُيُوتًا فَسَلِّمُوا عَلَىٰ أَنفُسِكُمْ تَحِيَّةً مِّنْ عِندِ اللَّهِ مُبَارَكَةً طَيِّبَةً⟫
>
> "When you enter houses, greet one another with a greeting from God, blessed and good."

The third is the peace of one who concludes the prayer.

The fourth is the peace of the believer in the face of the ignorant. He said:

> ⟪وَإِذَا خَاطَبَهُمُ الْجَاهِلُونَ قَالُوا سَلَامًا⟫
>
> "And when the ignorant address them, they say: peace."

The fifth through the ninth, as the Sunnah specifies, concern the order of precedence in initiating the greeting:

5. The rider on a camel initiates to the rider on a horse.
6. The rider on a horse initiates to the rider on a donkey.
7. The rider on a donkey initiates to the one walking.
8. The one walking initiates to the one standing.
9. The one standing initiates the greeting and then returns it.

The return of the greeting falls into three kinds: the virtuous reply, the complete reply, and the just reply. The virtuous reply is to greet whoever initiates with something better than what they offered. The complete reply is to respond in kind, as God Almighty said:

> ⟪وَإِذَا حُيِّيتُم بِتَحِيَّةٍ فَحَيُّوا بِأَحْسَنَ مِنْهَا أَوْ رُدُّوهَا⟫
>
> "And when you are greeted with a greeting, return it with something better, or return it as it is."

The just reply applies when responding to dhimmis, for they do not initiate the greeting. If one of them does, however, the response is "And upon you." The reason is that some of them were reported to deceive Muslims in their greetings by saying ⟪السَّامُ عَلَيْكُم⟫ — "Death be upon you" — in place of ⟪السَّلَامُ عَلَيْكُم⟫. The Sunnah therefore prescribed responding with "And upon you," which is a just reply: whoever said something good received its like, and whoever said something bad received its like.

Our master Amir al-Muminin, the Imam al-Muizz li-Din Allah (ع), said in his prayer on Sunday, after praying for the higher borders: "O God, reach the lower borders, the five — the spokesman, the foundation, the culmination, the successor, and the wing." And he said (ع) in his prayer on Saturday: "O God, upon the seven days and the twelve months, send the purest, most honorable, and most noble of prayers."

Some of the saints of the religion, may God sanctify their souls, said in praying for the borders, in his prayer on Sunday: "O God, reach the proclaimers and their gates, the imams of their stations, their proofs, and their preachers." And he said in his supplication on Monday: "O Allah, pray for the seven proclaimers, the seven culminations, the seven caliphs, and the twelve successors — the purest prayer, the most growing, the most honorable, and the highest. And admit us into all the good into which You have admitted them, and spare us from every evil from which You have spared them." And he said in his supplication on Tuesday: "Reach Your manifest borders and Your righteous guardians — the spokesmen who call to Your light, the proven foundations of the truth of Your religion, the culmination, the successor, and the wing, through whom the gates of Your mercy were opened." And he said in his prayer on Wednesday: "O God, upon them and upon their gates, their proofs, and their preachers, send the best of Your holy prayers." And he said in his prayer on Thursday: "O God, bless the days and months, the hours of the day and the hours of the night, both the manifest and the hidden." And he said in his prayer on the Sabbath: "Dedicate to Muhammad, O God, and to the Imams of his station and the proofs of their ages, many times over what You have prayed for them all."

And our master Amir al-Muminin (ع) said in his Four Hundred Counsels: "When someone greets you, return the greeting with something better than it, or return it in kind. Shake hands with your enemy if he dislikes it, for God has commanded His servants to do so, as the Almighty says:

> ⟪ادْفَعْ بِالَّتِي هِيَ أَحْسَنُ فَإِذَا الَّذِي بَيْنَكَ وَبَيْنَهُ عَدَاوَةٌ كَأَنَّهُ وَلِيٌّ حَمِيمٌ⟫

'Repel with that which is better, and then the one between whom and you there was enmity will become as though he were a close friend.' And he said therein: 'If your brother says, "May God greet you with peace," then say: You are the peace of God, and the finest of abodes and stations.'"

I cite here from the book of questions — composed by the late Sheikh Shamoun ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi (رض) — and of answers, which are from our master Badr al-Din, our master Hasan (حَسَن) ibn our master Idris ibn Sayyidna al-Hasan ibn Sayyidna Abdullah al-Alaf, may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession and his companionship.

The question: "What do you say — you who purify our souls of the whispering of Shaytan that clings to them — regarding the offering of peace to sheikhs, teachers, and those close to them? Most of the people of India do not proclaim peace aloud in their presence; instead, they bow or lower their heads, intending thereby to greet them. Some say this resembles the practice of unbelievers. O our master, favor us with an answer in this matter that leaves the objectors with nothing to say."

The answer: "Let peace be proclaimed aloud, for that is better."

I also find therein the following question: "What do you say, O you who have illuminated the jewels of our souls with the light of knowledge, regarding the returning of a greeting to a Muslim — must it be in a measure that he hears it, or not? And is it obligatory upon the one returning the greeting to reply when he hears the Muslim's salutation, or not? As for sneezing: is the blessing obligatory upon the one who blesses when he hears the sneezer say 'Praise be to God,' or merely upon hearing the sneeze itself? And must the blessing be loud enough that the sneezer hears it, or not?"

The answer: "This is a courtesy and not an obligation. One may do it as one wishes, and there is no fault upon him if he does not — except in returning a greeting in general, whether aloud or silently."

## 4. Guardians, Proofs, and the Limits of Praise

I turn now to what has been transmitted from the Imams of the family of Muhammad (ع). Our master Jafar bin Mansour al-Yemen (رض) said, in the book of the Esoteric Nursing, commenting on the words of God:

> أَنَّ ٱلْأَرْضَ يَرِثُهَا عِبَادِىَ ٱلصَّلِحُونَ

The earth is inherited by My righteous servants.

By this, he taught, are meant the Imams among His servants. He said likewise concerning the words of God:

> وَنُرِيدُ أَن نَّمُنَّ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ ٱسْتُضْعِفُوا۟ فِى ٱلْأَرْضِ وَنَجْعَلَهُمْ أَئِمَّةًۭ وَنَجْعَلَهُمُ ٱلْوَرِثِينَ

And We wished to be gracious to those who were made weak in the land, and to make them Imams, and to make them the heirs.

By "those who were made weak" he means the descendants of Ali ibn Abi Talib. They were made weak in the land — that is, in the very person of Ali ibn Abi Talib himself — for he is the proof of Muhammad, and the earth is his likeness. And our master al-Muizz li Din Allah (ع) said in his supplication of Sunday:

> ثُمَّ نَصَبَ مِنْ دُونِ الْأَسَاسِ الْمُتِمِّ بِاِزَاءِ اِسْرَافِيلَ

Then he appointed, beneath the Foundation, the Terminus, set facing اسرافيل.

I mention next the stations of the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), their conditions, their innocence of whoever sets them in a place other than their own place, and the charge of unbelief against whoever transgresses the bounds concerning them.

Maulana Imam al-Muizz li Din Allah (ع) spoke in his supplication of Sunday the words already cited, appointing beneath الأساس the Terminus — المتم — set facing اسرافيل. And Maulana Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his supplication known as the supplication of victory and awe:

> وَبِالسِّتَّةِ الْمَأْثُورَةِ وَبِالسَّبْعَةِ الْمَنْظُورَةِ

And by the six that are transmitted, and by the seven that are beheld.

And he said in it:

> وَبِالتِّسْعِ الْآيَاتِ وَبِالْعَشْرِ الْكَلِمَاتِ

And by the nine signs, and by the ten words.

And he said in it:

> وَبِالْإِحْدَى وَالْعِشْرِينَ

And by the twenty-one.

And he said in it:

And by the seven Imams, and the seven successors, and the seven witnesses, and the seven substitutes.

And he (ع) said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> وَبِالسَّبْعَةِ الْأُمَّاءِ وَالسَّبْعَةِ الْخُلَفَاءِ وَبِالسَّبْعَةِ الْأَشْهَادِ وَبِالسَّبْعَةِ الْأَبْدَالِ

> مَنْ تَمَسَّكَ بِنَا لَحِقَ وَمَنْ تَمَسَّكَ غَيْرَ طَرِيقِنَا مُحِقَ

Whoever holds fast to us has joined the truth, and whoever holds fast to a way other than ours is undone.

I mention next the stations of the proofs of the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), their preachers, their hudud, and their followers.

Maulana Imam al-Muizz li Din Allah (ع) said in his supplication of Sunday:

> وَنَصَبَ مِنْ دُونِ الْمُتِمِّ اللَّاحِقَ بِاِزَاءِ مِيكَائِيلَ وَنَصَبَ مِنْ دُونِ اللَّاحِقِ الْجَنَاحَ بِاِزَاءِ جِبْرَئِيلَ

And he appointed beneath the Terminus the After, set facing Michael, and he appointed beneath the After the Wing, set facing Gabriel.

Then he said:

> فَكَانَ هَؤُلَاءِ الثَّلَاثَةُ فِي الْأَدَاءِ إِلَى الْمُسْتَجِيبِينَ عَنِ النَّاطِقِ وَالْأَسَاسِ فِي الْعَالَمِ الْجِسْمَانِيِّ كَمَا كَانَتِ الثَّلَاثَةُ فِي الْأَدَاءِ عَنِ الْأَصْلَيْنِ الَّذِينَ هُمَا الْقَلَمُ وَاللَّوْحُ فِي الْعَالَمِ الرُّوحَانِيِّ

By this he (ع) means the Grandfather (الْجَدّ), the Opener (الْفَتْح), and the Imagination (اَلْخَيَال) — that is, Israfil, Michael, and Gabriel. He (ع) also said in his supplication of Friday:

> وَبِالْحُدُودِ الَّتِي هِيَ النَّاطِقُ وَالْأَسَاسُ وَالْمُتِمُّ وَاللَّاحِقُ وَالْجَنَاحُ وَالْمَأْذُونُ وَالْمُسْتَجِيبُ وَالْحُجَجِ الْأَثْنَىْ عَشَرَ الْمُرَتَّبَةِ فِي الْجَزَائِرِ الِاثْنَىْ عَشَرَ

And by the hudud — which are:

- the Speaker
- the Foundation
- the Terminus
- the After
- the Wing
- the Authorized
- the Respondent

— and the twelve proofs ranked across the twelve regions.

This is as God Almighty has said:

> لوّاحة للبشر عليها تسعة عشر

لَوَّاحَةٌ لِّلْبَشَرِ عَلَيْهَا تِسْعَةَ عَشَرَ — Searing to mankind; over it are nineteen.

And Maulana Amir al-Muminin (ع) said in the supplication of victory and awe:

> وَبِالثَّمَانِيَةِ الْحَافِّينَ بِعَرْشِكَ يَا رَبَّ الْعَالَمِينَ

And by the eight who encircle Your Throne, O Lord of the worlds.

And he said in it:

> وَبِالْإِحْدَى عَشَرَ السَّاجِدِينَ وَبِالِاثْنَى عَشَرَ الْمَعْدُودِينَ الرَّاكِعِينَ

And by the eleven who prostrate, and by the twelve counted ones who bow.

And our master Muhammad bin Tahir (رض) said in a great supplication of his:

> وَأَتَوَسَّلُ إِلَيْكَ اللَّهُمَّ بِأَبْوَابِهِمْ وَحُجَجِهِمْ وَدُعَاتِهِمْ وَمَأْذُونِيهِمْ وَمُسْتَجِيبِي أَدْوَارِهِمْ وَمَنْ يَنْحُو نَحْوَهُمْ وَيَلُوذُ مَلَاذَهُمْ وَيَأْتَمُّ بِهِمْ وَبِتَابِعِيهِمْ أَجْمَعِينَ

I seek nearness to You, O God, through their gates, their proofs, their preachers, their authorized ones, the respondents of their cycles, all who incline toward them, all who take shelter in their shelter, all who take them as guides, and all their followers.

And Maulana the Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> من أراد منكم أن يعلم کیف منزلته عند الله تعالى فلينظر کیف منزلة الله منه عند الذنوب

Whoever among you wishes to know his station with God Almighty, let him look at what station God holds with him at the moment of sin.

And he said in it:

> اَلْمُؤْمِنُ نَفْسُهُ مِنْهُ فِي تَعَبٍ وَالنَّاسُ مِنْهُ فِي رَاحَةٍ

And he said in it:

> أَنَّ أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ لَيَنْظُرُونَ إِلَىٰ مَنَازِلِ شِيعَتِنَا كَمَا يَنْظُرُ أَحَدُكُمْ إِلَىٰ الْكَوْكَبِ الدُّرِّيِّ فِي السَّمَاءِ

I turn now to the prohibition of exaggeration, of falling short, and of what resembles these two.

Maulana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> إِيَّاكُمْ وَالْغُلُوَّ فِينَا قُولُوا إِنَّا عِبَادٌ مَرْبُوبُونَ وَقُولُوا فِي فَضْلِنَا مَا شِئْتُمْ

Beware of exaggeration concerning us. Say that we are servants under a Lord, and say of our excellence whatever you wish.

And he said in it:

> إِذَا تَبَيَّنَ بِكُمُ الْحَقُّ فَلَا تَكُونُوا مَدَافِعَ عَجَالَى فَإِلَيْنَا يَرْجِعُ الْغَالِي وَبِنَا يَلْحَقُ الْمُقَصِّرُ

When the truth has been made plain through you, do not be rash in defending it, for the one who exaggerates will return to us, and we ourselves will raise up the one who falls short.

Next, I record the commandments of the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) to their guardians, and the commandments of their preachers to the Shia.

Maulana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> من احبنا فليعمل عملنا ويستعين بالورع فانه أفضل ما يُستعان به في الدنيا والآخرة ولا تجالسوا لنا عايبا ولا تمدحوننا معلنين عند عدوّنا فيظهر حبنا ويستر بغضنا وتذلوا انفسكم عند سلطانهم ألزموا الصّدق فانه منجاة ارغبوا فيما عند الله وأطلبوا مرضاته وطاعته واصبروا عليها ما اقبح بالمؤمن أن يدخل الجنّة وهو مهنوك الستر لا تعبونا بالطلب والشفاعة لكم يوم القيامة بسبب ما قدمتم ولا تفضحوا أنفسكم عند عدؤكم يوم القيامة ولا تجعلوا أنفسكم عندهم في منزلتهم عند الله بالحقير من الدنيا تمسّكوا بما أمركم الله تعالى به فما بين أحدكم وبين أن يغتبط ويرى ما يحب الآ أن يحضره رسول الله صلى الله عليه وعلى آله وما عند الله خير وأبقى وتأتيه البشارة والله لتقرّن عينه وليحينّ لقاء الله

Whoever loves us, let him do our work and seek help through God-fearing restraint, for it is the finest means by which help can be sought, in this world and the next. Do not sit with anyone who reviles us, and do not praise us openly before our enemy — so that our love may be made plain and hatred of us concealed, and so that you may humble yourselves before their authority. Cling to truthfulness, for it is deliverance. Desire what is with God, seek His good pleasure and His obedience, and be patient in that. How ugly it is for a believer to enter Paradise with his covering torn away. Do not burden us with having to plead and intercede for you on the Day of Resurrection because of what you have sent forward. Do not disgrace yourselves before your enemy on the Day of Resurrection, and do not place yourselves — in their sight — at their own station before God for the sake of some paltry thing of this world. Hold fast to what God Almighty has commanded you. Nothing stands between any one of you and his rejoicing and seeing what he loves except that the Messenger of God should come to him. What is with God is better and more lasting, and the glad tidings will reach him. By God, his eye shall be gladdened, and the meeting with God shall surely come.

And he said in it:

> إِيَّاكُمْ وَالْجِدَالِ فَأِنَّهُ يُورِثُ الشَّكَّ

Beware of disputation, for it bequeaths doubt.

And he said in it:

> اِحْسِبُوا كَلَامَكُمْ مِنْ أَعْمَالِكُمْ يَقِلَّ كَلَامُكُمْ اِلَّا فِي الْخَيْرِ

Reckon your speech as part of your deeds, and your speech will become little save what is good.

And he said in it:

> بَادِرُوا بِعَمَلِ الْخَيْرِ قَبْلَ أَنْ تُشْغَلُوا عَنْهُ بِغَيْرِهِ لِيَكُنْ جُلُّ كَلَامِكُمْ ذِكْرَ اللَّهِ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ اِحْذَرُوا الذُّنُوبَ فَأِنَّ الْعَبْدَ يُذْنِبُ الذَّنْبَ فَيُحْبَسُ عَنْهُ الرِّزْقُ

And he said in it:

> أَفْضَلُ عَمَلِ الْمُؤْمِنِ انْتِظَارُ الْفَرَجِ

The best deed of the believer is awaiting the relief.

And he said in it:

> اَلسَّعِيدُ مَنْ وُعِظَ بِغَيْرِهِ فَاتَّعَظَ

Blessed is the one who is admonished by the case of another and takes the admonition.

I turn next to the affection owed to the Imams of the family of Muhammad (ع), the desires bound up with loyalty to them, and loyalty to their preachers.

It was said in the nineteenth council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon its author:

> فاستمعوا الآن ما نذكره مما أمر الله به من صلة القرابة وفيها سبعة واثنى عشر فأمّا السبعة فصلة من أمر الله بصلته من قرابةٍ رسول الله صلى الله عليه وآله لقوله تعالى قل لا أسألكم عليه أجراً الا المودة في القربى وهم أئمة الدين صلوات الله عليهم وعدّتهم سبعة يترددون كأيام الجمعة

Now listen to what we recount of what God has commanded regarding the ties of kinship — within which there are seven and twelve. As for the seven: this is the tie with those whom God commanded to be joined, from among the kin of the Messenger of God, as God says: "Say, I ask you no reward for it except affection toward my kin." These are the Imams of the religion, and their number is seven, recurring like the days of Friday.

## 5. Parents, Kin, and the Neighbor at Your Side

I turn now to the affection believers owe one another, and to what follows from it.

Maulana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred manners:

> لَا تَحْقِرُوا ضُعَفَاءَ إِخْوَانِكُمْ فَإِنَّهُ مَنِ اِحْتَقَرَ مُؤْمِنًا حَقَّرَهُ اَللَّهُ وَمَقَتَهُ وَلَمْ يَجْمَعْ بَيْنَهُمَا يَوْمَ اَلْقِيَامَةِ إِلَّا أَنْ يَتُوبَ، وَلَا يُكَلِّفُ أَحَدُكُمْ أَخَاهُ اَلطَّلَبَ إِلَيْهِ إِذَا عَرَفَ حَاجَتَهُ، تَزَاوَرُوا وَتَعَاطَفُوا وَتَبَاذَلُوا، وَلَا تَكُونُوا بِمَنْزِلَةِ اَلْمُنَافِقِ اَلَّذِي يَصِفُ مَا لَا يَفْعَلُ.

Do not despise the weak among your brothers, for whoever despises a believer is belittled and detested by God, and the two will not be brought together on the Day of Resurrection unless he repents. Let none of you force his brother to ask when he already knows his need. Visit one another, be tender with one another, and give freely to one another. Do not be like the hypocrite, who describes what he does not do.

He said in it:

> الْمُسْلِمُ مِرْآةُ أَخِيهِ الْمُسْلِمِ، فَإِنْ رَأَيْتُمْ مِنْ أَخِيكُمْ هَفْوَةً فَلَا تَكُونُوا عَلَيْهِ أَلْبًا وَأَرْشِدُوهُ وَانْصَحُوا لَهُ وَتَرَفَّقُوا بِهِ.

> إِيَّاكُمْ وَالْخِلَافَ فَإِنَّهُ مَرُوقٌ، وَعَلَيْكُمْ بِالْفَضْلِ، تَرَافَقُوا وَتَرَاحَمُوا.

The Muslim is the mirror of his Muslim brother. If you see a slip in your brother, do not band together against him; guide him, counsel him, and deal gently with him. Beware of discord, for it leads a person away from the path. Hold fast to generous conduct, keep company with kindness, and show mercy to one another.

He said in it:

> إِيَّاكُمْ وَالْغِيبَةَ فَإِنَّ الْمُسْلِمَ لَا يَغْتَابُ أَخَاهُ، وَقَدْ نَهَى اللَّهُ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ عَنْ ذَلِكَ فَقَالَ: أَيُحِبُّ أَحَدُكُمْ أَن يَأْكُلَ لَحْمَ أَخِيهِ مَيْتًۭا فَكَرِهْتُمُوهُ.

Beware of backbiting, for a Muslim does not speak ill of his brother behind his back. God, mighty and majestic, has forbidden it, saying: Would any of you like to eat the flesh of his dead brother? You would hate it.

He said in it:

> إِذَا لَقِيتُمْ إِخْوَانَكُمُ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ فَصَافِحُوهُمْ وَأَظْهِرُوا لَهُمُ الْبَشَاشَةَ وَالْبِشْرَ، تَفْتَرِقُوا وَمَا عَلَيْكُمْ مِنَ الْأَوْزَارِ قَدْ ذَهَبَتْ.

When you meet your believing brothers, greet them with a handshake and a bright, welcoming face. By the time you part, whatever burdens you each carried will have lifted.

I also present here the book of questions — the question from the venerable Shaykh Shimon bin Muhammad al-Ghuri al-Hindi (رض), and the answer from our master Badr al-Din, Sayyidina Hassan bin Sayyidina Idris bin Sayyidina al-Hassan bin Sayyidina Abdullah al-Ala. The question concerned a custom widespread among the people of India: some of them ask their neighbors for water, salt, food, and similar provisions, and this is done freely on both sides, each considering it permissible. Those who ask are not unable to purchase such things; they simply do not have them at that moment, and so they turn to whoever does. The question was whether such asking is permitted to them or not. The answer was:

> لَا بَأْسَ بِهِ، هَذَا لِصَفْوِّ الْمَوَدَّةِ وَحُسْنِ الْأُخُوَّةِ.

There is no harm in it. This belongs to the purity of affection and the excellence of brotherhood.

I turn now to the righteousness owed to parents — both the spiritual and the bodily.

He said in the seventeenth council of the Mustansiriya councils, may God's peace be upon its author: the explanation of the seven obligatory rights we mentioned has now been completed. By that is meant the seven that correspond to the seven clauses of the testimony, matching the number of its clauses, which are not subject to dispute. There remain still the twelve practices, corresponding to the number of its letters, in the manner we already defined. The first of these is righteousness toward parents. God Almighty said:

> وَوَصَّيْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ بِوَلِدَيْهِ إِحْسَنًا حَمَلَتْهُ أُمُّهُۥ كُرْهًۭا وَوَضَعَتْهُ كُرْهًۭا

And We have enjoined upon man goodness toward his parents; his mother carried him with hardship and gave birth to him with hardship.

He thus enjoined goodness toward both parents together, yet He laid the mother's deed to the child's account and not the father's. And He said in another place:

> وَوَصَّيْنَا ٱلْإِنسَنَ بِوَلِدَيْهِ حَمَلَتْهُ أُمُّهُۥ وَهْنًا عَلَىٰ وَهْنٍۢ

And We have enjoined upon man goodness toward his parents; his mother carried him in weakness upon weakness.

In both these places, it is the mother's deed that is counted upon the child. Whoever God has distinguished by mention must be distinguished with honor and righteousness.

He said in the eighteenth council of them: We had reached, in the matter of righteousness toward parents, what God counted upon the child from the deed of the mother — a deed that earns her an added rank in righteousness and in dignity. So listen now to what is recited to you: in righteousness toward parents there are seven obligatory parts and twelve manners.

The believer must show righteousness to his father and mother, who are the cause of his bodily form coming into being, and who labored to nourish him with the most beneficial and pleasing of foods until he attained the fullness of what they hoped for him. He must show righteousness to his two parents in religion, who are the cause of his form coming into being in knowledge — they are the Imam of the age (ع) and his proof. And he must show righteousness to the two fathers of this community, who convey to them the divine sciences — they are the Prophet and the legatee, and the Imams from their progeny. It is related from the Messenger of God that he said:

> أَنَا وَأَنْتَ يَا عَلِيُّ أَبَوَا هَذِهِ الْأُمَّةِ

I and you, O Ali, are the two fathers of this community.

The seal of this righteousness is a person's righteousness toward his own soul: that he make the truth plain to it and establish it therein, that he magnify it for the soul and so awaken its longing, that he place fear in it, deal gently with it, deliver it from what would destroy it, and rescue it from what has bound it.

As for the twelve manners in righteousness toward parents, they are that he honor their dwelling place, that he rise for them when he sees them, that he answer their call, that he lower his voice in their presence, that he magnify them and not rebuke them, that he lower to them the wing of humility out of mercy, that he not walk ahead of them in light nor ahead of them in darkness, that he not show any opposition to their command, that he not say to either of them a word of complaint, that he desire life for them in their old age as they desired life for him in his childhood, and that he provide for the needs of each of them when they have need of him, just as they provided for him when he had need of them.

He said in the twenty-fourth council of them: among the obligatory rights there are three rights.

1. The right of God.
2. The right of parents.
3. The right of the child.

God has commanded that they be discharged, and He will not accept them unless they are discharged in full. The right of God is to come out from what God has forbidden and to enter into what God has commanded. The right of parents is thankfulness for what has passed and obedience in what remains. The right of the child is that he be raised with tenderness, nourished with food, and taught the Book and wisdom.

Maulana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred manners:

> مَنْ خَالَفَ وَالِدَيْهِ عَقَّهُمَا

Whoever goes against his parents has been undutiful to them.

I mention next the affection owed to relatives — those of religion and those of the world.

He said in the nineteenth council of the Mustansiriya councils: as for a person's relatives, they are divided into twelve categories, the first of them being:

1. The children, male and female.
2. Then the children of the father by the same mother, who are the brothers of one stock, called الأشقاء (full brothers).
3. And the children of the father by another mother, who are بَنُو الْعَلَّاتِ (بَنُوا الْعَلَّاتِ).
4. And the children of the mother by another father, who are الأخياف (maternal half-brothers), called brothers through the mother.
5. and 6. The grandfathers and grandmothers on the father's side.
7. and 8. The grandfathers and grandmothers on the mother's side.
9. The paternal uncles.
10. The paternal aunts.
11. The maternal uncles.
12. The maternal aunts.

Whoever is related through one of these takes that one's ruling. As for the tie with them that has been commanded, it belongs to those who do good that they keep the tie with them even if the relatives cut them off, that they give to them even if the relatives withhold from them, that they draw near to them even if the relatives push them away, and that they love them even if the relatives abandon them.

Maulana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred manners:

> ولا يمين في قطيعة رحم

لا يمين في قطيعة رحم — there is no binding oath in the severing of kinship.

I turn now to the affection owed to neighbors — both those of religion and those of the world — and to all that follows from it.

I said in the twentieth council of the Mustansiriya councils: building upon what I mentioned of righteousness toward parents and the ties of kinship, among the religious practices set forth earlier, comes the safeguarding of the neighbor. God Almighty said:

> وَٱلْجَارِ ذِى ٱلْقُرْبَىٰ وَٱلْجَارِ ٱلْجُنُبِ وَٱلصَّاحِبِ بِٱلْجَنۢبِ

And the neighbor who is kin, and the neighbor who is a stranger, and the companion at your side.

This subject is divided into seven parts and twelve manners. The first of them:

1. Safeguarding the believing neighbor between whom and you there is kinship; he holds three rights — the right of neighborliness, the right of kinship, and the right of faith.
2. The second is the one between whom and you there is both neighborliness and lineage, but no bond of faith between you; he holds two rights — the right of neighborliness and the right of kinship.
3. The third is the neighbor who is a stranger — that is, the believing neighbor between whom and you there is no kinship; he holds the right of neighborliness and the right of faith.
4. The fourth is the companion at your side — that is, the neighbor who differs from you in religion and creed; he holds the right of neighborliness.
5. The fifth is the companion aboard the ship — the one who sits beside you for the length of your journey; he holds the right of nearness.
6. The sixth is the one who shares your litter.
7. The seventh is the one who sits with you to receive wisdom; his is the most binding sanctity and the most obligatory claim upon you.

As for the twelve manners of neighborliness, they are:

1. That the neighbor not peer into his neighbor's house.
2. That he not listen in on his neighbor's speech within his dwelling.
3. That he not disclose any matter of his neighbor's that comes to his knowledge.
4. That he not let his neighbor hear from his house what his neighbor would hate to hear.
5. That he not show his neighbor what his neighbor would hate to see.
6. That he not withhold a household implement from his neighbor when asked for it.
7. That he not act haughtily toward his neighbor when he himself is of higher station.
8. That he not envy his neighbor when his neighbor is above him.
9. That he help his neighbor when his neighbor seeks his help.
10. That he cover his neighbor's 'awrah (that which should remain concealed) if it becomes exposed.
11. That he cause his neighbor no injury of any kind.
12. That he bear his neighbor's injury with patience.

> فَهَذِهِ أَفْعَالُ قَوْمٍ قَدِ امْتَحَنَ اللَّهُ قُلُوبَهُمُ التَّقْوَى

These are the deeds of a people whose hearts God has tested for piety.

## 6. What Must Be Learned, What Must Be Concealed

I turn now to what is sought in the pursuit of knowledge, in the Quran, and in what belongs with them.

Mawlana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred maxims of conduct:

> لَا صَمْتَ إِلَى اللَّيْلِ اِلَّا فِي ذِكْرِ اللَّهِ ، لَا تَعَرَّبْ بَعْدَ الْهِجْرَةِ

There is no silence until nightfall except in the remembrance of God; and do not turn back to the desert after the migration.

And he said in it:

> لَا يَقْرَأُ الْعَاقِلُ الْقُرْآنَ إِذَا كَانَ عَلَى غَيْرِ وُضُوءٍ حَتَّى يَتَطَهَّرَ لَهُ

The man of sound mind does not recite the Quran while in a state of ritual impurity — not until he has purified himself for it.

I turn now to the one from whom knowledge must be taken, and to the one who must be shunned and whose word refused, and all that belongs with that.

Mawlana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred maxims of conduct:

> لَا يَغْلِبُ عَلَيْكُمُ الْمُرْجِئَةُ بِرَأْيِهَا

Let not the Murjiah overcome you with their opinion.

It is said in the book of questions — which comes from the most exalted Sheikh Shamoun ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi (رض), together with the answers of our master Badr al-Din, Sayyidina Hasan ibn Sayyidina Idris ibn Sayyidina al-Hasan ibn Sayyidina Abdullah al-Anf — as follows.

The question: What do you say, O illuminator of truths for those who deserve them, concerning this matter? Our master Hamid al-Din mentioned in the book al-Hadi wal-Mustahdi, with conclusive proofs; and likewise our master al-Numan mentioned in the book al-Daaim; and the most exalted and most excellent Dai, most perfect in composition, Sayyidina Idris ibn al-Hasan, stated in the book Zahr al-Maani that al-Sadiq (ع) had granted al-Shafii license while he kept it concealed. So, our master, did his condition change as the conditions of al-Mughirah and Abu al-Khattab and their like changed? Or did the ignorant invent a falsehood in claiming his school, as one faction claimed the Imamate of Muhammad ibn al-Hanafiyya and Abdullah ibn Muawiyah, when neither of those two ever claimed it for himself? Be gracious with an answer — brief and sufficient for those who possess insight in religion.

The answer:

> نعم قد كان أطلق له لاقراره فضل أهل البيت عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ لا غير

Yes, he had granted him license, but only because of his acknowledgment of the excellence of the Ahl al-Bayt, and for nothing else.

It is said in the same book of questions.

The question: What do you say, O school of insights and of the inmost thoughts, concerning the saying of the Prophet:

> مَنْ نَظَرَ فِي كِتَابِ أَخِيهِ بِغَيْرِ أِذْنِهِ فَكَأَنَّمَا يَنْظُرُ فِي النَّارِ

Whoever looks into the book of his brother without his permission is as one who looks into the Fire.

Our master: when the people of a school gather together, each freely looks into the others' books without asking permission. What, then, does this saying mean?

The answer:

> هَذَا فِي كِتَابِ الْحَقِيقَةِ لَا يَنْظُرُ الْإِنْسَانُ اِلَّا بِأِذْنِ مَنْ أَطْلَقَ لَهُ ذَلِكَ وَأَمَّا فِي غَيْرِهَا فَأَدَبٌ وَلَيْسَ بِلَازِمٍ

This concerns the book of inner truth — أُطْلِقَ الْإِنْسَانُ — into which no one may look except by the permission of the one who granted him access to it. For all other books, the matter rests on good manners alone and carries no binding obligation.

And it is said in it:

The question: What do you say, O comfort of our eyes, about a man who borrows the Quran or another book from someone and finds in it damage and disorder? Is it permitted for him — or for anyone الا who notices this — to cut away the disorder and set it right without the owner's permission, or not?

The answer:

> لَا بَأْسَ بِأَنْ يُصْلِحَهُ وَلَيْسَ لَهُ لَازِمٌ

There is no بأس in his correcting it, and it is not لازم upon him.

And it is said in it.

The question: What do you say, O gate of our sought goal — which none may reach except through it — concerning a man who has died leaving behind books of the dawah and other goods, and who owed a debt to people that the value of those goods is insufficient to discharge? Are the books to be held in the dawah's treasury, or may they be used to settle his debt?

The answer:

> إِنْ كَانَتِ الْكُتُبُ مِنْ كُتُبِ الدَّعْوَةِ فَلَيْسَ لَهُ ذَلِكَ لِأَنَّهَا لِلدَّعْوَةِ لَا تُورَثُ

If the books are among the books of the dawah, that is not permitted to him, because they belong to the dawah and are not inherited.

And it is said in the questions that come from certain of the sheikhs of India, with the answers from certain of our masters, the callers of Yemen.

The question: Is it permitted for those who have been granted license (الْمَفْسُوحِينَ) to read the benefits of the religion to women who are close kin and to others in a single gathering?

The answer:

> إذا كان بين الرجل والمرأة سترة قراء عليها بحيث تسمع قوله ولا يراها ولا يخلو رجل بامرأة الاّ أن يكون معها غيرها أو معه غيره

If a screen stands between the man and the woman, he may read to her so that she can hear his words, even though he cannot see her. No man may be alone with a woman unless another woman is present with her, or another man is present with him.

Among the questions I have already mentioned, it is said:

> وأمّا ما سألت من العلم الواجب الذي لا يسع جهله فهو ما تعهّد الله عزّ وجل العباد به من معرفته وتوحيده وتنزيهه عن التشبيه والتعطيل والعدم ثم الإقرار برسله أوّلهم وآخرهم والشهادة بالبلاغ لهم والإقرار بمحمد خاتم النبيين وسيد المرسلين والتصديق بما جاء به والإقرار بالأئمة من ذريته الذين أمر الله عزّ وجل بتوليهم وردّ المسألة إليهم ووصل في كتابه طاعته وطاعة رسوله بطاعتهم فقال عزّ وجل أطيعوا الله وأطيعوا الرسول وأولي الأمر منكم ومعرفة إمام الزمان واعتقاد إمامته وولايته والتسليم لأمره والأخذ عنه وأن الله عزّ وجل لم يخل الأرض من حجة له على عباده وإمام يدعوهم إلى طاعته لقوله لا شريك له يوم ندعوا كلّ أناس بإمامهم وقال لمحمد صلى الله عليه وآله إنما أنت منذر ولكل قوم هاد فمن علم هذا وأعتقده وأقرّ به وصدّقه وجب علينا اتباع الدليل واقتفاء اثار الرسول وسؤالهم والأخذ عنهم قال الله أصدق القائلين فاسألوا أهل الذكر إن كنتم لا تعلمون فبقدر ما يعلم المؤمن بعد الإقرار والتصديق ترفع درجّه وتعلو منزلّه قال الله تعالى يرفع الله الذين آمنوا منكم والذين أتوا العلم درجات

You asked about the knowledge that is obligatory — the knowledge no believer may leave unlearned. It is what God, mighty and exalted, has charged His servants to know:

- Knowledge of Him: declaring His oneness, holding Him far above all likeness, far above the stripping away of His attributes, and far above nonexistence.
- Acknowledgment of His messengers — the first and the last of them — and testimony that they conveyed their message.
- Acknowledgment of Muhammad, the Seal of the Prophets and the Master of the Messengers, and affirmation of everything he brought.
- Acknowledgment of the Imams from his progeny — those whom God, mighty and exalted, commanded us to take as our guardians and to whom He commanded that every question be referred. In His Book He joined obedience to Himself and obedience to His Messenger with obedience to them, for He said, mighty and exalted: *Obey God, and obey the Messenger, and those vested with authority among you.*
- Recognition of the Imam of the age: belief in his Imamate and his guardianship, submission to his command, and the taking of knowledge from him.

All of this rests on the reality that God, mighty and exalted, has never left the earth without a proof against His servants and without an Imam calling them to His obedience. He who has no partner said: *On the day We shall call every people by their Imam.* And He said to Muhammad: *You are only a warner, and for every people there is a guide.*

Whoever knows this, believes it, acknowledges it, and affirms it as true — we are bound to follow the guide, trace the footsteps of the Messenger, ask them, and take from them. God, most truthful of all who speak, said: *Ask the people of remembrance, if you do not know.* In proportion to what a believer knows, after acknowledgment and affirmation, his degree is raised and his station elevated. God, exalted, said: *God raises those of you who believe, and those who have been given knowledge, by degrees.*

And Ali, Amir al-Muminin (ع), said:

> قِيمَةُ كُلِّ امْرِئٍ مَا كَانَ يُحْسِنُ مِنَ الْعِلْمِ

The worth of every man is what he has mastered of knowledge.

And the Messenger of God said:

> طَلَبُ الْعِلْمِ فَرِيضَةٌ عَلَى كُلِّ مُسْلِمٍ وَمُسْلِمَةٍ

The seeking of knowledge is an obligation upon every Muslim man and every Muslim woman.

I now address the prohibition of analogy in religion.

Mawlana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred maxims of conduct:

> لا تقيسوا الدين فأنه لا يقاس وسيأتي قوم يقيسون الدين وهم أعداءه وأول من قاس إبليس

Do not measure the religion by analogy, for it cannot be measured. A people will come who measure the religion by analogy — and they are its enemies. The first to reason by analogy was Iblis.

And he said in it:

> إِذَا سَمِعْتُمْ مِنْ حَدِيثِنَا مَا لَا تَعْرِفُونَهُ فَرُدُّوهُ إِلَيْنَا وَقِفُوا عِنْدَهُ تَسْلَمُوا

When you hear something in our discourse that you do not recognize, refer it back to us — stop there — and you will be safe.

I now take up the principle of referring back, in every obscure matter, to God, to His Messenger, to his legatee, to the Imams of his progeny, and to their callers.

Our master al-Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad said in his book Mafatih al-Nimah, and al-Sadiq (ع) has said:

> مَا أَشْتَكَلَ عَلَيْكُمْ شَيْءٌ مِنْ عُلُومِنَا فَرُدُّوهُ إِلَيْنَا وَلَا تَرُدُّوهُ عَلَيْنَا يَعْنِي رُدُّوا إِلَيْنَا لِنُبَرْهِنَ لَكُمْ بُرْهَانَهُ وَنُعَرِّفَكُمْ تَأْوِيلَهُ وَلَا تَرُدُّوهُ عَلَيْنَا بِأَنَّ تُكَذِّبُوا بِهِ وَتَرْجِعُوا عَنْهُ

Whenever anything of our sciences is obscure to you, refer it back to us — do not throw it back at us. That is: bring it to us, so that we may establish its proof for you and make its interpretation known to you; do not throw it back at us by calling it false and turning away from it.

I turn now to the command to conceal knowledge, the secrets, the religion, and the practice of precaution.

Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred maxims of conduct:

> مَنْ أَذَاعَ سِرَّنَا أَذَاقَهُ اللَّهُ بَرْدَ الْحَدِيدِ

Whoever divulges our secret, God will give him the cold of iron to taste.

## 7. Water and Intention: The Anatomy of Ablution

I mention now the circumstances that make ablution (اَلْوُضُوء) necessary.

It is said in the book known as the Book of Question and Answer, arranged according to the order of the doors of Daaim al-Islam, the questions being those of certain teachers of the Indian lands and the answers those of the summoners of the Yemeni lands:

Question: What do you say, O best of those who have cast upon us the lights of his knowledge, regarding a matter over which our minds have labored? It has come in the Book of Purity that a man is forbidden to urinate beneath fruit-bearing trees. O our master, what is intended by these trees — those upon which fruit is actually present, or those whose property it is to bear fruit? Answer: The trees in which fruit is present, or those whose property it is to bear it.

Question: What do you say regarding what has come in the books of the dawah forbidding the greeting of one who is in a state of hadath, and forbidding speech in that state? O our master, if the one in that state rises from that place, having cleansed himself with a stone or with something else, is it permitted for him to speak, and is it permitted to greet him? Answer: Once he has risen, he may be greeted, and he must return the greeting and may speak.

Question: What does our master say regarding a stone or a clod of earth with which a man cleanses himself after urinating, which afterward dries, and which he then uses a second time — is that permitted? Answer: There is no harm in it, provided that no impurity and no moisture are evident upon it.

It is said in the book Mukhtasar al-Musannaf that what nullifies ablution is:

- Sleep
- Whatever issues from the front or rear passage, whether a stone or wind
- An enema
- Fainting
- Insanity
- The disappearance of the glans into the front passage of a living person or a dead one

It is said in the book Tawil al-Daaim that it has likewise come down that if one performing ablution interrupts it, he may build upon what he has already done, so long as the water has not dried upon the limbs already washed.

It is also said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf that whoever has had both hands amputated above the elbows, so that nothing remains of them, the obligation of washing the hands has fallen away from him.

Our master and lord, the Trusted of the Imam, the Banner of Islam (رض), said in his gatherings entitled Majalis al-Bayan and Majalis al-Hikma, known as the Councils of al-Mustansiriyya, in the fifth council:

You have heard what was recited to you, wherein we singled you out with the splendid glad tidings and joined you to the company of those of whom God Almighty said:

> لَهُمُ ٱلْبُشْرَىٰ فِى ٱلْحَيَوٰةِ ٱلدُّنْيَا وَفِى ٱلْآخِرَةِ

Theirs are the glad tidings in the life of this world and in the hereafter.

We set forth the honoring of the number nineteen by its conjunction with the clauses of the testimony and its seven letters and its twelve, and that Islam is built upon the seven obligations, to which twelve practices are attached — in a manner not hidden from one who possesses knowledge and discernment. Listen now, then, to what is recited to you: that to every obligation among the obligations already mentioned there belong seven obligations and twelve practices. Among them is the walaya (الْوِلَايَة), which is the first and most excellent of them. It has been said before that in every allusion made by these numbers, the ranking of the friends of God is the utmost aim and intention.

After the walaya comes purity, and in it are seven obligations and twelve practices. The seven obligations are those of ablution, which is the lesser purity (الطَّهَارَةُ الصُّغْرَى):

1. Pure water
2. Intention
3. Washing the face
4. Washing the hands
5. Wiping the head
6. Wiping the feet
7. Order, which is that one begins with what God Almighty has begun by mentioning

The twelve practices are:

1. Cleansing oneself after relieving oneself (اَلِاسْتِنْجَاءُ)
2. Washing the hands before entering them into the vessel
3. Pronouncing the name of God when sitting down for ablution
4. Rinsing the mouth
5. Drawing water into the nose
6. Moving the ring so that the water reaches what is beneath it
7. Combing the water through the beard
8. Wetting the eyes with water
9. Giving the right limbs precedence over the left
10. Wiping the ears — their inner and outer parts — with water
11. The second and third washings of the face, each a practice, while the first is the obligation
12. The siwak (السِّوَاك)

We say that the greater purity (اَلطَّهَارَةُ الْكُبْرَى), which is the washing of the whole body, is likewise divided into seven obligations and twelve practices. The obligations are:

1. The washing from janabah (الْجَنَابَة)
2. The washing from menstruation
3. The washing of the unbeliever when he enters Islam
4. The washing of one who has fallen unconscious when he recovers
5. The washing from the blood of nifas (النِّفَاس)
6. The washing after immersion in impurity
7. The washing of the dead, which is an obligation upon the living until one of them performs it

The practices are:

1. The washing of Friday
2. The washing for the festival of الْفِطْر
3. The washing for the festival of al-Adha
4. The washing for ihram
5. The washing for entering the Haram
6. The washing for entering the Kabah
7. The washing for entering Madinah
8. The washing on the day of Arafah
9. The washing on three nights of the month of Ramadan, the night of the nineteenth
10. The night of the twenty-first
11. The night of the twenty-third
12. The washing after washing the dead

Our lord Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred maxims of conduct:

> تَنَظَّفُوا بِالْمَاءِ مِنَ الرِّيحِ الْمُنْتِنَةِ

Cleanse yourselves with water from the foul odor.

And he said therein:

> إِذَا أَرَادَ أَحَدُكُمُ الْخَلَاءَ فَلْيَقُلْ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ اللَّهُمَّ أَمِطْ عَنِّي الْأَذَى وَأَعِذْنِي مِنَ الشَّيْطَانِ الرَّجِيمِ الْحَمْدُ لِلَّهِ الَّذِي أَطْعَمَنِيهِ فِي عَافِيَةٍ وَأَخْرَجَ مِنِّي أَذَاهُ فِي عَافِيَةٍ

When any of you intends to go to the privy, let him say: "In the name of God. O God, remove the filth from me and protect me from the accursed devil. Praise be to God, who fed me in good health and expelled its harm from me in good health."

> إِذَا نَظَرَ أَحَدُكُمْ إِلَىٰ حَدَثِهِ بَعْدَ فَرَاغِهِ فَلْيَقُلِ اللَّهُمَّ ارْزُقْنِي الْحَلَالَ وَجَنِّبْنِي الْحَرَامَ فَاِنَّ رَسُولَ اللَّهِ صَلَّىٰ اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ قَالَ مَا مِنْ عَبْدٍ إِلَّا وَقَدْ وَكَّلَ اللَّهُ بِهِ مَلَكًا يَلْوِي عُنُقَهُ إِذَا أَحْدَثَ حَتَّىٰ يَنْظُرَ إِلَيْهِ وَيَنْبَغِي لَهُ أَنْ يَسْأَلَ اللَّهَ الْحَلَالَ فَاِنَّ الْمَلَكَ يَقُولُ يَا ابْنَ آدَمَ هَٰذَا مَا حَرَصْتَ عَلَيْهِ فَانْظُرْ مِنْ أَيْنَ أَخَذْتَهُ وَإِلَىٰ مَا صَارَ

When one of you looks at what has passed from him after he finishes, let him say: O God, grant me the lawful and keep me from the unlawful. For the Messenger of God said: There is no servant but that God has appointed over him an angel who turns his neck when he relieves himself, so that he looks at it. It is fitting for him to ask God for the lawful, for the angel says: O son of Adam, this is the thing you were so eager for — consider where you took it from and what it has come to.

He also said in that same context:

> إِذَا خَالَطَ النَّوْمُ الْقَلْبَ وَجَبَ الْوُضُوءُ

When sleep mingles with the heart, ablution becomes obligatory.

## 8. The Manners of the Washing Place

I turn now to the manners that belong to the washing place and to the ablution itself.

The book of questions and answers — arranged according to the chapters of Daaim al-Islam — records the following.

Question: What does our master, may God illumine his inner being, say concerning one who forgot the istinja from a major impurity, then prayed and let the prayer time pass before he remembered? May he perform istinja and wudhu and repeat the prayer, or not? Answer: He repeats the prayer, for one does not pray while impurity is upon him.

Question: What does our master, may God perpetuate his support, say concerning a man who performs wudhu and washes himself in the washing place while uncovered, the place being screened so that none of the people sees him? Does he pray with that wudhu or not? Answer: What was mentioned of the concealment of some of the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) in the water was mentioned for the excellence of good manners. If a man is in a place where none of the people sees him and he uncovers himself, there is no harm in that — though concealment is better — and he prays with the purity he performed while uncovered.

From another source: whoever urinates in a place should not perform the istinja in that same place, but in a second place; and whoever has cleansed himself with a stone after relieving himself should not return to that stone.

From Kitab al-Idah, the Messenger of God said:

> أمرني جبرئيل عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ عن ربي أن اغسل فنكي عند الوضوء

Gabriel (ع) commanded me on behalf of my Lord to wash the two hollows beside my chin when I perform the ablution.

And in the sound narration:

> إِذَا تَوَضَّأْتَ فَلَا تَنْسَيَنَّ الْفَنْكَيْنِ

When you perform the ablution, do not forget the two hollows beside the chin.

That is, the sides of the tuft below the lower lip, on the right and on the left — and these two are the neglected place.

He said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: whoever forgets the wudhu until he has performed the ghusl, let him perform wudhu afterward, and nothing is upon him. And if a man performs wudhu and then begins his ghusl, but before completing the ghusl a state of impurity befalls him, he carries on with the ghusl and performs wudhu after it for the prayer.

He said in Tawil al-Daaim: whoever intends the wudhu in its outward sense goes to the vessel in which is the water with which he performs wudhu, purifies himself, and places it on his right.

In the book of questions and answers already mentioned — arranged according to the chapters of Daaim al-Islam — it is asked: What does our master, may God perpetuate his support, say concerning one who entered the prayer after performing wudhu and then recalled that he had forgotten the istinja? Does he complete his prayer or repeat it? Answer: Prayer is not permitted while a man carries an impurity of which he knows, until he washes it away.

He said in Tawil al-Shariah, in the section on the manners of the wudhu: the manners of the wudhu are twelve.

1. Do not enter the privy except with sandals.
2. Advance the left foot.
3. Cover your head until you come out.
4. Do not face the qiblah, nor turn your back to it.
5. Conceal yourself.
6. Lean upon your left leg.
7. Do not sit long in the privy.
8. Do not cleanse yourself with dung, nor with a decayed bone, nor with animal droppings, nor with a crumbling bone, but let it be three fresh stones.
9. Cleanse yourself with your left hand.
10. Do not pour water upon the excrement.
11. Do not speak until you come out.
12. Then advance your right foot when you leave.

The dung mentioned here is human excrement, and the two terms rendered as decayed and crumbling bone both mean a worn bone.

He said therein:

> وَلَا تَجُوزُ الصَّلَاةُ بِغَيْرِ وُضُوءٍ

Prayer is not valid without ablution.

And he said that the Prophet forbade urinating in burrows, saying:

> إِنَّهَا مَأْوَى الثَّعَابِينِ وَالْحَيَّاتِ

They are the lairs of serpents and snakes.

The Prophet said:

> لَا تُقْبَلُ الصَّلَاةُ اِلَّا بِطُهُورٍ

Prayer is not accepted except with purification.

Our master the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> اَلْمَضْمَضَةُ وَالِاسْتِنْشَاقُ بِالْمَاءِ عِنْدَ الطُّهُورِ طَهُورٌ لِلْفَمِ وَالْأَنْفِ وَهُوَ مِنَ السُّنَّةِ

Rinsing the mouth and drawing water into the nose at the time of purification purifies the mouth and the nose, and it is part of the practice of the Prophet.

He said therein:

> اَلْاِسْتِنْجَاءُ بِالْمَاءِ الْبَارِدِ يَقْطَعُ الْبَوَاسِيرَ

Cleansing with cold water cuts off piles.

He said therein:

> الْوُضُوءُ بَعْدَ الطُّهْرِ عَشْرُ حَسَنَاتٍ فَتَطَهَّرُوا

Ablution after purification is ten good deeds, so purify yourselves.

He said therein:

> لَا يَتَوَضَّأُ الرَّجُلُ حَتَّىٰ يَقُولَ قَبْلَ أَنْ يَمَسَّ الْمَاءَ بِسْمِ اللَّهِ اللَّهُمَّ اجْعَلْنِي مِنَ التَّوَّابِينَ وَاجْعَلْنِي مِنَ الْمُتَطَهِّرِينَ

No man should perform ablution until, before water touches him, he says: "In the name of God. O God, اجْعَلْنِي مِنَ التَّوَّابِينَ وَاجْعَلْنِي مِنَ الْمُتَطَهِّرِينَ — make me among those who turn to You in repentance, and make me among those who purify themselves."

He said therein:

> إِذَا تَعَرَّى الرَّجُلُ نَظَرَ إِلَيْهِ الشَّيْطَانُ فَطَمِعَ فِيهِ فَاسْتَتِرُوا
