When a man uncovers himself, Satan looks at him and covets him — so conceal yourselves.

He said therein:

> لَيْسَ لِلرَّجُلِ أَنْ يَكْشِفَ ثِيَابَهُ عَنْ فَخِذِهِ وَيَجْلِسَ بَيْنَ أَيْدِي قَوْمٍ

A man must not draw his garment back from his thigh and sit before a company of people.

He said therein:

> اِغْسِلُوا أَيْدِيَ صِبْيَانِكُمْ مِنَ الْغَمَرِ فَأِنَّ الشَّيْطَانَ يَشُمُّ الْغَمَرَ فَيُفْزِعُ الصَّبِيَّ فِي رُقَادِهِ وَيُؤْذَى بِهِ الْكَاتِبَانِ

Wash your children's hands clean of the grease of food, for Satan smells it, the child is startled in his sleep, and the two recording angels are harmed by it.

I turn now to the qualities of the wudhu.

In the same book of questions and answers it is asked: What do you say, O best of those who have drawn us to the love of wisdom and of the wise, concerning what has come in the book of purity — that rinsing the mouth, drawing in water through the nose, and the istinja are prophetic practices that must not be deliberately abandoned, and that whoever forgets them or is unaware of them has not thereby spoiled his wudhu and owes no repetition if he has already prayed? What is intended by this istinja?

Answer: The istinja, the rinsing of the mouth, and the drawing in of water are practices that must not be deliberately abandoned, and whoever omits them has not spoiled his prayer. The istinja is nonetheless obligatory for the removal of impurity, because the removal of impurity is itself obligatory.

Question: What do you say, O best of those whose blessed meeting has made us forget all love of family and friends, concerning what has come in Daaim al-Islam in the command to wash the face while letting the eyes drink of the water? Does any deficiency enter the wudhu of one who closes his eyes while washing his face?

Answer: If he lets his eyes drink, that is better; and if he does not, his wudhu is complete.

Question: What do you say, O one with whom God is pleased only as He is pleased with His righteous servants, concerning a man who has upon his face or his hands a wound or a boil that water cannot touch? Must he leave it when performing the wudhu or the ghusl?

Answer: Whoever has upon his legs or elsewhere an ailment, wound, or boil that water cannot touch may wipe over the bandages and whatever else covers the wound. If he fears that removing them would harm him through cold or severe injury, or if he is sick, he performs tayammum. That is the ruling for wudhu. As for the greater purification: whoever has an ailment such as smallpox, wounds, or the like pours water over it in a stream; and if he fears for himself, he must perform tayammum, whether he is traveling or at home.

Question: What does our master — may God grant us his pleasure — say concerning wiping over the two feet when most of the foot is exposed and the lesser part is covered beneath the strap of the sandal? Is such wiping permitted?

Answer: This is an obligation, and the wiping must be carried out beneath it.

Question: What does our master — may God perpetuate his support — say concerning one who entered the prayer after performing wudhu, then recalled that he had forgotten the istinja with water? Does he complete his prayer or repeat it?

Answer: The prayer is not permitted while a man carries an impurity of which he is aware, until he washes it away.

From another source: whoever performs wudhu, forgets the hand and the face, prays, and only recalls after the time has passed does not repeat the prayer. If a man performs wudhu, then rises and walks about until the limbs dry, he begins the wudhu again from the start; and if they have not dried, he completes what remains. Whoever performs wudhu for the prayer, then rises and afterward finds a spot on one of the wudhu limbs that has dried, does not repeat the wudhu but wipes the place that has dried. As for one whose hands are cut off and who cannot perform wudhu for the prayer, the bondmaid who belongs to him performs it for him, or his wife; and if he finds neither of them, he performs tayammum and prays.

Question: What do you say, O one whom God Most High has made the trustee of His friends, concerning a pool or an empty hollow with no water, in which people relieve themselves, and then water flows into it together with the filth until it gathers and becomes overpowering — above the measure, subduing the impurities — so that none of their qualities can be detected in it? Is purification with it permitted?

Answer: If the water overpowers the impurity, purification with it is permitted, provided it is abundant and above the measure stated in the Shariah.

Question: What do you say, O one whom God Most High has made the delight of the eye of the beloved of God, concerning a vessel filled with water to above the measure, in which an impurity then appears or falls and one of its qualities becomes apparent, after which water is poured into it in a stream until the trace of that impurity disappears? Is purification with it permitted?

Answer: The water has become impure, and purification with it is not permitted, unless it rises above the measure after the vessel has been purified.

Question: What do you say, O one whom God Most High has made a spring of water by which the souls of the people of Islam are made to endure, concerning a small quantity of pure water with which one who bears no impurity on his body purifies himself, then another purifies himself with it, and then another — and likewise the dust of tayammum? The Shariah has stated that used water is pure and purifying, yet we find in it no mention of used dust, and ordinary people do not permit using water or dust for purification a second time.

Answer: Purification with it is permitted, once and many times.

Question: What do you say concerning the meaning of his saying (ع):

> مَنْ لَمْ يُطَهِّرْهُ الْبَحْرُ فَلَا طَهَّرَهُ اللهُ

Whoever the sea does not purify, may God not purify him.

Answer: Water purifies and is not itself purified; and likewise the knowledge of the Imam: whomever it does not purify, nothing else will purify him.

If the water in a pool is eight cubits in its length and breadth — though its depth be only a span or less — purification in it is permitted. If rain falls in the city and the water runs together with filth and refuse into a great pool in which a man and a camel might drown, and the pool is filled, washing and purification with it are permitted so long as its color or its taste or its smell has not changed. And if water is drawn from a well and the water is running, and an unbeliever washes a garment in it and its dirt is seen, purification with it is permitted, because it has the ruling of running water.

From Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: if a man finds a little water upon the ground, or in a hollow, or the like, and it is greatly changed, and it is not known whether impurity has mixed with it, he performs wudhu with it nonetheless — for water may change without any impurity having mixed with it. What is extracted from substances by pressing or distillation, such as rosewater and the like, may not be used for purification. What has been used in purifying the limbs when no impurity was upon them, and has not gathered in an impure vessel, may be used for purification. And it is permitted to perform wudhu with water that has already been used once for wudhu, so long as no impurity has mixed with it.

It is mentioned in the Muayyadi Sessions: washing with rainwater at the time of the rain suffices. And it is said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: whoever has on his hand a ring bearing the name of God must not touch impurity while wearing it.

Our master the Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad, may God exalt his sanctity, said in Kitab al-Idah, in the section on the description of washing the hands, that he had mentioned what was related from Abu Abdillah Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع), who said:

> وَلَا يَنْبَغِي أَنْ تَمْسَحَ ذِرَاعَيْكَ اِلَّا مِنَ الْمِرْفَقِ إِلَى فَوْقُ وَامْسَحْهُ مَسْحًا فَإِذَا كَانَ يَلْزَمُ فِي الْيَدَيْنِ عَلَى قِلَّةِ شَعَرِهِمَا فَهُوَ فِي الرَّأْسِ اَلْزَمُ

It has likewise come down that when a person performing the wudhu interrupts it, he builds upon what he has done, so long as the water has not dried from the limbs he has already washed.

He said in Tawil al-Shariah of our master al-Muizz li-Din Allah, may the blessings of God be upon him, that the Prophet said:

> إِذَا وَلَغَ الْكَلْبُ فِي إِنَاءِ أَحَدِكُمْ فَلْيَغْسِلْهُ سَبْعَ مَرَّاتٍ آخِرَهُنَّ بِالتُّرَابِ

When a dog laps from the vessel of one of you, let him wash it seven times, the last of them with dust.

It is said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: when a vessel is made impure by a dog's lapping, it is washed seven times, the first of them with dust; and the pig is like it. It is said therein also: if an impurity falls into one of two vessels and it is not known which of them it is, he does not perform wudhu with either of them, but performs tayammum and prays, and nothing is upon him.

It is said in Majalis al-Bayan that the obligatory acts of the wudhu are seven:

1. Pure water.
2. The intention.
3. Washing the face.
4. Washing the hands.
5. Wiping the head.
6. Wiping the feet.
7. The order — that one begins with what God began with in His mention.

And its Sunnah acts are twelve:

1. The istinja.
2. Washing the hands before entering them into the vessel.
3. Pronouncing the name of God when sitting down for the wudhu.
4. Rinsing the mouth.
5. Drawing water into the nose.
6. Moving the ring.
7. Running the fingers through the beard.
8. Letting the eyes drink of the water.
9. Giving precedence to the right sides over the left.
10. Wiping the two ears.
11. The first washing, which is obligatory, the second and the third being Sunnah.
12. The tooth stick.

The obligatory forms of ghusl are seven:

1. The ghusl from major ritual impurity.
2. The ghusl of menstruation.
3. The ghusl of the unbeliever when he enters Islam.
4. The ghusl of the madman and of one who has fainted, when they recover.
5. The ghusl from the blood of childbirth.
6. The ghusl from immersion in impurity.
7. The washing of the dead, which is an obligation upon the living until one of them undertakes it.

And the Sunnah forms of ghusl are twelve:

1. The ghusl of Friday.
2. and 3. The ghusl for the two festivals.
4. The ghusl for entering the state of consecration.
5. The ghusl for entering the sacred precinct.
6. The ghusl for entering the Kabah.
7. The ghusl for entering Madinah.
8. The ghusl on the day of Arafah.
9. The ghusl on three nights of the month of Ramadan: the night of the nineteenth.
10. The night of the twenty-first.
11. The night of the twenty-third.
12. The ghusl after washing the dead.

And the prayer is disliked in twelve places:

1. The graveyard.
2. The slaughterhouse.
3. The bathhouse.
4. The privy.
5. The resting places of camels.
6. Praying toward a sleeping person.
7. Praying without a screen.
8. Upon wet, impure ground.
9. Upon anything of food.
10. In the places of worship of the Jews.
11. Of the Christians.
12. Of the Magians, unless water be sprinkled in them.

He said in Tawil al-Shariah that there is no fixed limit for the istinja other than purity, the settling of the heart and the soul, and the arrival of certainty that purity has been attained and impurity removed. He said therein also that the Prophet forbade wiping over the socks and forbade the abandoning of saying aloud بِسْمِ ٱللَّهِ ٱلرَّحْمَنِ ٱلرَّحِيمِ.

Our master Jafar ibn Mansur al-Yaman, may God exalt his sanctity, said at the beginning of Kitab al-Rida al-Batin that the Sunnah in the wudhu is three times over, three times over, in every place of the wudhu, except the private parts — meaning that these are washed until the impurity is gone from them. Then he said, after that, may God exalt his sanctity, that the wudhu is threefold, threefold. And he said therein, after further words, that the Sunnah in the ghusl is that a man wash himself with water until it drips and flows; and if he does not find abundant water, then let him wipe with as much as will reach his whole body with water.

It is said in Mukhtasar al-Athar, in the mention of the qualities of the wudhu, that one then rinses the mouth and draws in water three times, and that this is of the Sunnah and should not be abandoned; and if one leaves it without intending to leave it, nothing is upon him. It is said therein: he washes it three times, and if he washes it twice or once in a complete washing, it suffices him — three is better, and there is no merit beyond three. And it is said therein: once suffices him, and if he wipes it three times, that is good.

Our master the most exalted summoner Zayd (زَيْد) ibn Muhammad ibn al-Fadl, may God sanctify his soul, said in al-Risalah al-Maknunah: God, the most truthful of those who speak, said:

> إِنَّ ٱللَّهَ يُحِبُّ ٱلتَّوَّبِينَ وَيُحِبُّ ٱلْمُتَطَهِّرِينَ

Truly God loves those who turn to Him in repentance, and He loves those who purify themselves.

And the Messenger of God said:

> لَا تُقْبَلُ الصَّلَاةُ اِلَّا بِطُهُورٍ

Prayer is not accepted except with purification.

Purity is of two kinds, and so is filth. Outward purity is achieved through outward water, cleansing from the outward filth of urine and excrement; inward purity is achieved through inward water — which is knowledge — cleansing from the inward filth of doubt and ignorance.

He said therein that the name of wudhu covers seven meanings: four obligatory and three Sunnah. He also noted that the Messenger placed the three Sunnah acts before the four obligations, and he mentioned washing the hands before putting them into the vessel.

He said that the mouth is what a person uses to take nourishment from all kinds of food and drink. A person has no other faculty through which nourishment comes to him; the mouth is the one that conveys nourishment to the five senses, and articulate expression belongs to it alone. Then he said: it is like the mouth of the lesser world — whoever seeks nourishment through anything other than the mouth is neither nourished nor alive.

As for drawing water through the nose, he said: the nostrils draw breath from the air, by which the body lives and rests. When the nostrils are blocked, that function passes to the mouth, which takes their place with effort. But when both faculties together — the nose and the mouth — are blocked, the person perishes and can never live again.

Then he said: rinsing the mouth, drawing in water, and the istinja are three Sunnah acts; washing the face and washing the hands are two obligations; and wiping the head and wiping the feet are two obligations. That is seven bounds. Of the two washings, one is bounded and the other is unbounded; and of the two wipings, one is bounded and the other is unbounded.

The face bears the meaning of the Speaker, for in it are the seven openings: the two eyes, the two ears, the two nostrils, and the mouth. A person is known by his face; and likewise, no one is known except through his prophet, and there is no deliverance except by turning toward him.

And our master the Commander of the Faithful Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> لَيْسَ فِي شُرْبِ الْمُسْكِرِ وَالْمَسْحِ عَلَى الْخُفَّيْنِ تَقِيَّةٌ

There is no dissimulation in the drinking of intoxicants or in wiping over the socks.

## 9. Major Impurity and the Cleanliness of Things

### Mentioning the Ghusl from Janabah

I take the questions and answers that follow from the book of questions and answers mentioned earlier, which one of its compilers arranged according to the chapters of the book *Daaim al-Islam*.

Question: What do you say, O best of those for whom our tongues fall short in praise and gratitude, concerning what has come in certain books of the Shariah: that when a man fears the cold and a ghusl is due upon him, he performs tayammum and prays, and then, when he is able to use water, he washes and repeats the prayer? Does this ruling apply equally to the resident and the traveler, or not?

Answer: If he fears that the water will harm him — whether he is a resident or a traveler — he performs tayammum and prays, and nothing further is required of him.

Question: What do you say, O one to whose excellence the far and the near both bear witness, concerning the ghusl from janabah (جَنَابَة), since marital relations are a likeness of imparting and receiving? Explain, then, the likeness of the washing and what the one who washes is obliged to call to mind while washing, for the exposition of this in the books of the guiding Dawah is very long.

Answer: The washing is a likeness of the removal of doubt, and doubt arises only at the moment of imparting and receiving.

Question: What do you say, O one whom God Most High has made a rope extended from heaven to this world for the people of the earth who remember God while sitting and while standing, concerning what has come in the Shariah: that one who has fallen unconscious, when he revives after some days and does not know whether he may have become in a state of janabah, must perform the ghusl? O our master, what is the measure of this if he sees no trace of semen?

Answer: If he does not know his own condition, the ghusl becomes obligatory upon him once doubt has entered him concerning it.

Question: What does our master say — may God inspire him with the welfare of the Dawah — concerning a weak man who has been overtaken by janabah and who fears the cold if he washes, when the time of prayer has come? May he perform tayammum as the sick man does, or not?

Answer: If he fears the water, he performs tayammum and prays, and he does not repeat the prayer.

Question: What does our master say — may God perpetuate his support — concerning a man who wakes from sleep and sees on his garment the trace of semen, wet or dry, though he did not see in his sleep that he had emitted? Is the ghusl obligatory upon him or not?

Answer: The ghusl is obligatory upon him.

Question: What do you say, O fulfiller of the needs of those who ask, concerning what is mentioned in the Shariah: that it is disliked for the one in janabah and for the menstruating woman to remove from themselves any hair or nail, and that the woman in post-natal bleeding is in the same state as the menstruating woman? O our master, is this permitted to them despite the dislike, or is it not permitted at all?

Answer: To leave it is better, until such a one is purified.

Question: What do you say, O one whom I approached from where I came — out of nothing but pure love — concerning the one in janabah and the menstruating woman who read supplications or other books in which there is something of the Quran, whether the reader recognizes it or does not? How is such a one to act — reading it or leaving it — when it has come in the radiant Shariah that the two do not recite the Quran and do not touch it? And may the one in janabah and the menstruating woman recite the declaration of God's oneness, the magnification, the praise, and the glorification, all of which are in the Quran? And may such a one recite the اَلْبَسْمَلَة, when it has been said that it is a verse of the Quran?

Answer: Neither the menstruating woman nor the one in janabah may recite the Quran, nor any part of it in a supplication or in books, deliberately; and neither may recite the اَلْبَسْمَلَة with the intention of reciting the Quran.

In the book *Mukhtasar al-Musannaf* it is said that whoever forgets the ablution until he has performed the ghusl performs the ablution, and there is nothing against him. It is also said there: when a man has performed the ablution and then begins the ghusl, and a minor impurity occurs before he has completed his washing, he continues with the ghusl and performs the ablution after it for the prayer.

Among the answers of certain summoners of Yemen to certain dignitaries of the Indian lands (رض): if one in janabah and a deceased person are brought together and there is not found of water except what suffices for one of them, the one in janabah performs the ghusl and tayammum is performed for the deceased.

The author of *al-Risala al-Maknuna* said: God has imposed the ghusl from janabah, for He said — exalted be He who speaks:

> وَإِن كُنتُمْ جُنُبًۭا فَٱطَّهَّرُوا۟
>
> "And if you are in a state of major impurity, then purify yourselves."

And the Prophet said:

> تَحْتَ كُلِّ شَعْرٍ جَنَابَةٌ فَبُلُّوا الشَّعْرَ وَاَنْقُوا الْبَشَرَ
>
> "Beneath every hair there is major impurity, so wet the hair and cleanse the skin."

Then he said: whoever does not do that is junub, and al-junub is the one far from a thing. It is said, "he is a stranger to me" — that is, far from my lineage. And the obedience of the one in janabah is void and false.

The Commander of the Faithful, our master Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> لَا يَنَامُ الْمُسْلِمُ وَهُوَ جُنُبٌ وَلَا يَنَامُ اِلَّا وَهُوَ عَلَىٰ طَهُورٍ فَاِنْ لَمْ يَجِدِ الْمَاءَ فَلْيَتَيَمَّمْ بِالصَّعِيدِ فَاِنَّ رُوحَ الْمُؤْمِنِ تَرْتَفِعُ إِلَى اللَّهِ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ فَيَقْبَلُهَا وَيُبَارِكُ عَلَيْهَا فَاِنْ كَانَ قَدْ حَضَرَ أَجَلُهَا جَعَلَهَا فِي أَحْسَنِ صُورَةٍ وَاِنْ لَمْ يَحْضُرْ أَجَلُهَا بَعَثَ بِهَا مَعَ أُمَنَاءِهِ مِنَ الْمَلَائِكَةِ فَرَدُّوهَا إِلَىٰ جَسَدِهِ

### Mentioning the Purity of Bodies, Garments, Ground, Carpets, and What Belongs to That

I take these also from the book of question and answer mentioned earlier, arranged according to the chapters of *Daaim al-Islam*.

**Question:** What do you say, O best of those who have commanded us to endure with patience the decree of God and His judgment, concerning vomit? Does it render impure what it falls upon, as blood and pus and the other impurities do, or not?

**Answer:** Vomit does not render impure what it falls upon, though it is better that the place be washed.

**Question:** What do you say, O one whom God Most High has made a provision for the traveler who wishes to arrive at God Most High, concerning ground on which children urinate and relieve themselves, which then dries so that the smell of the impurity and its substance are gone from it? Is prayer upon it permissible without a mat, and is tayammum from it permissible, or not?

**Answer:** If the substance of the impurity has gone, its ruling is the ruling of pure ground, provided it is not ground set aside as a privy for need.

**Question:** What do you say, O one whom God Most High has made a measure by which the corrupt is tested against the pure in the religion of God, concerning what has come in the Shariah: that when impurity strikes the ground and water then runs over it and it dries, and the smell of the impurity and its substance are gone from it, prayer upon it and tayammum from it are permitted? And it has also come that there is no harm in praying in the dry places among the resting grounds of camels and the folds of sheep. O our master, what is the difference between this and that?

**Answer:** The sun and the light purify it. So when the substance of the impurity and its smell have gone from it, prayer upon it and tayammum from it are permitted.

**Question:** What does our master say, may God make lasting the throne of his governance, concerning the meaning of the words of the Commander of the Faithful:

> مَنْ مَسَّ جَسَدَ مَيِّتٍ بَعْدَ أَنْ يَبْرُدَ لَزِمَهُ الْغُسْلُ
>
> "Whoever touches the body of a dead man after it has grown cold must perform the ghusl."

What is the meaning of his words, "after it has grown cold"?

**Answer:** Its meaning is: after it has become impure. So when it touches the living with its moisture, he becomes impure, and this is the meaning of the ghusl becoming binding — that it be decreed upon him. And the washing performed by the one who washes the corpse is from the Sunnah.

**Question:** Concerning a thing that a man wishes to purify but is not able to rub or to wring out three times — such as a fur, a mat, a rug, the quilts used in the mosque, and bedding — what is the manner of purifying it?

**Answer:** For a thing that a man wishes to purify but is not able to rub or to wring out three times, he passes his hand over it until the impurity and its smell are gone, then lifts it from the water and leaves it until what is in it has dripped out, then repeats this three times. As for what is other than this, he treads it with his feet in the manner described, then takes it out of the water and presses upon it until the water has come out of it, then repeats this three times.

**Question:** Concerning a woman's milk that falls upon the garment of another, is it pure or not?

**Answer:** From the Walid Izz al-Din Maad ibn Abdullah ibn Ali: the milk of a nursing woman, if she is a Muslim, is pure, both for her and for others. And I say that this is an established position, since her milk is like her saliva, and the saliva of every Muslim is pure and does not render impure what it touches.

**Question:** Concerning a man who shaves his head, and something of his hair falls while it is wet upon his garment or upon his body: does that hair render impure what it touches or not?

**Answer:** If that is a small amount, then a small amount of impurity has been excused.

**Question:** Concerning the animals that feed upon filth — of cattle and camels and sheep — and the milk and clarified butter of these, and the white of the eggs of hens, when they fall upon the garments of those praying or upon their bodies: does this render them impure or not?

**Answer:** That follows what was mentioned in the first case: a small amount of it is excused.

**Question:** Concerning the traveler and the sick man, whose garments are touched by impurity and who are not able to cleanse them, is the prayer complete for them with tayammum, or must it be made up afterward?

**Answer:** The prayer is not complete except with a pure garment. Tayammum has been mentioned for ritual impurity, and it has not been mentioned for the impurity of garments. As for the sick man, he instructs someone to wash his garments for him.

**Question:** The polytheist worships idols and knows neither the lawful nor the forbidden, and he weaves the cloth, and after that he bleaches it, and after that he sews it. Is his work pure, and is prayer in it permitted or not? For God Most High has said:

> إِنَّمَا ٱلْمُشْرِكُونَ نَجَسٌۭ
>
> "The polytheists are nothing but impurity."

**Answer:** All such garments are pure until he wears them; and if he has worn them, then they are impure, or if impurity has appeared in them. As he said in the book *al-Daaim*:

> ورخصوا الأئمة صلوات الله عليهم في الصلاة في الثياب التي يعملها المشركين ما لم يلبسوها أو تظهر فيها النجاسة

He said also: the same applies to a dyed garment when the dyer is a polytheist — it remains pure until he has worn it; once he has worn it, it is impure.

**Question:** What does our master say, may God perpetuate his support, concerning the bleaching that polytheists perform on whatever they bleach — does it carry purity or not? Be so good as to make this clear.

**Answer:** I say — trusting in God's granting of success and drawing support from the blessings of His friends — that there is no path to the purity of polytheists' garments except those they have made before wearing them. As for what they have bleached and washed with soap, its purity is not allowed except by washing. Perhaps those who heard the saying about this did not grasp the root of the question in it.

**Question:** What does our master say, may God perpetuate his support, concerning the garments of polytheists التي they have made but have not worn, which they sprinkle with impure water at the time of the weaving and the spinning, after which the water dries and the garments are sold, and they have neither worn them nor washed them with soap? Is prayer in them permitted or not?

**Answer:** There is no harm in praying in them, and the radiant Shariah has permitted them.

**Question:** What do you say, O one whose people of response have come to take pride in the praiseworthy account of his conduct, concerning the wide, long garment and the bedding of which one part is pure and another part impure? Is prayer performed upon the part of it that is pure? For the common people say that if the garment is of such a size that when one end of it is moved the other end does not move, prayer is permitted upon the part of it that is pure.

**Answer:** One does not pray upon it, and prayer upon it is not permitted.

In the book *Mukhtasar al-Musannaf* it is said that prayer in the garments of children who guard themselves against impurity is permitted so long as no impurity is known in them, and dispensation has been given for a slight sprinkling. It is said there also: as for the houses out in the open country, praying in them has not been forbidden, though it is not recommended.

I turn now to the book of question and answer whose questions are from the most excellent Sheikh Yusuf ibn Ismail ibn Ibrahim al-Samti al-Hindi and whose answers are from our master Idris ibn al-Hasan.

**Question:** Is prayer permitted upon stone and upon plaster? Favor us with a sufficient and clear answer.

**Answer:** The Prophet said:

> جُعِلَتْ لِي الْأَرْضُ مَسْجِدًا وَطَهُورًا
>
> "The earth has been made for me a place of prostration and a means of purification."

Anything of the ground from which tayammum is not permitted may not be prayed upon.

**Question:** What do you say about sweeping houses? Most people in India hold that houses must not be swept at night at all, and they treat it as a religious scruple; they likewise hold that shops and stalls are not to be swept at night. How does this stand — may God make us your ransom?

**Answer:** This view is not reliable, and there is no blame on one who does it.

In *Tawil al-Shariah* it is recorded that the Prophet said:

> إِذَا وَلَغَ الْكَلْبُ فِي إِنَاءِ أَحَدِكُمْ فَلْيَغْسِلْهُ سَبْعَ مَرَّاتٍ إِحْدَاهُنَّ بِالتُّرَابِ
>
> "When a dog laps from the vessel of one of you, let him wash it seven times, one of them with earth."

*Mukhtasar al-Musannaf* states: when a vessel becomes impure from a dog's lapping, it is washed seven times, the first of them with earth, and the pig is treated the same way. It is also stated there: if impurity falls into one of two vessels and it is not known which of them it is, neither vessel is to be approached; one performs tayammum and prays.

The Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Manners:

> أَخْذُ الشَّارِبِ مِنَ النَّظَافَةِ وَهُوَ مِنَ السُّنَّةِ
>
> "Trimming the mustache is part of cleanliness, and it is of the Sunnah."

> غَسْلُ الرَّأْسِ بِالْحِطْمِيِّ يُذْهِبُ بِالدَّرَنِ وَهُوَ مِنَ السُّنَّةِ وَيَنْفِي الْأَقْذَارَ
>
> "Washing the head with mallow takes away grime, and it is of the Sunnah, and it drives off filth."

> النُّورَةُ مَشَدَّةٌ لِلْبَدَنِ وَطَهُورٌ لِلْجَسَدِ
>
> "The depilatory paste strengthens the body and is a purification for the flesh."

> تَقْلِيمُ الْأَظَافِرِ يَمْنَعُ الدَّاءَ الْأَعْظَمَ وَيَجْلِبُ الرِّزْقَ وَيَدُرُّهُ
>
> "Trimming the nails wards off the greatest disease, and draws sustenance and makes it flow."

> نَتْفُ الْآبَاطِ يَنْفِي الرَّائِحَةَ الْمُنْكَرَةَ وَهُوَ طَهُورٌ وَهُوَ مِنَ السُّنَّةِ
>
> "Plucking the armpits removes the offensive odor; it is a purification, and it is of the Sunnah."

> غُسْلُ الْأَعْيَادِ طَهُورٌ لِمَنْ طَلَبَ الْحَوَائِجَ بَيْنَ يَدَيِ اللَّهِ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ وَأتِّبَاعُ السُّنَّةِ
>
> "Bathing on the feast days is a purification for whoever presents his needs before God, mighty and majestic, and a following of the Sunnah."

> غَسْلُ الثِّيَابِ يُذْهِبُ بِالْهَمِّ وَيَكْبِتُ الْعَدُوَّ وَهُوَ طَهُورٌ لِلصَّلَاةِ
>
> "Washing the garments takes away anxiety and subdues the enemy, and it is a purification for the prayer."

> لَا تَنْتِفُوا الشَّنَبَ فَاِنَّهُ نُورٍ لِلْمُسْلِمِ
>
> "Do not pluck out the mustache, for it is a light for the Muslim."

> مَنْ شَابَ فِي رَأْسِهِ شَيْبَةٌ فِي الْإِسْلَامِ كَانَتْ لَهُ نُورًا يَوْمَ الْقِيَامَةِ
>
> "Whoever grows a white hair upon his head while in Islam, it will be a light for him on the Day of Resurrection."

> ولا يتغوّطنّ أحدكم على الحجة ولا يبل في سطح الهواء ولا في ماء راكد فمن فعل فأصابه شيء فلا يلومنّ الا نفسه فان للماء أهلاً وللهواء أهلاً فإذا بال أحدكم فلا يطمحنّ ببوله ولا يستقبل به الربح
>
> "Let none of you relieve himself on the road, nor urinate into the open air or into standing water. Whoever does so and something befalls him, let him blame none but himself — for the water has its folk, and the air has its folk. And when one of you urinates, let him not make his urine splash, and let him not face the wind with it."

> ولا ينا منّ أحدكم على الحجة ولا على وجهه فمن رأيتموه نام على وجهه فانبهوه
>
> "And let none of you sleep on the road, nor upon his face. Whoever you see sleeping upon his face, wake him."

> تَنَظَّفُوا بِالْمَاءِ مِنَ الرِّيحِ الْمُنْتِنَةِ وَتَعَاهَدُوا أَنْفُسَكُمْ فَاِنَّ اللَّهَ تَعَالَى يُبْغِضُ الْعَبْدَ الْقَاذُورَةَ الَّذِي یَتَأَذَّى بِهِ مَنْ جَلَسَ إِلَيْهِ
>
> "Cleanse yourselves of foul odor with water, and take care of your cleanliness, for God Most High hates the filthy servant who offends those who sit near him."

> تَنَزَّهُوا عَنْ قُرْبِ الْكِلَابِ فَمَنْ أَصَابَهُ كَلْبٌ جَافٌّ فَلْيَنْضَحْ ثَوْبَهُ بِالْمَاءِ وَاِنْ كَانَ الْكَلْبُ رَطِبًا فَلْيَغْسِلْهُ
>
> "Keep yourselves away from dogs. Whoever is touched by a dry dog should sprinkle his garment with water; if the dog was wet, he must wash it."

> اُحِبُّ لِلْمُؤْمِنِ أَنْ يَطَّلِّيَ فِي كُلِّ خَمْسَةَ عَشَرَ يَوْمًا مَرَّةً بِنُورَةٍ
>
> "I love for the believer that he anoint himself once every fifteen days with the depilatory paste."

What follows I take from a book of questions and answers: the questions come from the most excellent Sheikh Shimon ibn Muhammad Ghori al-Hindi (رض), and the answers are from our master Badr al-Din, our master Hasan ibn Idris.

Question: What do you say, O one by whom our souls have life, concerning a man who lets the hair of his head grow long and neither combs nor parts it each day? Does his prayer suffer any defect, or not? And how often ought he to comb and part it?

Answer: If he parts it into two halves, there is no defect upon him; yet it is not fitting that he leave it without a parting. If he does so deliberately, in defiance of the ruling, his prayer is void.

Question: What do you say concerning the meaning of the words of Ali (ع):

> مَنْ لَا يُطَهِّرُهُ الْبَحْرُ فَلَا طَهَّرَهُ اللهُ
>
> "Whomever the sea does not purify, may God not purify him."

Answer: Water purifies and is not itself purified. So likewise is the knowledge of the Imam: whomever it does not purify, God does not purify him.

## 10. Tooth-Stick, Dust, and the Purity of Food

I turn now to the mention of the tooth-stick (السِّوَاك).

I record what he stated in the book of questions and answers mentioned above, which one of its compilers arranged according to the chapter order of the book دَعَائِمُ الْإِسْلَامِ:

Question: What do you say, O you at whose words our souls find rest, concerning the custom of most people of India — that after finishing with the siwak they take a split piece of bamboo cane or something similar and scrape their tongues with it to remove what has gathered there? Is this permissible or not?

Answer: The siwak is the mainstay, and there is nothing against them if they do that.

From another place: one should not clean between the teeth with a stalk that has a knot in it, taken from dried grass. From another: it is disliked for anyone to use the siwak while in a state of janaba — that is, while in the condition of major ritual impurity.

From the book شَرْحُ الْأَخْبَارِ: الريحان is every plant of fragrant scent, and the myrtle (اَلْآس) has been singled out for that name. From the book تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ: الريحان in the language means the tips of every sweet-scented plant when its blossom comes forth, and الريحان is a comprehensive name for all fragrant plants; most often, however, the myrtle is singled out for it, since it is the most lasting and the strongest of them all.

The Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his four hundred points of etiquette:

> السِّوَاكُ مَرْضَاةٌ لِلرَّبِّ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ وَمَطْيَبَةٌ لِلْفَمِ وَهُوَ مِنَ السُّنَّةِ

The tooth-stick is a means of pleasing the Lord, mighty and majestic; it sweetens the mouth, and it belongs to the established practice.

I turn now to the mention of dry purification (اَلتَّيَمُّم), again from the book of questions and answers mentioned above, arranged by one of its compilers according to the chapter order of the book دَعَائِمُ الْإِسْلَامِ.

**Question:** What do you say, O best of those who have urged us to sit in the company of the learned, concerning a smooth rock fixed firmly in the ground: may one perform tayammum on it even when there is no dust on it and no soil is to be found anywhere? And if tayammum on it requires dust, what is the difference between it and any other stone?

**Answer:** If dust is on it, he performs tayammum. If there is no dust on it, he does not.

**Question:** What does our master say concerning a man who leaves a city for a destination short of the distance that counts as travel, and the time of prayer comes upon him on the road with no water: may he perform tayammum?

**Answer:** Tayammum is not permitted to a settled man except for cause. But if he is on a journey, far from water, and cannot perform the ablution before the time lapses — or if going to the water would put his person or his property at risk — he performs tayammum.

**Question:** Concerning a man who owes the full washing (اَلْغُسْل) during a night of Ramadan and forgets it until he has set out on a journey: does tayammum suffice him for prayer and for fasting?

**Answer:** If he forgets the washing until he is already traveling and finds no water, tayammum suffices him for both the prayer and the fast.

**Question:** Concerning a man who dies on a journey and water cannot be found?

**Answer:** In that case he is given tayammum. From another: whoever is on a journey and the time comes while water is present, but fears illness for himself if he performs the ablution, performs tayammum and prays.

**Question:** Concerning a traveler who has with him only a little water — enough either to wash the limbs or to cleanse himself after relieving himself: with which does he begin? And if he does not cleanse himself, does the washing suffice him in place of tayammum?

**Answer:** Our master al-Numan said in the book of purity: if he cleanses himself with stones or with something else and removes what is there, he performs the ablution, since this arises from a cause — and the absence of water is that cause — and there is no call for tayammum.

**Question:** Concerning a man settled in town who has an ailment in his private part that prevents him from cleansing himself: if he washes the limbs, is tayammum then obligatory upon him as well?

**Answer:** This has already been addressed in the reply to the first question.

**Question:** Concerning a traveler who prays the obligatory prayers with tayammum before their times lapse: if those times pass him by, must he make up the prayer with tayammum?

**Answer:** When there is no water, prayer with tayammum is valid — both the prayer offered in its time and the one made up afterward.

From another: the traveler's wife, if she menstruates and then becomes pure and finds no water, performs tayammum and prays, and it becomes lawful for her husband to have intercourse with her. If a company are traveling and have an imam among them, and he becomes junub, and they have with them only water enough for one man's ablution, their imam performs tayammum and leads in prayer those of them who have also performed tayammum. If a man is on a journey with only enough water for his ablution, and there is impurity on his garment, he washes the garment with that water, then performs tayammum and prays. If a man becomes junub on a journey and the noon prayer comes, and he has water enough for the ablution but not for the full washing, he performs tayammum and prays without performing the ablution; then when the afternoon prayer comes and that water is still with him, he performs the ablution with it and does not perform tayammum, because he is by then pure from janaba. If a deceased person and one in janaba come together and there is only water enough for a single washing, the one in janaba washes himself and the deceased is given tayammum. Whoever travels with a Christian or Zoroastrian wife, and she menstruates on the road and then becomes pure and finds no water, performs tayammum, and the husband teaches her the tayammum, and he may then go in to her.

The same rulings appear in the book مُخْتَصَرُ الْمُصَنَّفِ, with these further points:

- If water is found after the tayammum, the tayammum is broken and the man returns to the state of janaba. So if he has not yet washed and the sunset prayer comes and he has with him only water enough for the ablution and no more, he performs tayammum again; then when the final evening prayer comes and that water is still with him, he performs the ablution with it, since he is by then pure.
- If an unbeliever accepts Islam and finds no water, he performs tayammum and prays.
- It is recommended for a man who sleeps in the mosque of the Prophet and is touched by impurity and awakens that he perform tayammum, and that he not pass through the mosque except having performed tayammum, until he leaves it; and likewise within the sanctuary.
- If a deceased person and one in janaba come together and there is only water enough for a single washing, the one in janaba washes and the deceased is given tayammum.
- As for the invalid who cannot perform tayammum himself, a sound person performs it for him.

From the same book: a man in a state of minor impurity, while settled in the city, performs tayammum for the prayer over the dead if he fears that it will pass him by. And whoever prays with tayammum while there is water in his baggage that he has forgotten — if he remembers and time remains, he performs the ablution and prays; if the time has gone out, what he did suffices him and the prayer he offered with it stands.

He said in اَلْمُنْتَخَبَةُ:

> وَكُلُّ مَنْ لَمْ يَجِدِ التُّرَابَ
> فَلْيَنْفُضِ اللُّبُودَ وَالثِّيَابَ
> حتى إذا لم يرى شيئا طار
> وَلْيَتَيَمَّمْ ذَلِكَ الْغُبَارَ

Whoever finds no earth, let him beat out the felts and the garments; and then, even if he sees nothing fly up, let him take that dust for his dry purification.

He said in the book تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ, concerning a man seized in a crush who cannot get free — that is, cannot reach the water — and the prayer comes upon him: he performs tayammum and prays, and then repeats that prayer when he is able to reach the water and purify himself with it.

He said in the book of questions and answers mentioned above: if a woman becomes pure from menstrual blood in the month of Ramadan before sunset and fears harm from the full washing, she may perform tayammum and fast, and she need not make up that day. Likewise, a man who has an emission in his sleep during the month of Ramadan: if he fears illness from washing at night, he performs tayammum and fasts, and he does not make up that fast.

He said in a book containing legal questions — all of them marvels — another question: What do you say concerning two men who performed tayammum and prayed together in one place because they found no water, and the prayer of one was valid while the prayer of the other was not?

**Answer:** The case is this: one man said to his companion, "Teach me the tayammum," and the other set about teaching him and striking the earth for him, and they both prayed. The prayer of the one who was taught was valid; the prayer of the teacher was not, because he did not form the intention for himself.

I turn now to the purity of foods and drinks, drawing on the book of questions and answers mentioned above, which one of its compilers arranged according to the order of the chapters of the book دَعَائِمُ الْإِسْلَامِ.

**Question:** What do you say, O you whom God has made a stronghold at whose door even the accursed may hope for refuge, concerning the oil of the neem (النِّيم)? People know that almost all of the neem's fruit is eaten by crows, so that the stones pass out through their droppings and dry, and people gather them and sell them. Pressers buy those stones, press them, and sell the bitter oil that results, in which no trace of impurity is visible. Is it permitted to sell this oil, to buy it, to burn it for light, and to touch it, or not? **Answer:** Whatever is not lawful to eat, its dung is impure. It is not lawful to eat such oil, nor to sell it, nor to buy it.

**Question:** What do you say, O best of those who have given us sight from the stored treasure of his knowledge, of what our eyes were veiled from, concerning what has come in the book of purity — that there is no harm in the leavings of a menstruating woman, a Jew, a Christian, beasts of prey, and riding animals — while it has also come therein that what a dog has lapped from is impure? How are these two reports to be reconciled? **Answer:** If there is moisture, it has been defiled and is impure, and there is no harm in selling it nor in buying it, as with the hide of carrion, which it is not lawful to sell. The primary ruling concerns water that is above the limit.

**Question:** What does our master, the possessor of all, say concerning honey, clarified butter, olive oil, and sesame oil in which a mouse is found? Is such a substance to be fed to the beasts or buried in the ground? And if soap is made from it, may it be sold in the market and elsewhere, or is it to be given to people without price? **Answer:** Any liquid in which an animal having flowing blood has died is not to be eaten, and it is not fitting that an animal lawful to eat be fed what is forbidden. There is no harm in making soap from it, but it may not be sold unless the buyer is informed of its corruption. A garment washed with such impure soap must afterward be rinsed with pure water.

From the book of reports: such a substance is fed to dogs and to beasts whose flesh is not eaten. And from it: if a dead mouse is found in a cooking pot, the broth is poured away, the meat is washed, and it is eaten. From another: food from which a single grain of mouse droppings has been removed is eaten; likewise if there are two. But if three grains come out of it, it is not permitted as food for a human being, nor for an animal whose milk is drunk.

**Question:** What do you say concerning betel that is chewed with a small amount of lime (اَلنُّورَة) put into it? Is that permitted — may it be eaten or not? **Answer:** If the lime is a small amount that does no harm to a person, there is no objection to eating it along with the rest. Similarly, drinking water may contain a small amount of earth, and that is not forbidden unless it is gross and would harm the one who takes it. God and His friends know best.

**Question:** Concerning the clarified butter, sesame oil, sugar, honey, and other such foodstuffs held by unbelievers, which they sell in the markets of the Muslims: is it lawful to eat them or not? **Answer:** And from God is the granting of success. The slaughtered animals of unbelievers were forbidden only because they do not pronounce the name of God over them and do not turn them toward the qibla. As for the food and moist provisions they possess: what they have touched, they have defiled, and what they have not touched with their hands and their impure implements, there is no harm in its being sold, bought, eaten, and used as a relish. Yet their handiwork is hardly ever free of this, since they are impure and handle their implements while these are wet. The obligation, therefore, is to avoid what they have prepared if something other than it can be found. If there is need, then nothing is forbidden except their slaughtered animals, and their moisture is impure. If something falls upon a garment from the relish prepared by the people of association, or from moist food, and it is known that they defiled it, then whatever of that is on the garment or on a person's body is washed.

From another: if the leavings of a Jew, a Christian, a Magian, or a cat fall into a small vessel, purification with it remains valid so long as no saliva is apparent.

He said in the questions of Sheikh Hasan ibn Nuh (نُوح) to our master Husayn (حُسَيْن) ibn Idris, and his answers to him: as for the slaughtered animals of the People of the Book, if a Muslim has observed that they turned them toward the qibla and pronounced the name of God over them, they are eaten. As for the rest of their food, it is lawful by the text of the Noble Book; but if they have touched it with their moisture, it becomes impure. And God knows best.

He said in the brief legal discourse whose questions are from Sheikh Hasan al-Hindi and whose answers are from the masters of Yemen: eating the cooking of a serving girl who is not careful about ritual purity is permitted.

## 11. Bodies, Hair, and the Reform of Custom

I turn now to the matters of cleanliness and the purifications of the natural disposition. I quote first from the book of questions and answers already mentioned, which its compiler arranged according to the order of the chapters of the book *Daaim al-Islam*.

**Question:** What do you say, O diver into the current of the sciences, concerning what has come in the Shariah — that no uncircumcised man is left so in Islam until he is circumcised, even if he reaches eighty years of age? So, our master, if a woman grows old and has still not undergone the circumcision of girls (الْخَفْض), is she to be left without it or not?

**Answer:** She undergoes it and is not left without it.

**Question:** What do you say, O our master, our support, and our pillar, concerning the custom of the lands of India — that men and women part the hair of the head toward the middle and not toward the nape? Does this suffice them as a parting or not?

**Answer:** Let the hair be parted in two halves, from the front of the head to its back, and let no one deliberately neglect this.

**Question:** What do you say, O best of those from whose direction we hope the gates of good will be opened to us, concerning the hair of the beard when some of it is long and some short, and the bearded man shortens part of it so that it is made even, which adds to his comeliness? Is there anything against him in that or not?

**Answer:** He has been permitted to trim from the two sides of the beard (الْعَارِضِينَ). Other than that, it is not permitted to trim from it.

**Question:** What do you say, O one whom God has made a fountainhead of honor for the people of obedience to God, concerning the hair of the human body apart from that whose removal has been commanded? Is there anything against a man if he shaves it, shortens it, or plucks it?

**Answer:** There is nothing against him in that, except the beard — he removes nothing from it.

**Question:** What do you say, O one whom God has made a physician who heals the souls of His friends of every ailment, concerning a man and his wife combing their hair with a single comb, given that some of the people of India dislike this?

**Answer:** There is no reliance to be placed upon that.

**Question:** What do you say, O best of those who have driven from the eyes of our insight, by the light of his knowledge, every blindness, concerning what has come in the book of purity — that the first to be circumcised was Ibrahim (ع) at the age of eighty? Our master, is the intent, in the outward sense, that this ruling and what follows it had not been sent down before that, or not? May I be your ransom.

**Answer:** This is established in the outward sense, for Ibrahim (ع) was not commanded with purification until he reached the age of eighty.

**Question:** What do you say concerning what has come in the book of purity, that Ali (ع) said:

> يَا مَعْشَرَ النِّسَاءِ إِذَا خَفَضْتُنَّ بَنَاتِكُنَّ فَلَا تَسْتَقْصِيهِ وَلْتَتْرُكْ بِهِ شَيْئًا فَهُوَ أَحْسَنُ لِلْوَجْهِ وَأَحْظَى لِلزَّوْجِ
>
> "O company of women, when you circumcise your daughters, do not take it to the utmost, and let something of it be left, for that is better for the face and more favored by the husband."

Our master, is there sin upon them if they take it to the utmost, and what is the meaning of his words, that it is better for the face?

**Answer:** One acts upon what has been mentioned, and it is not taken to the utmost, for that is better for the husband's inclination toward her.

**Question:** What do you say, O best of those whom God has made a storehouse for the secrets of His friends, concerning the saying of the Prophet:

> لَيْسَ لِامْرَأَةٍ حَاضَتْ أَنْ تَتَّخِذَ قُصَّةً وَلَا جُمَّةً
>
> "It is not for a woman who has begun to menstruate to take up a qassa or a jumma."

Our master, may I be your ransom, what is its meaning?

Answer: The jumma (الجمّة) is the plaiting of hair from the side locks to the nape. The qassa (القصّة) is when the hair of the head is cut, leaving about a quarter of it at the front. As for the qunzua (الْقُنْزُعَة), it is when the hair is gathered with certain portions left standing up — and that, too, is forbidden.

Question: What do you say, O sea of the sciences, concerning this: most women of the people of India engrave their palms and feet with henna for adornment, and some dye their teeth with a red preparation or something else, likewise for adornment — yet the Shariah has forbidden the engraving of dye. What is their condition?

Answer: The engraving of dye has indeed been forbidden. As for the teeth, there is no harm in dyeing them.

Question: What does our master say concerning a man who applies henna to his hands and feet for the sake of adornment and not for any benefit? Is that permitted or not?

Answer: It is not fitting for a man to apply henna for adornment. Women are commanded to use it; for a man to leave it when there is no cause is better. Yet there is no sin upon one who does it.

Question: What do you say, O treasury of the jewels of wisdom, concerning a man or woman whose teeth are uneven — some long and some short — and who levels them by filing with a stone or with iron or with something else, as an increase in comeliness? Is there sin in that or not? Favor us with a satisfying answer; may God make us your ransom.

Answer: There is no harm in that for women.

Question: What do you say, O sea of hopes, concerning this: the women of India pierce their own ears and the ears of their infant daughters, and keep the piercings open as far as they are able, so as to wear ornaments. Is there anything against them in that or not?

Answer: There is no harm in that.

Question: What do you say, O one by whom our souls have life, concerning a man who lets the hair of his head be, neither combing it nor parting it each day? Does deficiency enter into his prayer or not? And how often should he comb it and part it?

Answer: If it is in two halves, there is no deficiency upon him. He should not leave it without a parting; and if he does so deliberately in defiance of the ruling, his prayer is corrupted.

Question: What do you say, O dwelling place of all who come to him out of perplexity, concerning combing the hair on the night of Friday, for most of the people of India hold that leaving it is a binding necessity?

Answer: No reliance is to be placed upon that.

Question: What does our master say, may God perpetuate his elevation, concerning what has been mentioned in the Shariah — that the uncircumcised is not left so in Islam until he is circumcised? Is he circumcised after his death if he was a child, or not?

Answer: The preferred practice of the sunna is that he be circumcised after his death.

Another has said: the prayer of a woman who has not undergone the circumcision of girls is valid, as is intercourse with her, and likewise for the man; but the sunna is that one hasten to it.

From the answer of our master Qadi al-Numan, may God sanctify his soul, to one who questioned him, namely the governor of his districts, al-Khattab ibn Wasim: You asked about a man found to be uncircumcised, who had married a woman and fathered children by her and had not been circumcised. It is upon him to be circumcised, for the uncircumcised is not left so in Islam, even if he reaches eighty years of age — unless he was born circumcised, for it has come down that some of the prophets (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) were born in that state. If the glans, or most of it, is exposed, he is not compelled to circumcision; and it is recommended, if he is young, that the skin be drawn back and whatever remains of it over the glans be cut away.

Our master and mawla Jafar ibn Sulayman, may God exalt his sanctity and grant us his intercession, wrote in his noble letter which he named السَّيْفُ الْأَقْطَعُ فِي إِبْطَالِ الْبِدَعِ, by which he counseled his people and roused those among them who had grown heedless and long asleep, among the people of the Island of Yemen while he dwelt and settled there:

Section: Among these matters is what we have come to learn — that some of the womenfolk among your women, especially those bound by covenant among them, crop the hair of their heads by shaving, as men shave their heads. This, together with what we mentioned first, belongs to the contrary and inverted order of things: for that was something with which faces and heads are adorned, and this is something by which heads and faces are disfigured. When one of you reflects upon this, the aspects of the matter become plain to him. God, glorified and exalted, has placed the adornment of men in the hair of their beards, in its combing and its grooming, and the adornment of women in the hair of their heads, in its combing and its lengthening. As for hair, it has come down concerning it, in what has reached us of the prophetic reports, that it is one of the two beauties. So a woman's shaving of it and the cropping of her head is nothing but a disfigurement of her pleasing form. The Prophet has said concerning this that which removes the rust from hearts, where he said:

> إِذَا أَرَادَ أَحَدُكُمْ أَنْ يَتَزَوَّجَ امْرَأَةً فَيَسْأَلُ عَنْ شَعْرِهَا كَمَا يَسْأَلُ عَنْ وَجْهِهَا فَاِنَّ الشَّعْرَ أَحَدُ الْجَمَالَيْنِ
>
> "When one of you wishes to marry a woman, let him ask about her hair as he asks about her face, for the hair is one of the two beauties."

And from him:

> اَلشَّعْرُ الْحَسَنُ مِنْ كِسْوَةِ اللهِ فَمَنْ عَرَّى جِسْمَهُ مِنْ كِسْوَتِهِ لَا سِيَّمَا كِسْوَةِ رَبِّهِ سُبْحَانَهُ الَّتِي كَسَاهُ إِيَّاهَا فَقَدْ شَوَّهَ بِصُورَتِهِ وَمَنْ شَوَّهَ بِصُورَتِهِ شَاهَ فِي دُنْيَاهُ وَآخِرَتِهِ

I marvel at those who have declared this permissible and made it a rule unto themselves, and equally at those who have raised no prohibition against it and offered no restraint — all the more so when every one of them has learned what the noble book *Daaim al-Islam* contains regarding the hair of women's heads, a passage that clearly indicates the prohibition of shaving it, both for the people of the guiding dawa and for others. Its author, may God sanctify his soul, says:

> وقالوا يعني الأئمة من أهل بيت النبوة عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ في المرأة إذا تطهرت إنها تنقض شعرها الا أن تكون تعلم أن الماء يصل إلى بشرة رأسها ويبل شعرها كله وذلك أن تكون أظفار شعرها رخوة

He also wrote on this topic in discussing the shaving and trimming of hair upon release from ihram — for men and for women — the distinction between them in that matter, and the application of the pure Shariah's laws upon their proper foundation. He reported from Ali (ع) that he said, concerning the bald man:

> يَمُرُّ الْمُوسَي عَلَى رَأْسِهِ
>
> "The razor is passed over his head."

And from him (ع):

> إِذَا حَلَّتِ الْمَرْأَةُ مِنْ إِحْرَامِهَا أَخَذَتْ مِنْ أَطْرَافِ قُرُونِ رَأْسِهَا
>
> "When the woman is released from her ihram, she takes from the ends of the plaits of her head."

And he said also, may God sanctify his soul:

> وَقَدْ ذَكَرْنَا أَنَّ الْأَقْرَعَ وَالْأَصْلَعَ يُمِرَّانِ الْمُوسَي عَلَى رَأْسَيْهِمَا وَالْمَرْأَةُ تُقَصِّرُ إِذَا حَلَّتْ مِنَ الْإِحْرَامِ تَقْطَعُ شَيْئًا مِنْ أَطْرَافِ شَعْرِهَا وَلَا تَحْلِقُهُ وَلَا شَيْئًا مِنْهُ

We count this saying of his among the clearest proofs that women are forbidden from shaving their hair — whether a little or a great deal — for he forbade them that very act in the context of their release from ihram, with an emphatic prohibition that admits no ambiguity of any kind, large or small. Whoever follows this path has followed the way of right guidance and has kept misfortune from himself.

> وَمَنْ يَرْغَبُ عَنْ مِلَّةِ إِبْرَاهِيمَ اِلَّا مَنْ سَفِهَ نَفْسَهُ
>
> "And who turns away from the creed of Ibrahim except one who has made a fool of himself?"

Then he said, concerning a matter of the same kind:

Section: Among these matters is what we have come to learn: that the womenfolk among you, entirely — including those who do not shave their hair — hold that they must shave the edges of their heads around the crown, and shave the temples likewise. This is something with which God is not pleased in the woman who does it; indeed, it is among the things that cast them into destruction. This follows from what has already been cited from the saying of our master Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad, set down above — that the man with scalp disease and the bald man pass the razor over their heads, and that the woman shortens her hair when she is released from ihram, cutting something from the ends of her hair and shaving neither it nor anything of it. For if it is not permissible for women, upon their release from ihram, to shave the ends of the hair of their heads — nor anything of it, ever — but rather they cut from it only what they cut, then such shaving is never permissible as a standing custom, and its practice leads to no guidance. Whoever among them contravenes this has contravened the marks of the upright religion and has pulled down its foundation; she has turned away from the creed of Ibrahim, and none turns away from it except one who has made a fool of himself. Nothing is incumbent upon us but this kind of instruction for the people of religion, because of the word of God, glorified and exalted:

> وَمَا عَلَى ٱلرَّسُولِ إِلَّا ٱلْبَلَغُ ٱلْمُبِينُ
>
> "The Messenger has no duty but clear delivery of the message."

Then he said, concerning the circumcision of girls:

Section: Among these matters also is what we have come to learn: that you hasten the circumcision of your girls in the days falling short of ten, or beyond that up to a limit of forty days — which is the outermost term among your elite and common alike. That is contrary to the Shariah of the Prophet and to the religion of Islam, and contrary to what our master Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad set down concerning it in the noble book *Daaim al-Islam*, reporting it from your mawla the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), where he stated it with the clearest exposition; and from him (ع) that he said:

> أَسْرِعُوا بِخِتَانِ أَوْلَادِكُمْ فَأِنَّهُ أَطْهَرُ لَهُمْ
>
> "Hasten the circumcision of your children, for it is purer for them."

And he said:

> لَا تَخْفِضِ الْجَارِيَةَ دُونَ أَنْ تَبْلُغَ سَبْعَ سِنِينَ
>
> "The girl is not circumcised before she reaches seven years."

This is a clear prohibition from the one who summons to guidance, and it may never be transgressed. It belongs to the firm limits of God, blessed be His names.

> وَمَنْ يَتَعَدَّ حُدُودَ فَقَدْ ظَلَمَ نَفْسَهُ
>
> "Whoever transgresses the limits has wronged himself."

We have further learned, in this matter, that at the time of the circumcision of girls, members of the household and neighbors are gathered together, hospitality is prepared, and the forbidden prayer is performed for it with the call to prayer and the summons. People then spread word of the circumcision, so that near and far, close neighbor and distant, all come to hear of it. None of this has any place in that practice. Such ceremony belongs only to the purification of male children. The circumcision of girls is to be carried out in concealment, known to no one except the girl herself, the woman who performs it, and a few of the women of the household. Whoever makes that affair public has offended against the canons of his religion — and offended again. This belongs to the chapter of modesty, of which the Prophet said:

> اَلْحَيَاءُ مِنَ الْإِيمَانِ وَمَنْ لَا حَيَاءَ لَهُ لَا دِينَ لَهُ

By the same measure, the deliberate deferral of girls' circumcision carries a meaning — one among the meanings the Mighty Creator intends in the affairs of His precious religion. His will may not be contravened in this matter, nor His command transgressed in what He has forbidden — glorified be He — in any forbidden matter. This holds in all matters without exception, in their branches and their roots alike. Every sensible matter has an intelligible counterpart set opposite it, and every outward likeness holds within it that which is likened. Whoever contravenes the established practice in this has nullified it and damaged the road and its passages — and that is never permissible, as the friends of God (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), the friends of their ages, have indicated and by which they have given warning. God, glorified and exalted, says concerning this:

> مَٓا أَتَاكُمُ الرَّسُولُ فَخُذُوهُ مَٓا نَهَاكُمْ عَنْهُ فَانْتَهُوا
>
> "Whatever the Messenger gives you, take it; and whatever he forbids you, abstain from it."

Then our master Jafar said, concerning the purification of children:

Section: Among these matters is also what has become known — the delay practiced by most of you in the purification of your male children, whose hastening is commanded in the transmitted prophetic report, in which the speaker among the imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) said: hasten the circumcision of your children, for it is purer for them. This is the reverse of your practice with the girls, and so you have inverted the matter in both cases, contrary to the command of the Mighty Creator. I have learned of people in other regions who leave their children uncircumcised until close upon twenty years of age — and that is a manifest contravention of the Shariah of Islam.

The wonder of wonders, for whoever reflects upon it as it deserves — in what has been of affairs and what shall be, and surely in that are signs for a people who reflect — is this: all the People of the Book among the protected communities who came before you hasten the circumcision of their children with the utmost haste, on the seventh day of their lives, in compliance with what Moses, the one addressed by God (ع), prescribed for them in his law from his Lord. They have carried on with that practice through these successive thousands of years, enduring for their children the pain of it at that tender age. Yet you are nearer than they to the covenant of your Prophet — and still you cast aside his command in this, when he commanded you to hasten, deferring it to what lies beyond and above those days, with this deliberate delay contrary to the canons of the Shariah of Islam. Glory be to God the Mighty, and again glory be to God the Mighty, at what you do in this — for its matter is grave.

> يَعِظُكُمُ اللَّهُ أَنْ تَعُودُوا لِمِثْلِهِ أَبَدًا إِنْ كُنْتُمْ مُؤْمِنِينَ وَبَيَّنَ اللَّهُ لَكُمُ الْآيَاتِ وَاللَّهُ عَلِيمٌ حَكِيمٌ
>
> "God admonishes you never to return to the like of it, if you are believers. God makes clear to you the signs, and God is All-Knowing, All-Wise."

And our master Jafar said, concerning the practice of men applying henna, in the same noble letter:

Section: Among these matters also is what has become known and reported of some among you who are bound by the bond of allegiance to the friends of God (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) — that they imitate the women of their households by dyeing their hands with henna, and some of them their hands and their feet as well. Neither the law of the Prophet nor the religion of Islam permits this. It was appointed in both cases for women and not for men, just as jewelry belongs to women and not to men in every circumstance. A man who does this — taking it from the women, adorning himself with it and making himself resemble them — is like one who adorns himself with their ornaments of silver and gold. None would choose this for himself unless his reason had been lost and gone. Beware of committing it, for it is among the acts of disobedience to God, the Mighty and Exalted. Adorn yourselves in its place with the fear of God, for you will not attain your hope in any of your affairs except through the fear of God. You have heard the report current in the sunna among non-Arabs and Arabs alike:

> حَلْيُ الرِّجَالِ الْأَدَبُ وَحَلْيُ النِّسَاءِ الذَّهَبُ

Leave the opponents of the guiding dawa and what they uphold in this matter, for the one whom they claim as their master and the owner of their affair had already gone before them in that practice — and that detestable deed is reckoning enough for him and for them alike. God, God — keep yourselves clean of it, so that your faces may be made white before Him on the day when faces are made white and faces are made black.

Our master Jafar, may God exalt his sanctity, said in answering the questions that I, his servant and the author of this book, put to him — questions he answered from his Island of Yemen to the Island of Hind: As for what you asked, that it has come in the books of the pure Shariah that girls are not circumcised before seven years — if one of them passes away while below that limit, is she circumcised as male children are circumcised when they have not been circumcised in their lifetimes, or is she buried in her state without it? The point is that, since that stated limit has been set for their circumcision, it is not permissible that it be transgressed in their case.

Our master Muhammad ibn Fahd al-Mukarrami, may God exalt his sanctity, was asked about a daughter who dies before reaching seven years: is she circumcised or not? He answered his questioner: she is not circumcised, because she has not reached the age of circumcision.

Our mawla the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his four hundred counsels:

> اِخْتِنُوا أَوْلَادَكُمْ يَوْمَ السَّابِعِ وَلَا يَمْنَعْكُمْ حَرٌّ وَلَا بَرْدٌ فَأِنَّهُ طَهُورٌ لِلْجَسَدِ وَاِنَّ الْأَرْضَ تَضِجُّ إِلَى اللهِ مِنْ بَوْلِ الْأَقْلَفِ عَلَيْهَا
>
> "Circumcise your children on the seventh day, and let neither heat nor cold prevent you, for it is a purification of the body; and the earth cries out to God because of the urine of the uncircumcised upon it."

## 12. Skins, Blood, and Waiting Periods

I turn now to the purity of skins, bones, hair, and wool. What follows is drawn from the book of questions and answers mentioned earlier, which its compiler arranged in the order of the chapters of the book دَعَائِمُ الْإِسْلَامِ.

Question: What do you say, O ray of the sun of guidance, concerning impure garments — or the skins of carrion, or the skins of beasts of prey — when they cling to the body while either the skin or the body is wet? Must the body be washed, or not? The same question applies to garments washed by the hands of polytheists.

Answer: If any such thing clings to the body, the body must be washed. The same ruling applies to whatever polytheists have washed with their hands.

Question: What do you say concerning what has come in تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ — that the goat is not shorn of its hair as the sheep is shorn of its wool, that benefit may be taken from it while the animals are alive, and that it is lawful to be clothed in it and to pray in it and upon it? The passage further states that the rest of the animal is lawful — its flesh, its skins, and its bones — and that these are purified if the animal is properly slaughtered. Given all this, our master, may one pray upon the goat's hair and skins if they were taken after the slaughter?

Answer: If the goat's hair and skins are taken after it has been properly slaughtered, prayer upon them is permitted. The goat is a kind of sheep: its private parts are uncovered, its tail turns upward, and it has two horns. It is most common in India.

Question: Concerning a lawful animal — when some of its wool, fleece, or hair is shorn from it while it is still alive: must that shorn material be washed as an impure garment is washed, or not? And if there is moisture in it before it is washed, and it touches a garment or the body, does it render them impure, or not?

Answer: The text states that prayer is not permitted in it until it has been washed, yet the material itself is not impure.

Question: What does our master say — may God perpetuate his guidance — concerning a vessel made from the skins of carrion in the district of Waghar, which is a large town whose people are mostly unbelievers? The vessel is filled with ghee or oil and brought to Ahmedabad, and what is in it is then sold. Is that permitted or not? And if soap is made from it, mixed with lye and soda ash, is the sale and purchase of that soap permitted or not?

Answer: If anything edible is placed in this vessel, it becomes impure, and one may not pray in a garment that such a thing has touched. The sale and purchase of the ghee or the soap are permitted, but the buyer must be informed that they are impure. It is not lawful, however, to sell or to buy the skins of carrion themselves.

And from the book تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ:

> وَلَا يُجَزُّ الشَّعْرُ مِنَ الْمَعْزِ اِلَّا بَعْدَ الذَّبْحِ فَاِنْ جُزَّ وَهُوَ حَيٌّ فَهُوَ مَيَّةٌ

The hair of the goat is not to be shorn except after slaughter. If it is shorn while the animal is alive, it counts as carrion. This ruling applies to the goat in particular. And from the same book: the goat is not shorn of its hair as the sheep is shorn of its wool, so that benefit may be taken from it while the animals are alive, and it is lawful to be clothed in it and to pray in it and upon it. The rest of the animal is likewise lawful — its hair, its skins, its bones, and whatever else belongs to it — and these are purified if the animal is properly slaughtered.

And from مُخْتَصَرُ الْمُصَنَّفِ: if a person's bone is broken, it is not permitted to patch it except with the bone of an animal whose flesh is eaten and which has been properly slaughtered. Whoever patches one of his bones with the bone of carrion, or with the bone of an animal whose flesh is not eaten, or with the bone of a dead human being, must remove it and repeat every prayer he has prayed. The ruling authority compels him to do so if his case becomes known. If it is not known until he dies and he himself did not know of it, it is not removed after his death. And one may not take benefit from the hair of human beings as one takes benefit from the hair of beasts.

And from تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ, on the view that benefit may be taken from a covering made of carrion that does not adhere: the meaning is that it does not cling to anything pure while either of the two is wet, so that impurity reaches it. And from كِتَابُ الْأَخْبَارِ:

> وَكَرِهُوا أَنْ يُنْتَفَعَ بِلَحْمِ الْخِنْزِيرِ

And they held it reprehensible to take benefit from the flesh of swine.

I come now to menstruation and postpartum bleeding, drawing again upon that same book of questions and answers.

Question: What do you say concerning what has come in the Sharia — that the menstruating woman wraps herself with a wrapper from below the navel to the knees, and that her husband may have of her what is above the wrapper and what is below it? So, our master, may he touch her whole body short of intercourse? Answer: Yes, he may touch her body, but it is better that he avoid what is beneath the wrapper, for fear that the fluid reach her womb.

Another matter: if a woman menstruates and remains in that state for ten days, and after those ten days the blood continues for two, three, four, or five more days, she does not pray and does not fast, her husband does not approach her, and she does not make up the prayers after her purification, provided she is certain that menstrual blood is distinguishable from the blood of istihadah. If she is in doubt about the blood, then she is a mustahadah.

Another matter: whoever misses the prayer of the Feast of Ghadir and its fast makes them up after that day, on whatever day it may be, performing the make-up at the time of the sun's decline, just as it would have been.

Question: What do you say concerning a woman who becomes pure from menstruation or from postpartum bleeding within the time of a prayer, and when she has finished the washing, the time of the prayer has passed? Is she obliged to make up that prayer or not? Answer: If she was leisurely, she makes it up; but if she was diligent in her washing and did not finish it until the time passed, she is not obliged to make up that prayer.

And from the answers of our master al-Numan, may God sanctify his soul, to the governor of Zawatah, Khattab ibn Wasim: You asked me about a blind woman, and how she is to manage her menstruation. Command her to insert a piece of cotton or a white cloth, and to have it inspected by a woman whose religion she trusts. As long as the blood comes out upon it she is menstruating; and when it comes out white she has become pure, so she washes, prays, and fasts, and her husband comes to her. She does likewise at the beginning of her menstruation if she does not perceive it herself.

Another matter: if a woman becomes pure from menstrual blood in the month of Ramadan before sunset and fears harm from washing, it is permitted to her to perform tayammum and to fast, and she does not make up that fast. Likewise the man who has a nocturnal emission in the month of Ramadan: if he fears illness for himself from washing at night, he performs tayammum and fasts, and he does not make up that fast.

Question: Concerning the woman who becomes pure in the month of Ramadan from menstruation and fasts nine days, and menstruation comes to her on the tenth day — is her fasting of those nine days established or not? Answer: It has been mentioned that the least of menstruation is three days, and the least of purity is ten days.

Question: From the book of purification, that the most of the days of menstruation is ten days: if the bleeding exceeds this term, are her prayer and her fasting established, even if the blood is thick and foul, or not? Answer: Menstrual blood and the blood of istihadah have both been described, and the distinction between them made, so rely upon the text in that matter.

Question: It has come in the Sharia that the limit of postpartum bleeding is forty days, and in some books sixty days. Which of the two, our master, is more correct? Answer: This answer is from our master Hasan ibn Nuh in his own words, may God sanctify his soul. He said: the woman in her postpartum period reckons the nifas according to the duration to which she is accustomed, and the blood of nifas is distinguishable from the blood of istihadah. Whenever she becomes pure and is clean of blood, she washes, prays, and fasts, and her husband comes to her. And it is said in the book of purification: the woman in her postpartum period, when she has given birth, or has seen blood before delivery in a state of menstruation, continues with the blood until it ceases from her; and the menstruating woman washes when she becomes pure. If the blood is prolonged with her, she is a mustahadah, and the blood of nifas is distinguishable from the blood of istihadah, just as we mentioned the distinction of menstrual blood from the blood of istihadah.
