And it is said in مُخْتَصَرُ الْآثَارِ: the woman in her postpartum period is like the menstruating woman, and in a state like hers; what is obligatory upon her and upon her husband in her affair is what is obligatory in menstruation, and she washes when the blood of nifas ceases from her. And it is said in مُخْتَصَرُ الْمُصَنَّفِ: the woman in her postpartum period leaves the prayer for the number of the days of her nifas and according to what her custom has been, if she has a custom; and likewise in دَعَائِمُ الْإِسْلَامِ, which sets her upon this same course.

I myself saw, in one of the books of the Sharia of the people of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), the term of the woman in her postpartum period reckoned by days. It is mentioned there that the least of nifas is twenty days, the middle is forty days, and the most is sixty days; and that this is when the blood continues with her, so that it does not exceed sixty days; and that menstrual blood is other than the blood of istihadah; and that it never falls short of twenty days, and the woman in her postpartum period does not become pure before twenty days. This is the sense of the words of that book. As for its exact wording, I did not find it at the time of writing this page; if I live and come upon that book, I will set down its wording exactly, God willing.

As for the point intended, I have made clear to you that the woman in her postpartum period, when she is clean of blood, has become pure after twenty days. This is the ruling concerning her, decisively. Thereafter she relies upon what she is accustomed to. If the blood exceeds her custom, she looks to see whether the blood has changed or is as it was. If it has changed, then it is the blood of istihadah; and if it has not changed, then its ruling is as I described at first. And our master al-Numan, may God sanctify his soul, mentioned in اَلْمُنْتَخَبَةُ:

> وَالنُّفَسَاءُ تَجْلِسُ قَدْرَ الْحَيْضِ
> فَاِنْ تَمَادَى دَمُهَا بِالْفَيْضِ
> تَرَيَّصْتُ لِمَبْلَغِ الشَّهْرَیْنِ
> ثُمَّ قَضَتْ مَا بَيْنَمَا الْوَقْتَيْنِ
> تَطَهَّرَتْ حِيَاطَةُ لِتُسْلِمَ
> مِنَ الصَّلَاةِ وَالصِّيَامِ بَعْدَ مَا
> إِنِ اسْتَحَاضَتْ بَعْدَ هَذَا الْاِنْقِضَا
> وَقَدْ ذَكَرْتُ حُكْمَهَا فِي مَا مَضَى

This applies when the postpartum blood extends beyond a woman's established pattern, and the blood is turbid, thick, and foul — the character of true nifas blood. In that case, she refrains from prayer and fasting and keeps away from her husband until two full months have elapsed. If the blood continues beyond the two months, she is unquestionably a mustahadah. She then washes and makes up the prayers and fasts she abandoned — from the completion of her customary term to the completion of the two months — by way of precaution, since this is a matter of doubt: it is possible that menstruation followed the nifas. That is the ruling of nifas, and God and His Messenger know best. With this, the answer of our master is complete, by the praise and bounty of God.

It is said in the book مُخْتَصَرُ الْمُصَنَّفِ: if she begins the prayer at its proper time and then menstruates, she need not make up that prayer. If she menstruates for a day or two and the blood then stops, and she observes ten days of purity, that does not count as menstruation — for menstruation is not less than three days — and if she abandoned the prayer during that time, she repeats it. If she sees blood for a day or two, then sees purity for fewer than ten days, then sees blood again, the first and second episodes together constitute menstruation.

It is said in تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ that the word nifas was mentioned only in connection with childbirth, and that what is meant by it is the blood that accompanies it. This is made plain by what was reported from the Messenger of God, who said to one of his wives when she was with him on his bed and she drew back:

> مَا لَكَ أَنْفَسْتَ ؟ قَالَتْ نَعَمْ

What is the matter with you? Have you begun to bleed? She said: Yes. He meant that she had menstruated.

Likewise the mustahadah — she is the woman whose blood does not cease, though it is distinguishable from menstrual blood. When her menstruation is upon her, the blood comes thick and turbid; when it departs, the blood becomes thin. What is binding upon her is this: as long as the menstrual blood is with her, she is in a state of menstruation — she does not pray, does not enter the mosque, and her husband may not approach her. When the menstrual blood departs and the remaining flow thins, she purifies herself as she purifies herself from menstruation; she is then in a state of purity, praying, fasting, entering the mosque, and her husband may come to her. Whenever any of that blood appears, she performs ablution, for it is an ailment affecting her.

It is reported from تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ — regarding the teaching that whoever approaches a menstruating woman has approached what is not lawful to him and has done what he ought not to do — that he must seek God's forgiveness and repent to Him for his offense, and if he gives charity alongside that, it is good. The blood of nifas is distinguishable from the blood of istihadah in the same way that menstrual blood is distinguishable from the blood of istihadah, as we mentioned. It is also reported from تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ: the same obligation applies to the woman if she consented; but if he compelled her, there is nothing upon her. If the man did not know of her menstruation and she concealed it from him until he had intercourse with her, the sin falls upon her and nothing upon him, since he did not know of her menstruation. The menstruating woman does not touch the mushaf.

It is said in the questions that came from the most venerable Shaykh Shimon ibn Muhammad Ghuri al-Hindi to our master Hasan ibn Idris, may God exalt his sanctity.

Question: What do you say, O support of the Dawa, concerning the term of nifas? The common people claim that the limit — both its minimum and its maximum — is forty days.

Answer: It may be less than forty days, except that forty days must be reckoned. And you asked, O magnificent master and forbearing scholar, about menstruation that exceeds ten days: if it exceeds ten days, she is a mustahadah. Our master and mawla, the dear father Muhammad ibn al-Fahd, may God exalt his sanctity, used to say that if a woman sees blood after ten days of her menstruation she is a mustahadah. She washes for each prayer time if she is able, and prays. If she cannot wash, she performs tayammum and prays. If she can wash at one time but not at another, she does so when she is able; and when she is unable, she performs tayammum for that time and prays and fasts.

It is said in the book of the question that came from the most venerable Shaykh Shimon ibn Muhammad Ghuri al-Hindi (رض), together with the answer of our master, the moon of the world and of religion, Hasan ibn Idris, may God exalt his sanctity and grant us his intercession and his company.

Question: What do you say concerning the one in a state of janaba, and the menstruating woman, who recites supplications or other texts containing something of the Quran — whether the reciter knows this or does not? How is such a person to act: does he recite it or leave it? For it is established in the radiant Sharia that neither of these two recites the Quran nor touches it. And may the one in a state of janaba, or the menstruating woman, recite the tahlil, the takbir, the tahmid, and the tasbih, given that all of these appear in the Quran? And may such a person recite the basmala, given that it has been said to be a verse of the Quran?

Answer: The menstruating woman does not recite the Quran, nor does the one in a state of janaba, nor any portion of it — whether in supplication or in books. Nor does such a person recite the basmala with the intention of reciting the Quran.

> فَتَحَقَّقْ مَا ذَكَرْتُهُ لَكَ أَيُّهَا الْأَخُ وَكُنْ مِنَ الْمُوقِنِينَ وَأعْبُدْ رَبَّكَ حَتَّى يَأْتِيَكَ الْيَقِينُ

Hold fast to everything I have set before you, brother — be among those who know with certainty, and worship your Lord until that certainty comes to you.

I turn now to istibra, the clearing of the womb, and I will say what appears in the book of questions and answers I have already cited:

> مَنْ زَنَى بِامْرَأَةٍ بِكْرًا أَوْ ثَيِّبًا فَأَرَادَ أَنْ يَتَزَوَّجَهَا فَلَا يَجُوزُ الْعَقْدُ اِلَّا بَعْدَ الِاسْتِبْرَاءِ

Whoever commits fornication with a woman — virgin or previously married — and then wishes to marry her: the marriage contract is not permitted until her womb has been cleared.

The book الْاِقْتِصَارُ records the following: if a man has a wife who has a child by another man, and that child dies leaving property, her husband withdraws from her until her womb is cleared. If she is pregnant, the child in the womb inherits from the deceased, provided his mother does not inherit from him; otherwise, nothing is cast into the womb that has no right in the inheritance. This applies when the mother is a slave or a polytheist. If she is a free Muslim woman, his inheritance belongs to her rather than to the brothers, and she does not withdraw.

The book مُخْتَصَرُ الْمُصَنَّفِ states that the woman undergoing the clearing of the womb withdraws for two months. The book كِتَابُ الْأَخْبَارِ records two transmitted positions: that a woman who has reached maturity but has not menstruated is cleared by forty-five nights; and that a virgin who reaches maturity, has not menstruated, and is then deflowered by the seller is cleared by nine months.

The book كِتَابُ الزِّينَةِ, concerning the saying:

> اَلْوَلَدُ لِلْفِرَاشِ وَلِلْعَاهِرِ الْحَجَرُ

He said: what is meant is only that the fornicator has no right in the lineage of the child.

The book of questions and answers already mentioned states: whoever commits fornication with a man's slave girl and then buys her from him while she is pregnant may not approach her until she gives birth. If she bears a child, its sale is permitted — even though the child came from him — because الْوَلَدُ لِلْفِرَاشِ، وَلِلزَّانِي الْحَجَرُ.

## 13. The Pillar of Religion and Its Appointed Hours

I now enter the Book of Prayer, beginning with what has been transmitted regarding its merits, what is forbidden within it, and the matters connected to these.

### On the Virtues of Prayer, What Is Forbidden in It, and Related Matters

I record first what the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his four hundred precepts of good conduct:

> قِيَامُ اللَّيْلِ مَصْلَحَةٌ لِلْبَدَنِ وَرِضًى لِلرَّبِّ وَتَعَرُّضٌ لِلرَّحْمَةِ وَتَمَسُّكٌ بِأَخْلَاقِ النَّبِيِّينَ
>
> "Standing in prayer at night is a benefit to the body, a way of earning the Lord's pleasure, an opening of oneself to mercy, and a holding fast to the character of the prophets."

And he said in it:

> وَلَا يَقُومَنَّ الرَّجُلُ إِلَى الصَّلَاةِ مُتَكَاسِلًا وَلَا مُتَنَاعِسًا وَلْيُقْلِلِ الْعَبْدُ الْفِكْرَ إِذَا قَامَ بَيْنَ يَدَيِ اللَّهِ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ فَإِنَّمَا لَهُ مِنْ صَلَاتِهِ مَا أَقْبَلَ عَلَيْهِ وَلَا تَدَعُوا ذِكْرَ اللَّهِ فِي كُلِّ مَكَانٍ عَلَى كُلِّ حَالٍ وَلَا يَلْتَفِتَنَّ أَحَدُكُمْ فِي صَلَاتِهِ فَإِنَّ الْعَبْدَ إِذَا الْتَفَتَ فِيهَا قَالَ اللَّهُ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ: أَنِّي خَيْرٌ لَكَ مِمَّا الْتَفَتَّ إِلَيْهِ
>
> "Let no man rise to prayer in laziness or in drowsiness. Let the servant limit his wandering thoughts when he stands before God Almighty, for he receives of his prayer only that much of it to which he turned his attention. Do not abandon the remembrance of God in any place or in any condition. And let none of you look aside during his prayer, for when the servant looks aside in it, God Almighty says: I am better for you than that toward which you have turned."

And he said in it:

> إِيَّاكُمْ وَالْكَسَلَ فَإِنَّ مَنْ كَسِلَ لَمْ يُؤَدِّ حَقَّ اللَّهِ عَزَّ وَجَلَّ
>
> "Beware of laziness, for whoever grows lazy does not discharge the right of God Almighty."

And he said in it:

> لَا يَعْبَثْ أَحَدُكُمْ بِلِحْيَتِهِ فِي الصَّلَاةِ وَلَا بِمَا يَشْغَلُهُ عَنْهَا
>
> "Let none of you toy with his beard during the prayer, nor with anything that distracts him from it."

And he said in it:

> اَلصَّلَاةُ قُرْبَانٌ لِكُلِّ تَقِيٍّ

> اَلصَّلَاةُ قُرْبَانُ كُلِّ تَقِيٍّ

"Prayer is the offering of every godfearing person." And he said in it:

> لَيْسَ مِنْ عَمَلٍ أَحَبَّ إِلَى اللَّهِ مِنَ الصَّلَاةِ فَلَا تَشْغَلْكُمْ عَنْ أَوْقَاتِهَا أُمُورُ الدُّنْيَا فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ سُبْحَانَهُ ذَمَّ قَوْمًا اسْتَهَانُوا بِأَوْقَاتِهَا
>
> "There is no deed more beloved to God than prayer, so let not the affairs of this world distract you from its appointed times, for God — glory be to Him — reproached a people who made light of its times."

He then cited the words of God Almighty:

> فَوَيْلٌ لِّلْمُصَلِّينَ الَّذِينَ هُمْ عَن صَلَاتِهِمْ سَاهُونَ

"So woe to those who pray — those who are neglectful of their prayer." That is to say, heedless of it.

I turn now to what is recorded in a book of legal questions, each of them a marvel.

Another question: What is said of a man for whom prayer is not permissible, yet who is not excused for abandoning it? The answer is that he is a man who drank wine until his reason was gone. Prayer was therefore not permissible for him because of his drinking, yet he is not excused for leaving it, in view of the words of God Almighty:

> لَا تَقْرَبُوا الصَّلَاةَ وَأَنتُمْ سُكَارَىٰ

"Do not approach prayer while you are intoxicated." Consider what you say.

Another question: What is said of a man who prayed in a state of purity, yet whose prayer was not valid? The answer is that he was a man who had emigrated and prayed behind a bedouin (أَعْرَابِيّ), so his prayer was not valid, because the bedouins are harder in disbelief and hypocrisy, and it is not permissible to pray behind them.

Another question: What is said of an obligatory prayer in which the worshipper performs neither bowing nor prostration? The answer is: a man swimming who is unable to come out of the water onto dry land; upon him there is neither bowing nor prostration.

### On the Mention of Prayer Times and Related Matters

What follows I take from the aforementioned book of questions and answers, which is arranged according to the order of the chapters of the Book of the Pillars of Islam.

Question: What do you say — O you before whose awe the necks of great tyrants have bowed — concerning what has come in the Book of Purity: that when a man remembers his missed prayers (الْفَوَايِت) during the time of a current prayer, he prays the missed prayers first if there is room in the time, and afterward prays the one whose time he is in? Does our master hold that he sins if there is room in the time and he leaves the missed prayers and performs the current one instead, or not?

Answer: Yes, he sins if there is room in the time, nothing hinders him, and he neglects them.

Question: What do you say concerning what has come in the Book of the Pillars — that the Messenger of God, when he prayed the Fajr prayer after its time had passed, began with the sunnah before the obligatory prayer? How, then, should one act who has missed both the sunnah of Fajr and the obligatory Dhuhr?

Answer: He gives the sunnah of Fajr precedence, in making up, over the missed prayers that come after it.

Question: What does our master say concerning a man who prayed the sunnah — two rakahs or four rakahs — and then prayed the obligatory prayer with the imam? How does he pray the remainder of the sunnah: as a performance in its time (أَدَاء) or as a making up (قَضَاء)?

Answer: Whatever remains of the sunnah he makes up if its time has passed. The time of the sunnah is before the obligatory prayer, except in the case of Maghrib.

Question: What does our master say concerning one who performed the obligatory prayer with the imam and had missed the sunnah? Does he perform the sunnah before the optional prayer (نَافِلَة) as a performance in its time, or as a making up?

Answer: There is no harm in his praying the optional prayer as a performance in its time, so long as the time has not passed. The better course is that he pray the optional prayer as a performance in its time and then make up the sunnah.

Question: What does our master say concerning a man who prayed the obligatory prayer at the end of its time, so that the time then passed? Does he pray the optional prayer as a performance in its time, or as a making up?

Answer: He prays the optional prayer as a performance in its time, because he prayed the obligatory prayer as a performance in its time.

Question: What does our master say concerning a man who prays the obligatory Maghrib prayer and then falls to talking while the people are praying the sunnah, goes out to a nearby place, and then returns while the people are again in the sunnah prayer? Does he pray the sunnah as a performance in its time, or as a making up?

Answer: If he prays the sunnah at the time of drawing near and of glorification, then it is a performance in its time; if that has passed, it is a making up. And if he went out to a nearby place and returned while the people were still in the sunnah prayer, it is a performance in its time.

Question: What does our master say concerning a man who arrives while the imam is praying the Asr prayer with the people during the time of Dhuhr, and the latecomer knows that the imam is praying Asr? Does he intend Dhuhr with the imam, or Asr? Or does he pray Asr and Dhuhr apart from the imam?

Answer: If he enters while the imam is in the Asr prayer and the time of Dhuhr is still possible, he intends Dhuhr with him. If the time of Dhuhr has passed, he prays Asr with the imam if he wishes and then makes up Dhuhr. In praying with the imam there is a merit that is encouraged.

Question: Concerning the worshipper who prays the obligatory Dhuhr prayer and then departs while the optional prayer is still owed by him, and becomes occupied until the end of the time of Dhuhr, then wishes to pray it: is it a performance in its time or a making up?

Answer: Our master Malik al-Jami said: if a portion of the time remains, he prays the optional prayer as a performance in its time.

Question: Concerning one who enters the mosque at the declining of the sun and prays the prayer of the declining — six rakahs — and then remembers that sunnah prayers are owed by him from before which he had missed: is it incumbent upon him to make up those sunnah prayers between the prayer of the declining and the obligatory Dhuhr prayer, or not?

Answer: If he prayed the sunnah of the Dhuhr prayer at the first of its time, there is no harm in his making up the sunnah prayers between it and the obligatory prayer.

Question: If prayer within its appointed time is a performance in its time, and upon the passing of the times it is a making up, then how does the worshipper intend the sunnah of Maghrib, seeing that it is not prayed except after the time of Maghrib has passed?

Answer: The sunnah of Maghrib is prayed as a performance in its time after the obligatory prayer, provided that nothing separates the two. And if there was some slight matter and he returned while the congregation had not yet finished the sunnah of Maghrib, he prays it as a performance in its time.

Question: Concerning the worshipper who prays the prayer of the declining as a performance in its time, and when he has finished remembers that a prayer of the declining is owed by him as a making up: is it incumbent upon him to convert what he has already prayed into the making up and begin the performance afresh, or not?

Answer: That is not established. Rather, he makes up the one he missed.

Another matter: whoever prays what he wishes between the obligatory later Isha prayer and its optional prayer, and then prays the optional prayer, does not intend it as a making up, because it is called the night prayer.

Question: Concerning the making up of missed prayers at the rising of the sun, at its setting, and at its standing at the meridian, and the offering of voluntary prayer at these times on the day of Friday: is that permissible or not?

Answer: That is disliked (مَكْرُوه) on Friday and on other days as well.

Another matter: whoever prays the later Isha prayer with the imam while Maghrib is owed by him proceeds in the following order:

1. He makes up Maghrib first.
2. He makes up the sunnah of Maghrib and the later Isha as a making up.
3. He prays the optional prayer of the later Isha as a performance in its time until the middle of the night — and it is called the night prayer.

And whoever prays the Dhuhr prayer while Fajr is owed by him proceeds as follows:

1. He makes up Fajr first.
2. Then the sunnah of Dhuhr.
3. Then the optional prayer of Dhuhr as a making up.

Question: A man abandoned the prescribed prayer in the days of his youth and repented after a long period. How does he act? Does he perform the obligatory prayers for their times, or does he also pray the sunnah prayers and the optional prayers?

Answer: Everyone who abandons the prescribed prayers for a long period without excuse is not counted a Muslim during that period, because of the saying of the Prophet:

> مَا بَيْنَ الْعَبْدِ وَالْكُفْرِ إِلَّا تَرْكُ الصَّلَاةِ
>
> "There is nothing between the servant and disbelief except the abandonment of prayer."

And no deed of the legitimate deeds is accepted from him, because of the saying of our master, the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع):

> اَلصَّلَاةُ عَمُودُ الدِّينِ وَهِيَ أَوَّلُ مَا يَنْظُرُ اللهُ فِيهِ مِنْ عَمَلِ ابْنِ آدَمَ فَإِنْ صَحَّتْ نَظَرَ فِي بَاقِي عَمَلِهِ وَإِنْ لَمْ تَصِحَّ لَمْ يَنْظُرْ لَهُ فِي عَمَلٍ

> لَا حَظَّ فِي الْإِسْلَامِ لِمَنْ تَرَكَ الصَّلَاةَ
>
> "There is no share in Islam for one who abandons prayer."

By "disbelief" I mean here that nothing is accepted from the one who abandons prayer, since he stands in the position of a disbeliever. But what does this imply in practice?

- Is the poll tax to be taken from him?
- Is he to be killed?
- Are his offspring taken captive?
- Is he considered ritually impure, as a disbeliever is?
- Is what he slaughters forbidden to eat?

In all these matters his ruling remains that of a Muslim, because the two testimonies of faith have placed him within the rulings of Islam.

As for the one who finds himself in this condition and then repents — returning to the establishment of the prescribed prayer with a sincere intention toward God, seeking His good pleasure, without display and without hypocrisy — God turns to him in acceptance, for He says:

> يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا تُوبُوا إِلَى اللَّهِ تَوْبَةً نَّصُوحًا عَسَىٰ رَبُّكُمْ أَن يُكَفِّرَ عَنكُمْ سَيِّئَاتِكُمْ وَيُدْخِلَكُمْ جَنَّاتٍ تَجْرِي مِن تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهَارُ
>
> "O you who believe, repent to God with a sincere repentance. It may be that your Lord will efface your evil deeds from you and admit you into gardens beneath which rivers flow."

With that, he truly becomes a Muslim, and all his legitimate deeds are accepted. He is not required to make up the prayers he missed before his repentance; from that point on, he prays every prayer that falls due, together with its sunnah and optional prayers. Should he choose to make up the prescribed prayers he missed before his repentance and ask God's forgiveness for those sins, he is rewarded for doing so. If, however, he had deliberately broken his fast during the daytime of Ramadan before his repentance — during the period in which he was abandoning the prescribed prayer — then making up those fasts and performing the expiation are both required of him.

As for one who misses an obligatory prayer, or several obligatory prayers, out of necessity while otherwise constant in his prayer: he does not perform the sunnah or optional prayers of the current prayer time until he has first made up the obligatory prayer he missed.

The meaning of "O you who believe, repent to God with a sincere repentance" is: O you who have affirmed the two testimonies — that there is no god but God and that Muhammad is the Messenger of God. Faith in this verse is the affirming of those two testimonies.

One further matter: there is no harm in leaving the prayer when food is present, provided the prayer is not thereby missed, as the Prophet said:

> إِذَا حَضَرَ الْعَشَاءُ تَرَكَ الْعِشَاءَ
>
> "When the evening meal is present, the evening prayer is set aside."

This comes from the text of the book on the interpretation of the Pillars: a man may pray outside the prescribed time — meaning the later time — and what he missed of the earlier time would have been better for him than his family and his wealth.

Mukhtasar al-Musannaf records the following rulings for one who wishes to join Dhuhr and Asr on account of some excuse:

- If he prays Dhuhr before the sun's decline and prays Asr after it, he must repeat both prayers.
- If he prays Asr first after the sun's decline and then prays Dhuhr after it, the Dhuhr prayer is valid but he must repeat the Asr.
- If he prays Dhuhr after the sun's decline without ablution, then performs ablution and prays Asr, he must repeat both.
- If the Dhuhr prayer is rendered invalid for any reason whatsoever, praying Asr before it does not suffice him.

Whoever is in doubt about the time — because of cloud cover or any other reason — does not pray until he is certain; prayer at the end of the time with certainty is better than prayer at its beginning with doubt. Whoever prays part of his prayer, or all of it, before the time has entered, and then becomes certain of this, must repeat it.

The reports state: whoever has missed a prayer prays it at whatever time he remembers it, except at the moment of sunrise, the moment when the sun stands at the meridian, or the moment of sunset. So it is mentioned in the reports.

The reports further record that they agreed: there is no prayer after the Fajr prayer until the sun declines, unless one is making up a missed prayer or an obligatory prayer — such as:

- the prayer of the state of consecration,
- the two rakahs of the circumambulation,
- the two festival prayers,
- and what resembles these.

They disapproved of voluntary prayer in that interval, except on Friday, concerning which they said:

> لَا تُسَعَّرُ فِيهَا النِّيرَانُ
>
> "The fires are not stoked on it."

Mukhtasar al-Musannaf also states: there is no harm in a congregation who have missed the prayer making it up outside its time, with the call to prayer and the summons to stand, in congregation.

Another matter, transmitted from a book of questions and answers found in the hand of our master Hasan ibn Abd al-Malik: as for what has come from him (ع), that on Friday voluntary prayer is permissible from the beginning of the day until the Asr prayer — together with praying Dhuhr at the first of its time and after an hour has passed — it is nonetheless not permissible for him to offer voluntary prayer after he has prayed the Asr prayer. For whoever prays Dhuhr and then offers voluntary prayer as he wishes until the end of the Asr prayer, once he has prayed Asr its time has passed, and it is not permissible for him to offer voluntary prayer thereafter.

The book of Clarification records that the Messenger of God joined Dhuhr and Asr, and Maghrib and Isha, without journey, without illness, and without any pressing cause.

The aforementioned book of questions and answers also contains the following:

Question: What do you say, O you who are entrusted with the divine mysteries, concerning the prayer of the middle of the night — two rakahs? Are they prayed after the two halves of the night, when the one rising wakes from sleep, or only at the precise midpoint?

Answer: The time of the declining of the night is the more excellent.

Among the matters whose questions come from our master Sulayman ibn Hasan and whose answers come from our master Dawud (دَاوُد) ibn Ajab, it is recorded: "Your question concerns one who had cut himself off from prayer for a period of time, until God, glory be to Him, favored him with repentance and return, and who had not reckoned how much of it he had missed so as to make it up, and who perhaps prayed at some times and abandoned prayer at others without keeping guard over it. What is obligatory upon him? The answer is that if he repents with a sincere repentance and enters into the noble covenant, then he does not cut himself off from prayer thereafter, and there is no guilt upon him for what has passed; and whoever goes back to it, God will exact His retribution from him."

Our master and father Muhammad ibn al-Fahd ibn Salah also wrote on this subject in a letter. I now set down what that noble epistle contains, ascribing it to him without reservation; it is a path of guidance for those who seek guidance and a disclosure of what is obscure in the questions of fiqh among the matters of religion.

The first question. You asked — and may God the Exalted grant you success — about a man who died and bequeathed three letters' weight of gold to whoever would make up on his behalf the prayers and the fast he had not performed, and you mentioned that this stated wage is small for the one who would undertake to make them up on his behalf. The answer to you in that — and God, glory be to Him, grants success in what is correct — is that the books of the friends of God record: whoever has missed a prayer owes, in order:

1. the making up of its obligatory portions,
2. then the shaf and the witr,
3. then the sunan.

If the stated wage suffices to make up all of that together with the making up of the mentioned fast, then let it discharge both. If it does not, the prayer is advanced before the obligatory fast, and what remains of the wage goes toward the making up of the mentioned fast to the measure of what remains of the stated wage. Nor is it hidden from you that if the deceased owes only the making up of prayer, then the obligatory prayers are made up first, then the shaf and the witr, then the sunan, to the measure of the wage, whether that be half of it, less, or more. And God, glory be to Him, is the Patron of pardon and of acceptance.

He, may God exalt his sanctity, said further in the epistle mentioned:

The second question. You asked also — and may God, glory be to Him and exalted is He, grant you success in what pleases Him — about a woman who had children from a first husband, and who then married another man after him and bore a son by him. This later son killed her, not having reached puberty by his own account. When the matter of her killing was what it was and she died, her first children performed the Hajj on her behalf out of her property — the Hajj of Islam only, of all the religious obligations owed. Then her later son, the one who killed her, regretted what he had done, and he asked you:

- what is obligatory upon him in the killing of her,
- what will deliver him before God, glory be to Him, from the consequence of that deed,
- what good he may do on her behalf whose reward will reach her,
- and likewise whether any portion of the blood money is binding upon him toward those brothers of hers who have been mentioned, or not.

The answer to you in that also — and God, glory be to Him, grants success in what is correct — is that if his question is confined to what will deliver him before God, glory be to Him, from the consequence of that deed, then it is fitting that he undertake on her behalf her release and her discharge, which are known in the books of the friends of God (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ). Then, as to what he wishes to convey to her of good deeds — whether a charity out of some of his property continuing upon the believers, or other than that of the doors of goodness and righteousness — then above all the Hajj, concerning which our master al-Sadiq, Imam Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع), spoke when one of his companions asked him about it and said to him:

> يَا بْنَ رَسُولِ اللهِ أَنَا رَجُلٌ مُوسِرٌ وَقَدْ حَجَجْتُ حِجَّةَ الإِسْلَامِ وَقَدْ سَمِعْتُ مَا فِي التَّطَوُّعِ بِالْحَجِّ مِنَ الرَّغَايِبِ فَهَلْ لِي أَنْ صَدَّقْتُ بِمِثْلِ نَفَقَةِ الْحَجِّ أَوْ أَكْثَرَ مِنْهَا ثَوَابُ الْحَجِّ، فَنَظَرَ عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ إِلَى جَبَلِ أَبِي قُبَيْسٍ وَقَالَ لَهُ: لَوْ صَدَّقْتَ بِوَزْنِ هَذَا مَا أَدْرَكْتَ ثَوَابَ الْحَجِّ.

He is rewarded for what he does, and his reward comes back to him through that deed by virtue of the mercy God — glory be to Him — bestows upon him. This applies provided she was a believing woman who prayed. If she was neither a believer nor one who prayed, he makes up her prayer on her behalf, for prayer is the first of the son of Adam's deeds that God — glory be to Him — looks at. If it is sound, He looks into the rest of his deeds; if it is not sound, He does not look at him in any deed. His making up of her prayer is according to whatever he is capable of — all of the obligatory prayers, or some of them.

> وَمَنْ يَعْمَلْ مِثْقَالَ ذَرَّةٍ خَيْرًا يَرَهُ وَمَنْ يَعْمَلْ مِثْقَالَ ذَرَّةٍ شَرًّا يَرَهُ

Whoever does an atom's weight of good will see it, and whoever does an atom's weight of evil will see it.

He, may God exalt his sanctity, then spoke on the matter of blood money, and I shall bring that forward in its proper place, God willing.

Our master the Judge al-Numan ibn Muhammad, may God exalt his sanctity and multiply his intimacy, said in a letter he wrote to one of the judges, in reply to questions that judge had put to him — among them this: As for what you asked about the time of Dhuhr and 'Asr — when the sun declines, its time has entered, and one is not prevented from the Asr prayer within it in the presence; and that is the beginning of the seventh hour.

He said in the book of the interpretation of the Sharia: Whoever forgets a prayer, let him pray it when he remembers it, for that is its time.

He said in it: The times of prayer are five — hidden, not known except by some indication other than themselves. The time of Dhuhr is when the shadow of everything becomes its own length; the time of 'Asr is when the shadow of everything becomes twice its length; the time of the Maghrib prayer is at the setting of the sun's disc; the time of Isha' al-Akhirah is at the vanishing of the white twilight; and the time of the Fajr prayer runs from the breaking of dawn until the sun is about to rise. These are five times for five prayers. The times are hidden and imperceptible, while the prayers are manifest, being movements that are sensed and stillnesses that are felt. So these make ten, present in every day and night: five outward and five inward.

He said in it that prayer has four boundaries, like the boundaries of a house:

1. the time
2. the qibla
3. the wudu
4. the intention

He said in it: The obligatory elements of prayer are seven:

1. the takbir
2. the recitation
3. the bowing
4. the prostration
5. the glorification
6. the tashahhud
7. the sealing

He said in it: Prayer is not permitted except in its time. He said in it: The Fajr prayer is performed in the last darkness of the night. He said in it: The Maghrib prayer is at the falling of the disc. He said in it: The Prophet said:

> إِذَا اشْتَدَّ الْحَرُّ فَأَبْرِدُوا بِالظُّهْرِ فَإِنَّ شِدَّةَ الْحَرِّ مِنْ لَفْحِ جَهَنَّمَ
>
> "When the heat grows severe, wait for the cool for the noon prayer, for the severity of the heat is from the scorching breath of Hell."

He said in it: Imam al-Muizz li-Din Allah (ع) was asked about the Fajr prayer, whether it belongs to the prayer of the night or to the prayer of the day. He said:

> لَا مِنْ صَلَاةِ اللَّيْلِ وَلَا مِنْ صَلَاةِ النَّهَارِ، لِأَنَّهَا لَوْ كَانَتْ مِنْ صَلَاةِ اللَّيْلِ لَكَانَ يَجُوزُ لِلصَّائِمِ أَنْ يَأْكُلَ وَقْتَ الصَّلَاةِ، وَلَوْ كَانَتْ مِنْ صَلَاةِ النَّهَارِ لَمَا جَهَرَ فِيهَا بِالْقِرَاءَةِ، وَذَلِكَ لِأَنَّ الصَّلَوَاتِ خَمْسٌ: صَلَاتَانِ مِنْ قِسْمِ النَّهَارِ وَصَلَاتَانِ مِنْ قِسْمِ اللَّيْلِ وَصَلَاةٌ مُتَوَسِّطَةٌ بَيْنَ الصَّلَوَاتِ الْأَرْبَعِ لَا مِنْ قِسْمَةِ اللَّيْلِ وَلَا مِنْ قِسْمَةِ النَّهَارِ، وَهَٰذِهِ الْحِكْمَةُ الْبَالِغَةُ.

> وَصَلَاةُ الْفَجْرِ فَرِيضَتُهَا رَكْعَتَانِ بَعْدَ السُّنَّةِ، لِأَنَّهَا دَعْوَةٌ بَعْدَ دَعْوَةٍ مُتَقَدِّمَةٍ وَلَيْسَ بَعْدَهَا دَعْوَةٌ، لِذَلِكَ لَا صَلَاةَ بَعْدَهَا.
>
> "It is neither of the night prayer nor of the day prayer — for if it belonged to the night prayer, the fasting person would be permitted to eat at its time; and if it belonged to the day prayer, the recitation would not be made aloud in it. The prayers are five: two from the portion of the day, two from the portion of the night, and one set midway between all four, belonging to neither the night's division nor the day's division. This is the far-reaching wisdom. The obligatory portion of the Fajr prayer is two units following the sunnah, for it is a call that comes after a preceding call, yet with no call after it — and that is why there is no prayer after it."

Then he said: In the night prayer, the recitation is made aloud. Then he said: Every prayer in which the recitation is made aloud belongs to the night prayer and its portion. Then he said: The Prophet said:

> صَلَاةُ الْغَدَاةِ يَحْضُرُهَا مَلَائِكَةُ اللَّيْلِ وَمَلَائِكَةُ النَّهَارِ
>
> "The morning prayer is attended by the angels of the night and the angels of the day."

Our master Jafar ibn Mansur al-Yaman, may God exalt his sanctity, said in the Book of the Inward Nursing: The time of the first prayer is seven hours of the day. After setting forth the inward meaning of this, he continued: Likewise, the time of Asr follows the time of the first prayer and comes after it. He then said: There are no voluntary prayers after Asr. After it, he said, comes the Isha prayer — by which he meant the Maghrib. After that, he said, comes the prayer of the darkness. He said: The time of the prayer of the darkness begins after the twilight has vanished. Then, after further words, he said: After the night prayer comes the witr prayer, and it consists of three units. In the first unit, after the surah of al-Hamd, one particular surah is recited and no other surah of the Quran — and it is:

> سَبِّحِ اسْمَ رَبِّكَ الْأَعْلَى الَّذِي خَلَقَ فَسَوَّى
>
> "Glorify the name of your Lord, the Most High, who created and gave order."

Then he said: In the second unit, after al-Hamd, he recites:

> قُلْ يَا أَيُّهَا الْكَافِرُونَ
>
> "Say: O you who disbelieve."

Then he said: As for the third unit, after the surah of al-Hamd he recites the surah of sincerity in particular and not the other surahs — and it is:

> قُلْ هُوَ اللَّهُ أَحَدٌ
>
> "Say: He is God, One."

Then he said: In it is the qunut — the qunut of the witr. After bowing and raising his head, the one praying spreads out his hands and supplicates while standing, with the supplication of the qunut. Then he said: After these three units of the witr there is a separation, and then come two units that the one praying performs while seated in both of them. Then he said: In these two seated units he recites these two surahs — the surah:

> قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ الْفَلَقِ
>
> "Say: I take refuge with the Lord of the daybreak,"

in the first unit, and:

> قُلْ أَعُوذُ بِرَبِّ النَّاسِ
>
> "Say: I take refuge with the Lord of mankind,"

in the second — these two surahs specifically, and no others. Then he said: And then the dawn prayer. Then he said: It is the middle prayer, of which God said:

> حَافِظُوا عَلَى الصَّلَوَاتِ وَالصَّلَاةِ الْوُسْطَى وَقُومُوا لِلَّهِ قَانِتِينَ
>
> "Guard strictly the prayers and the middle prayer, and stand before God in devout obedience."

The qunut is in the dawn prayer. Then he said: It consists of four units — two voluntary and two obligatory.

The author of the Hidden Epistle, may God sanctify his spirit, wrote in it: God, mighty and exalted, has laid five prayers upon His servants across the day and night — seventeen units in all — and He has commanded them to observe these prayers with care. The Fajr prayer extends from the first break of morning until sunrise. The first prayer falls at the seventh hour of the day, at the declining of the sun from its rising to its setting. The 'Asr prayer falls at the ninth hour — that is, when the shadow of everything has become twice its length. The Maghrib prayer falls at the setting of the sun and the appearance of darkness. The Isha' al-Akhirah falls when the twilight has vanished and darkness has settled. These are the outward times in which God the Exalted has commanded you to stand before Him in devout obedience.

He wrote in it: There is no prayer after 'Asr until the sun sets. He wrote in it: And there is no prayer between the adhan and the iqamah of the Maghrib prayer, as one prays between the adhan and iqamah of the four prayers.

In the book of questions from the most eminent Shaykh Shimon ibn Muhammad Ghuri al-Hindi (رض), with answers by our master Badr al-Din Hasan ibn Idris:

The question: What do you say, O you who are entrusted with the divine secrets, concerning the prayer of two units in the middle of the night? Is it prayed once both halves of the night have passed, when the one who rises wakes from sleep, or only at the precise midpoint of the night?

The answer: The time of the declining of the night is more excellent.

Our master and lord Hibat Allah ibn Ibrahim said, in his answer to questions from his slave and the lowliest of his servants, Muhammad 'Ali ibn Salih al-Badri — which he answered for him from the island of Yemen to the island of Hind in the year 1145 of the Hijra:

Another question. O our master, may God perpetuate your support: it has come in the books of the radiant Sharia that the fasts men have missed are made up by men. But as for prayer, nothing has come concerning it. So, O our master, do women make up prayer on behalf of men, or not? May our master be gracious with a sufficient answer. And also: if a man has grown weak and is unable to make up what he has missed of prayer, does another make it up on his behalf, or not?

The answer to that, by the help of God the Exalted and the support of His friend and His full moon (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), is that the ruling on the making up of prayer is like the ruling on the fast: none makes it up on behalf of men except men. As for the man who is advanced in years and has reached an age that leaves him too weak for the making up of prayer, he prays as much as he is able, and another makes up on his behalf, and expiation is made. This applies once he has become bent and it is evident that his ability is slight.

## 14. The Disputed Words of the Call

I turn now to the adhan and the iqama.

In the book of questions and answers named at the outset — the one whose compiler arranged it according to the chapter order of Daaim al-Islam — the following is recorded.

The question: What do you say about a man from among the people of the call whom a tyrannical sultan, or his agents, appoints to a position — whether as prayer leader in a mosque, muezzin, teacher, or some other role — paid from the proceeds of the port levy or the jizya, while the people know that the one who holds the port levy takes from their wealth whatever he sees fit, not what is rightfully due? Is it permissible for such a man to accept this position or not?

The answer: This comes from the public treasury (بَيْتُ الْمَال). If it is administered according to the judgment of one appointed by the friends of God, it is lawful for him to consume it; if not, it is not lawful.

From Mukhtasar al-Idah, on the authority of Jafar ibn Muhammad ibn Ali (ع), concerning a man who gives the call to prayer while walking or mounted, he said:

> نَعَمْ إِذَا كَانَ وَقْتُ التَّشَهُّدِ مُسْتَقْبِلَ الْقِبْلَةِ فَلَا بَأْسَ

Yes; if at the moment of the testimony he is facing the direction of prayer, there is no harm in it.

As far as I have learned, and as far as I have seen in what I have gathered from the books attributed to the Ahl al-Bayt (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), there is agreement that the iqama is not valid unless one is standing upon the ground.

From the book Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: it is proper for the muezzin, when he says twice

> اَشْهَدُ أَنَّ مُحَمَّدًا رَسُولُ اللَّهِ

I bear witness that Muhammad is the Messenger of God,

that he say within himself

> اَشْهَدُ أَنَّ عَلِيًّا وَلِيُّ اللَّهِ

I bear witness that Ali is the friend of God.

And when he says twice

> حَيَّ عَلَى خَيْرِ الْعَمَلِ

Come to the best of deeds — that he say within himself:

> آلُ مُحَمَّدٍ خَيْرُ الْبَشَرِ

The family of Muhammad are the best of humankind.

That same book also states that it is not proper for anyone in the mosque, when the muezzin gives the call, to leave before praying — unless he leaves for a necessity and intends to return, knowing he will still reach the prayer; or unless he is without ablution, in which case he may leave to perform it and strive to return in time. If he does not reach the prayer, he prays alone, or with another congregation in another mosque, and no fault rests upon him.

The book Tawil al-Sharia states that it is permissible for the muezzin to give the call while not in a state of purity, but the iqama is not valid except from one who is pure. That book also states: he faces the direction of prayer and forms the intention first. It further states: the obligatory prayer is not prayed when the call has been given — it is prayed when the iqama has been given. The supererogatory prayer is joined to the adhan and the obligatory prayer to the iqama, with the supererogatory coming first. And that book records that the Prophet (ع) said:

> مَنْ أَحْرَمَ فَقَدْ دَخَلَ جَنَّةَ أَهْلِ الْحَقِّ

Whoever enters the sacred state of the prayer has entered the garden of the people of truth.

Our sayyid and mawla Idris ibn Sayyidina Hasan recorded in his book known as Uyun al-Akhbar — in its sixth part, on the matter of the call to prayer with the testimony that our mawla Ali is the friend of God, and that Muhammad and Ali are the best of humankind and their kindred the best of kindred, in his account of our mawla the Commander of the Faithful al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah (ع) and his pure forefathers — these being his words:

The muezzins gave the call in the time of the caliphate of the Commander of the Faithful al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah (ع), and they proclaimed in their call, after the testimony that Muhammad is the Messenger of God:

> أَشْهَدُ أَنَّ مَوْلَانَا عَلِيًّا وَلِيُّ اللَّهِ

I bear witness that our mawla Ali is the friend of God,

in confirmation of the proclamation of the Prophet (ع) on the Day of Ghadir:

> مَنْ كُنْتُ مَوْلَاهُ فَعَلِيٌّ مَوْلَاهُ

Whoever's master I am, Ali is his master.

That was a declaration of loyalty to the Messenger and of the guardianship of his legatee, upon whom he had expressly conferred it and to whom he had pointed. Then they proclaimed in the call:

> مُحَمَّدٌ وَعَلِيٌّ خَيْرُ الْبَشَرِ وَعِتْرَتُهُمَا خَيْرُ الْعِتَرِ

Muhammad and Ali are the best of humankind, and their kindred are the best of kindred.

This made plain the excellence of the Messenger of God, in whose glory they took pride, and who was raised from majesty to the highest of his rank. None was nearer to him than his brother and legatee Ali ibn Abi Talib, his daughter Fatima al-Zahra, his two grandsons al-Hasan and al-Husayn, and the pure Imams whom He chose from the progeny of His Messenger and made guides for His creation along the open road of His path. When Abdullah ibn Abbas was asked who the near kindred are, he said: Ali, and Fatima, and al-Hasan, and al-Husayn. For all his excellence, his learning, and his kinship, Abdullah ibn Abbas claimed it for no one else. The kindred of a man are his nearest folk; yet they relate from Abu Bakr ibn Abi Quhafa that he said:

> نَحْنُ عِتْرَةُ رَسُولِ اللهِ

We are the kindred of the Messenger of God.

By my life, Abu Bakr had no kinship with the Messenger (ع) except that he was of Quraysh — and that is remote when the lineage is traced. Ali and Fatima and al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) are worthier and nearer than every one who kept his company and drew close. Al-Kumayt said:

> بَنِي هَاشِمٍ رَهْطَ النَّبِيِّ مُحَمَّدٍ
> وَلَهُمْ وَبِهِمْ أَرْضَى مِرَارًا وَأَغْضَبُ

The sons of Hashim are the clan of the Prophet Muhammad — for them and through them I am pleased, time and again, and for them I am angered.

This shows that they are his kin. We have already mentioned the near kindred and what has come from Ibn Abbas (رض) in that exposition.

Many among the common people have denied that Ali is the best of all humankind. They say: we grant that Muhammad is the best of humankind, but we do not grant the same of Ali, because among humankind there are prophets. This is a saying whose true meaning they have not grasped and whose grounds they have not understood — a saying of obstinacy, a feeble argument, refuted and turned back upon itself. All Muslims are agreed that the community of Muhammad is the best of communities, for the word of God Most High:

> كُنتُمْ خَيْرَ أُمَّةٍ أُخْرِجَتْ لِلنَّاسِ

You were the best community brought forth for the people.

And among the communities before them were prophets sent as messengers and friends of God given precedence. If they say that this is by way of figurative speech and magnification, we say as they say — and every transgressing sinner is brought to loss. The Prophet (ع) said, in the sound narration from him on the authority of Hudhayfa ibn al-Yaman and others:

> أني مخلف فيكم ما أن تمسكتم به لن تضلوا كتاب الله وعترتي أهل بيتي كسفينة نوح من ركبها نجى ومن تخلف عنها غرق

I leave behind among you that which, if you hold fast to it, you will never go astray: the Book of God and my kindred, the people of my house. They are like the ark of Noah: whoever boards it is saved, and whoever holds back from it is drowned.

And he (ع) said:

> عَلِيٌّ خَيْرُ الْبَشَرِ مَنْ أَبَى ذَلِكَ فَقَدْ كَفَرَ

Ali is the best of humankind, and whoever refuses this has disbelieved.

That is sufficient guidance for the one whose path to truth has been opened. As for the community described as the best community ever raised up for humanity, its meaning has been explained by the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), as we have set out earlier in this book.

In the narration transmitted through its chain from Ali ibn al-Husayn al-Qari, at the inhabited palace in Cairo, he said: I was with the Imam al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah, the Commander of the Faithful (ع), at al-Gharafa, by a mosque known as that of al-Qudai. He was standing there when he heard a great outcry and tumult. He said to one of his attendants: Go and see what this shouting and tumult is, and tell me of it. The young man went and returned and said: O our mawla, they are the people of Atfih, and they are split into two parties. He (ع) said: Bring them to me. The young man went and brought them. Among them was a black man who was clinging to another man and pressing him hard. He (ع) said to him: Release the man. So he released him. The Commander of the Faithful said to the black man: Who are you? He said: I am the preacher at Atfih. He said: And who is the other man? He said: He is the dai. He said: What has passed between you? He said: O our mawla, something has appeared in our town the like of which we have never heard at all. He said: And what is it? He said: He gave the call, and in his call he declared that Muhammad and Ali are the best of humankind, and their kindred the best of kindred. We never heard this call before now. It may be accepted that Muhammad (ع) is the best of humankind, but it cannot be accepted that Ali is the best of humankind — for among humankind are Adam, Noah, Abraham, Moses, and Jesus, and it cannot be accepted that he is better than these prophets, the possessors of the revealed laws.

Our mawla (ع) said: I shall make it plain to you, O shaykh, that Ali (ع) is the best of humankind together with the Prophet (ع). Have you studied any of the hadith, O shaykh? He said: Yes, O Commander of the Faithful, a great deal. He said: Here is a report I bring to you; if it proves sound, confirm it, and if it proves false, refute it — so that I may bring you another. He said: Yes, O our mawla (ع).

He said: Ibn Khallad related to us from Sajja ibn Suwayd, who related it from al-Mamun al-Abbasi, from his father al-Rashid, from his father al-Mahdi, from his father al-Mansur, from Abdullah ibn Abbas, who said: One day we were gathered with the Messenger of God (ع), and he was speaking to us and teaching us the understanding of our religion, when his daughter Fatima al-Zahra (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) came to him with her eyes pouring out tears. The Prophet sprang up and said: O my daughter, what makes you weep? She said: O my father, I have lost my two sons al-Hasan and al-Husayn, and I feared my heart would split from the loss of them, O my father. He said: The Prophet raised his gaze to the heavens, grew tender, and prayed. Then Gabriel the Trustworthy descended upon him and said: O Muhammad, peace be upon you, do not be alarmed. The Peace sends you peace, and He says to you: your two sons al-Hasan and al-Husayn went to the enclosure of the Banu al-Najjar and played there, and sleep overcame them. So He sent to them an angel, kindly and gentle, who spread his right wing beneath them and covered them with his left — and so he found them and took charge of them.

The Messenger of God (ع) set out for the enclosure of the Banu al-Najjar, and his companions went with him. There he saw al-Hasan and al-Husayn (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) sleeping, with the angel seated beside them, guarding them by the command of God, mighty and majestic. The Prophet lifted them onto his shoulder, and Abu Bakr said: Let me carry them for you, O Messenger of God. He said:

> ذرهما فنعم المطي مطيهما ونعم الراكبان هما وأبوهما خير منهما

Leave them be, for excellent is their mount, and excellent riders are they both, and their father is better than the two of them.

He carried them on his shoulders and quickened his steps, hurrying to bring the good news to Fatima (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), until he reached the mosque. There he came upon a group of people who had been alarmed by this. He said:

> أَيُّهَا النَّاسُ أَنِّي أُخْبِرُكُمْ بِخَيْرِ النَّاسِ أَبًا وَأُمًّا هُمَا هَذَانِ الْحَسَنُ وَالْحُسَيْنُ أَبُوهُمَا عَلِيٌّ وَصِيِّي أَفْضَلُ الْوَصِيِّينَ وَأُمُّهُمَا فَاطِمَةُ ابْنَتِي أَفْضَلُ نِسَاءِ الْعَالَمِينَ أَلَا أُخْبِرُكُمْ بِخَيْرِ النَّاسِ جَدًّا وَجَدَّةً هَذَانِ الْحَسَنُ وَالْحُسَيْنُ جَدُّهُمَا رَسُولُ اللَّهِ وَجَدَّتُهُمَا خَدِيجَةُ أَوَّلُ مَنْ آمَنَ بِاللَّهِ وَرَسُولِهِ أَلَا أُخْبِرُكُمْ بِخَيْرِ النَّاسِ عَمًّا وَعَمَّةً
>
> "O people, I tell you who the best of people are in father and mother: they are these two — al-Hasan and al-Husayn. Their father is Ali, my legatee and the finest of all legatees; their mother is Fatima, my daughter and the finest of the women of the worlds. Shall I tell you who the best of people are in grandfather and grandmother? These two — al-Hasan and al-Husayn. Their grandfather is the Messenger of God, and their grandmother is Khadija, the first to believe in God and His Messenger. Shall I tell you who the best of people are in paternal uncle and paternal aunt?"

They said: Yes, O Messenger of God. He said:

> هَذَانِ الْحَسَنُ وَالْحُسَيْنُ عَمُّهُمَا جَعْفَرُ بْنُ أَبِي طَالِبٍ وَعَمَّتُهُمَا أُمُّ هَانِيٍ بِنْتُ أَبِي طَالِبٍ مَا أَشْرَكَتْ بِاللهِ طَرْفَةَ عَيْنٍ أَلَا أُخْبِرُكُمْ بِخَيْرِ النَّاسِ خَالًا وَخَالَةً هَذَانِ الْحَسَنُ وَالْحُسَيْنُ خَالُهُمَا قَاسِمُ بْنُ رَسُولِ اللهِ وَخَالَتُهُمَا زَيْنَبُ بِنْتُ رَسُولِ اللهِ اللَّهُمَّ أَنَّكَ تَعْلَمُ اَنَّهُمَا وَأَبَاهُمَا وَأُمَّهُمَا وَجَدَّهُمَا وَجَدَّتَهُمَا وَخَالَهُمَا وَخَالَتَهُمَا وَعَمَّهُمَا وَعَمَّتَهُمَا وَشِيعَتَهُمَا وَمُحِبِّيهِمَا فِي الْجَنَّةِ

Is this hadith sound? He said: Yes, by God, O Commander of the Faithful — and it is indeed among the choicest of reports. The Commander of the Faithful said: And in the hadith, the Messenger of God (ع) said before a company of his companions:

> وَلَدَايَ هَذَانِ إِمَامَا حَقٍّ قَامَا أَوْ قَعَدَا وَأَبُوهُمَا خَيْرٌ مِنْهُمَا

These two sons of mine are two rightful Imams, whether they rise or sit, and their father is better than the two of them.

He said: Yes. And the Prophet (ع) said:

> وَلَدَايَ هَذَانِ سَيِّدَا شَبَابِ أَهْلِ الْجَنَّةِ

These two sons of mine are the two masters of the youth of the people of the Garden.

Is the report sound, O shaykh, or not? He said: It is sound, O Commander of the Faithful. He said: Is it not in the report that the Messenger of God (ع) said:

> إِنَّ أَهْلَ الْجَنَّةِ شَبَابٌ لَا يَهْرَمُونَ

The people of the Garden are youths who do not grow old.

He said: Yes. He said: Is that sound? He said: Yes, O Commander of the Faithful. He said to him: O shaykh, are Adam and Noah and Abraham and Moses and Jesus in the Garden, or not? He said: Yes indeed, O Commander of the Faithful. He said: O shaykh, then if al-Hasan and al-Husayn are the two masters of the youth of the people of the Garden, and their father is better than the two of them, it is established that Ali is the best of humankind together with the Prophet (ع). At that the shaykh said: I have believed and I have affirmed it as true. Then our mawla al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah, the Commander of the Faithful (ع), turned to the dai and said: O shaykh, give the call as you wish, for no one will oppose you.

I now turn to the matter of the Commander of the Faithful Ali (ع) and the matter of the call to prayer. I set down here a complete epistle by our sayyid and mawla and father Muhammad ibn Fahd — may God exalt his sanctity — the epistle entitled *al-Muriba 'amma jarat bihi al-mubahatha*, in which there is a lesson for whoever takes a lesson from it. It concerns the question of the words of the one who says in the call to prayer that Muhammad and Ali are the best of humankind and their kindred the best of kindred: between the one who holds that this saying is not permitted in the canons of the upright religion and is not among what has been narrated in the transmitted reports, and the one who holds that it is among what the Noble Book has commanded, and that upholding it belongs to whoever obeys the command of those whose command is to be obeyed.

## 15. Eight Proofs for the Best of Human Beings

In the name of God, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful, and in Him I seek help.

> بِسْمِ اللَّهِ الرَّحْمَنِ الرَّحِيمِ وَبِهِ اَسْتَعِينُ

Praise belongs to God, Lord of the worlds, and may God bless His messenger, our master Muhammad, and his family, and grant them abundant peace. Having praised God, Lord of the worlds, and invoked blessings upon the best of His messengers, Muhammad al-Amin, upon his guardian, the most honorable of guardians, and upon all who are of the household of the two of them, the good and the pure — and (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ):

A discussion took place between two jurists among the people of knowledge, and I am obliged to record both the questioner's words and those of the one questioned, so that whoever comes upon this account may know which of the two spoke truth and which spoke outside the bounds of truth, guidance, and reasonable speech.

One of the two jurists said to the other: "How is it that the people of your school say in the call to prayer, 'Muhammad and Ali are the best of human beings, and their kindred are the best of kindred'? No other group in Islam says this."

The other jurist, the one questioned about it, answered him with these words, drawn from the saying of God, blessed and exalted:

> مَنْ عَمِلَ صَالِحاً فَلِنَفْسِهِ وَمَنْ أَسَاءَ فَعَلَيْهَا

Whoever does righteous work does so for his own soul, and whoever does evil does so against it.

"If our saying in the call to prayer — 'Muhammad and Ali are the best of human beings, and their kindred are the best of kindred' — is righteous work, then we have done it only for ourselves. And if it is not righteous work but wrongdoing, then it falls back upon us alone, especially in light of His saying, exalted is He:

> وَلَا تَزِرُ وَازِرَةٌ وِزْرَ أُخْرَى

No bearer of burdens shall bear the burden of another."

At that the questioner said to him: "You have spoken truly in what you have cited from these two verses of the most truthful of speakers. But I ask you for the clear argument and the plain proof you possess for the soundness of what you have mentioned — namely, that Muhammad, may God bless him and his family, and Ali are the best of human beings, and that you set Ali as the like of Muhammad, blessings be upon them both, according to what you employ in the call to prayer. It is a known matter that Muhammad, may God bless him and his family, is the best of human beings, since it is established that he is the master of the messengers. But as for Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), it is not established that he is the like of the Messenger in this excellence until you prove it to me with a plain proof."

The respondent answered him: You have asked me a question that must be answered. I shall make this clear with illuminating proofs, to the measure of my capacity and strength, and above every possessor of knowledge there is One who knows.

**1. The first proof.** I say, with the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that since the word of God is:

> إِنَّمَا يُرِيدُ اللهُ لِيُذْهِبَ عَنكُمُ الرِّجْسَ أَهْلَ الْبَيْتِ وَيُطَهِّرَكُمْ تَطْهِيرًا

God only wishes to remove all impurity from you, people of the household, and to purify you completely.

The people of the household are thereby established as equal to the Prophet in purity. The most excellent of the people of the household after him is Ali ibn Abi Talib, the Commander of the Believers, the blessings of God be upon him, who is the Prophet's guardian and friend — may God bless him and his family — and his successor in his community. Likeness to the Prophet is therefore established for him. He is the best of human beings alongside him, and their kindred are the best of kindred, by reason of their likeness to the two of them in purity. This is among the clearest of matters, leaving no room for ambiguity, for it rests on the word of the glorious Quran:

> لَا يَأْتِيهِ الْبَاطِلُ مِنْ بَيْنِ يَدَيْهِ وَلَا مِنْ خَلْفِهِ تَنزِيلٌ مِنْ حَكِيمٍ حَمِيدٍ

Falsehood does not come to it from before it nor from behind it; it is a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

**2. The second proof.** We say, with the help and enabling grace of God, that since the word of God, exalted is He, in His clear Book is:

> سَلَامٌ عَلَى آلِ يَاسِينَ إِنَّا كَذَلِكَ نَجْزِي الْمُحْسِنِينَ

Peace be upon the family of Yasin. Thus do We reward those who do good.

This verse declares that the family of Yasin — upon whom God, glorified is He, has sent peace — are the family of the Messenger of God, may God bless him and his family: the excellent ones, the people of his household. The first and most excellent among them was the one whom the Messenger of God, may God bless him and his family, chose as his guardian over his community, and that is Ali ibn Abi Talib.

It is therefore established that only the best of human beings could be the most excellent of the family of Yasin: those upon whom God sent peace and whom He described as those who do good. For the people of the household who came after him — who are the family of Yasin — are themselves among human beings and of them, and so he is the best of them. This is among the clear matters, leaving no room for ambiguity, for it rests on the word of the glorious Quran, to which falsehood does not come from before it nor from behind it, a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

**3. The third proof.** We say, with the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that the word of God, exalted is He, in this noble verse:

> كُنتُمْ خَيْرَ أُمَّةٍ أُخْرِجَتْ لِلنَّاسِ تَأْمُرُونَ بِالْمَعْرُوفِ وَتَنْهَوْنَ عَنِ الْمُنكَرِ وَتُؤْمِنُونَ بِاللهِ

You are the best community brought forth for humankind: you enjoin what is right, forbid what is wrong, and believe in God.

is an address from Him to the people of the household of the Messenger of God. His statement, exalted is He, that they are the best community brought forth for humankind requires that what He has appointed them to be admit of no alteration — namely, that they are the best community brought forth for humankind. Since the most excellent and the first of them is the Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib, according to what has already been explained in the two preceding proofs, it is therefore sound and established, and again sound and established, that he is the best of human beings after the Prophet and together with him, blessings be upon them both. This is among the clear matters, leaving no room for ambiguity, for it rests on the word of the glorious Quran, to which falsehood does not come from before it nor from behind it, a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

**4. The fourth proof.** We say, with the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that God has recounted in His noble Book the request of His prophet Moses (ع), which is his saying:

> وَاجْعَل لِّي وَزِيرًا مِّنْ أَهْلِي هَارُونَ أَخِي اشْدُدْ بِهِ أَزْرِي وَأَشْرِكْهُ فِي أَمْرِي كَيْ نُسَبِّحَكَ كَثِيرًا وَنَذْكُرَكَ كَثِيرًا إِنَّكَ كُنتَ بِنَا بَصِيرًا
>
> "And appoint for me a minister from my family, Aaron my brother. Strengthen my back through him and let him share in my task, so that we may glorify You much and remember You much. Indeed, You are ever watching over us."

The Creator's answer to him, exalted is He, was His saying:

> قَدْ أُوتِيتَ سُؤْلَكَ يَا مُوسَى

You have been granted your request, Moses.

From this I understand that Moses (ع) asked God, glorified is He, to appoint his brother Aaron as a minister — one through whom his back would be strengthened and who would share in his task. He stated his own reason openly: that together they might glorify Him much and remember Him much, for He is ever watching over them. Then, in another place of His noble Book, God, exalted is He, relates what Moses (ع) — the one He had addressed — went on to say:

> وَأَخِي هَارُونُ هُوَ أَفْصَحُ مِنِّي لِسَانًا فَأَرْسِلْهُ مَعِيَ رِدْءًا يُصَدِّقُنِي إِنِّي أَخَافُ أَن يُكَذِّبُونِ

And my brother Aaron is more eloquent than I in speech, so send him with me as a helper to confirm me. I fear that they will call me a liar.

And His saying, exalted is He, in answer to that:

> سَنَشُدُّ عَضُدَكَ بِأَخِيكَ وَنَجْعَلُ لَكُمَا سُلْطَانًا فَلَا يَصِلُونَ إِلَيْكُمَا بِآيَاتِنَا أَنتُمَا وَمَنِ اتَّبَعَكُمَا الْغَالِبُونَ

We shall strengthen your arm through your brother and grant you both authority, so that they will not reach you. By Our signs, you two and those who follow you will be the victors.

In another verse of His noble Book, God, exalted is He, speaks to His prophet Muhammad (ع):

> أُولَٰئِكَ الَّذِينَ هَدَى اللهُ فَبِهُدَاهُمُ اقْتَدِهْ

Those are the ones whom God has guided, so follow their guidance.

And in another verse of it:

> مَا يُقَالُ لَكَ إِلَّا مَا قَدْ قِيلَ لِلرُّسُلِ مِن قَبْلِكَ

Nothing is said to you except what was said to the messengers before you.

And in another verse of it:

> شَرَعَ لَكُم مِّنَ الدِّينِ مَا وَصَّى بِهِ نُوحًا وَالَّذِي أَوْحَيْنَا إِلَيْكَ وَمَا وَصَّيْنَا بِهِ إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَمُوسَى وَعِيسَى أَنْ أَقِيمُوا الدِّينَ وَلَا تَتَفَرَّقُوا فِيهِ

He has ordained for you of religion what He enjoined upon Noah, and what We have revealed to you, and what We enjoined upon Abraham and Moses and Jesus: that you establish the religion and not be divided in it.

All of this obliges the Messenger to appoint the Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib, as his guardian, his minister, and his brother, to have him strengthen his back and share in his task. It further establishes that God, glorified is He, strengthened his arm through him and granted to him and to the other the authority mentioned — especially given His words to him, exalted is He, in one place of His noble Book:

> وَلَن تَجِدَ لِسُنَّةِ اللهِ تَبْدِيلًا

You will never find any change in the way of God.

And in another place:

> وَلَن تَجِدَ لِسُنَّةِ اللهِ تَحْوِيلًا

You will never find any alteration in the way of God.

This being so, it is sound and firmly established that Ali (ع) is the minister of the Messenger of God, his brother, and his partner in his task and in the authority that God appointed for them both. This is confirmed by what has been narrated from the Messenger concerning him — his saying:

> عَلِيٌّ مِنِّي بِمَنْزِلَةِ هَارُونَ مِنْ مُوسَى إِلَّا أَنَّهُ لَا نَبِيَّ بَعْدِي

Ali stands to me in the station of Aaron to Moses, except that there is no prophet after me.

This being so, it is sound and firmly established — established again and beyond all doubt — by the force of these noble verses of the glorious Quran, to which falsehood cannot come from before it or from behind it: a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy, of which He said:

> إِنَّ هَٰذَا الْقُرْآنَ يَهْدِي لِلَّتِي هِيَ أَقْوَمُ

Indeed, this Quran guides to that which is most upright.

And by the force of the Prophet's words already cited, it is established that Ali, the Commander of the Believers, stands among the best of human beings after the Messenger — for God linked him to the Messenger in both task and authority. This is among the clearest and most self-evident of matters, admitting no ambiguity, for it rests on the word of the glorious القرآن, to which falsehood cannot come from before it or from behind it: a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

**5. The fifth proof.** We say, by the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that the word of God, mighty and majestic, in His noble Book is:

> إِنَّمَا وَلِيُّكُمُ اللهُ وَرَسُولُهُ وَالَّذِينَ آمَنُوا الَّذِينَ يُقِيمُونَ الصَّلَاةَ وَيُؤْتُونَ الزَّكَاةَ وَهُمْ رَاكِعُونَ

Your guardian is only God, and His Messenger, and those who believe: those who establish the prayer and give the alms while bowing.

> وَمَن يَتَوَلَّ اللهَ وَرَسُولَهُ وَالَّذِينَ آمَنُوا فَإِنَّ حِزْبَ اللهِ هُمُ الْغَالِبُونَ

And whoever takes God and His Messenger and those who believe as guardians, then the party of God are the victors.

This requires that those whom God, exalted is He, named in that verse as the believers — those He described as establishing the prayer and giving the alms, and as the victorious party of God — are among the people of bounty, of whom God said:

> وَيُؤْتِ كُلَّ ذِي فَضْلٍ فَضْلَهُ

And He gives every possessor of bounty his bounty.

And of whom He said:

> إِنَّ الْفَضْلَ بِيَدِ اللهِ يُؤْتِيهِ مَن يَشَاءُ وَاللهُ ذُو الْفَضْلِ الْعَظِيمِ

Indeed, bounty is in the hand of God; He gives it to whomever He wills, and God is the possessor of great bounty.

And no one among human beings had a better right or a stronger claim to this bounty than the people of the household of the Messenger of God, of whom God said:

> إِنَّمَا يُرِيدُ اللهُ لِيُذْهِبَ عَنكُمُ الرِّجْسَ أَهْلَ الْبَيْتِ وَيُطَهِّرَكُمْ تَطْهِيرًا

God only wishes to remove all impurity from you, people of the household, and to purify you completely.

For His will is turned back by none of the created. And God also said of them:

> كُنتُمْ خَيْرَ أُمَّةٍ أُخْرِجَتْ لِلنَّاسِ تَأْمُرُونَ بِالْمَعْرُوفِ وَتَنْهَوْنَ عَنِ الْمُنكَرِ وَتُؤْمِنُونَ بِاللهِ

You are the best community brought forth for humankind: you enjoin what is right, forbid what is wrong, and believe in God.

And God also said of them:

> سَلَامٌ عَلَى آلِ يَاسِينَ إِنَّا كَذَلِكَ نَجْزِي الْمُحْسِنِينَ

Peace be upon the family of Yasin. Thus do We reward those who do good.

The first and most excellent of them was the one whom the Messenger made his brother, his guardian, his minister, and his successor over his community. It is therefore sound and established — and sound and established once more — that Ali ibn Abi Talib holds command after the Messenger, is the one intended by the verse cited above, and is the Messenger's partner in guardianship over the believers. No one can occupy such a station except one who is the best of human beings after the Messenger and alongside him, by reason of that shared guardianship. This is among the clearest of matters, admitting no ambiguity, for it rests upon the words of the glorious Quran — to which falsehood does not come from before it nor from behind it, a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy — and especially so after the clarification the Messenger of God gave on the day of the festival of Ghadir Khumm, the day of the explicit designation of Ali: that he had made him, like himself, a master over the believers in his lifetime, saying:

> مَنْ كُنْتُ مَوْلَاهُ فَهَذَا عَلِيٌّ مَوْلَاهُ اللَّهُمَّ وَالِ مَنْ وَالَاهُ وَعَادِ مَنْ عَادَاهُ وَانْصُرْ مَنْ نَصَرَهُ وَاخْذُلْ مَنْ خَذَلَهُ وَأَدِرِ الْحَقَّ مَعَهُ حَيْثُ دَارَ

And truly, even the portion we have already set forth — these verses and arguments — is sufficient, fully sufficient, for anyone who has a heart, or who gives ear while he is a witness.

**6. The sixth proof.** We say, with the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that the word of God, exalted is He, in this noble verse of His noble Book is:

> إِنَّ الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا وَعَمِلُوا الصَّالِحَاتِ أُولَٰئِكَ هُمْ خَيْرُ الْبَرِيَّةِ

Indeed, those who believe and do righteous deeds are the best of creation.

It is a known matter that the people of the household of the Messenger are the best of creation — for they are the finest of those who believed in God and performed righteous deeds, and because of what God has declared concerning them: that they are the best community brought forth for humankind. It is likewise a known matter that Ali, the Commander of the Believers, is the most excellent of the people of the household. It therefore follows, soundly and firmly, that our words in the call to prayer — that Muhammad and Ali are the best of human beings, and their kindred are the best of kindred — are the truth, free of all ambiguity. This truth comes from the glorious Quran, to which falsehood does not come from before it nor from behind it: a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

**7. The seventh proof.** We say, with the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that since the word of God in His noble Book is:

> إِنَّ اللهَ اصْطَفَى آدَمَ وَنُوحًا وَآلَ إِبْرَاهِيمَ وَآلَ عِمْرَانَ عَلَى الْعَالَمِينَ ذُرِّيَّةً بَعْضُهَا مِنْ بَعْضٍ وَاللهُ سَمِيعٌ عَلِيمٌ

Indeed, God chose Adam and Noah and the family of Abraham and the family of Imran above all the worlds, descendants one from another, and God is Hearing, Knowing.

This verse makes plain that God chose the family of Abraham and the family of Imran just as He chose Adam and Noah, singling them out for His message and prophethood. Now, it is well established that the Messenger of God is more excellent than both Adam and Noah. It therefore follows, soundly, that his family is more excellent than the family of Abraham and the family of Imran (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) taken together. And since this is so, it follows equally that our master the Commander of the Believers, Ali ibn Abi Talib, is the most excellent member of the Messenger's family. It is therefore sound and established — sound and established — that our saying in the call to prayer, that Muhammad and Ali are the best of human beings and their kindred are the best of kindred, is among those whose soundness and firmness are witnessed by the verses of the glorious Quran, to which falsehood does not come from before it nor from behind it: a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

**8. The eighth proof.** We say, with the help and enabling grace of God, exalted is He, that since the word of God, exalted is He, in this noble verse is:

> وَلَقَدْ نَجَّيْنَا بَنِي إِسْرَائِيلَ مِنَ الْعَذَابِ الْمُهِينِ مِن فِرْعَوْنَ إِنَّهُ كَانَ عَالِيًا مِّنَ الْمُسْرِفِينَ وَلَقَدِ اخْتَرْنَاهُمْ عَلَى عِلْمٍ عَلَى الْعَالَمِينَ

This verse makes plain that God chose the Children of Israel, in their era, above all the worlds and preferred them over all creation — for the worlds are human beings, and human beings are creation. The family of the Messenger, then, are the best of human beings not only in their own era but also in the eras of all who came before them, since it is established that the Messenger is the master of all messengers and all prophets — the most honorable and excellent of them all. This being so, it is sound and settled — and sound and settled again — that his family, who are his kindred, are more excellent than the family of every prophet and more honorable than the kindred of every prophet, and that they are chosen above all the people of his era. It is likewise established that our saying in the call to prayer — that Muhammad and Ali are the best of human beings and their kindred are the best of kindred — is true and correct speech, because Ali is the most excellent of the family of the Messenger of God, the most honorable among them and the best of them. This judgment is a true judgment, as attested by these verses of the noble Book that we have cited here — along with others like them that we have not cited — and as attested by the noble verse we have mentioned, from the glorious Quran, to which falsehood does not come from before it nor from behind it: a revelation from One who is Wise, Praiseworthy.

Having completed the mention of these eight answers on the matter in question, together with the proofs drawn from the saying of the most truthful of speakers — proofs that expand the breast and remove doubt from the hearts of believers — we now seal this treatise of ours, which we devoted to that subject, with praise to God, Lord of the worlds, and with blessings upon our master Muhammad, master of the messengers, and upon his pure family until the Day of Judgment. (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ). God is sufficient for us, and how excellent a trustee.

In the name of God, the Most Gracious, the Most Merciful.

There is a confusion raised at this point by certain objectors who oppose the noble school — namely, that what Umar ibn al-Khattab removed from the call to prayer, the phrase

> حَيَّ عَلَى خَيْرِ الْعَمَلِ

is not to be accepted as a charge against him, because no such narration exists in their books; and that even granting it, it is neither objectionable nor an error, since it was a sound exercise of judgment; and that if it were permissible to call it an error on his part, then what certain imams added — the saying

> أَشْهَدُ أَنَّ مَوْلَانَا عَلِيًّا وَلِيُّ اللهِ

— would likewise be an error, since one who adds to the call to prayer is like one who subtracts from it.

I say: this is confusion on his part and a sophistry played upon fancies, as I shall set out and clarify in what follows, with the help and enabling grace of God. Even if we were to concede, for the sake of argument, that error lies in both addition and subtraction in the call to prayer, we do not concede that the one who adds and the one who subtracts stand equal in their degree of error. To make them equal is gross error and plain misguidance, for several reasons:

**The first:** consider how many are those who follow the subtraction from the call to prayer compared with those who follow the addition in it — as one to a hundred thousand or fewer. The one who subtracted therefore led great numbers of people astray, so his offense is greater than that of the one who added.

**The second:** he ascribes this addition to our master the Imam al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah, the blessings of God be upon him. If that is granted, then the one who subtracted must have preceded him in error and in leading the community astray by more than four hundred years, since al-Hakim was not born until after the fourth century. The one who goes before in error holds a greater degree of offense, and the two are not equal.
