Our master and Mawlana Sharaf al-Din Jafar, son of our master Sulayman — may God raise their sanctity in the chambers of the Garden — set down in his book الْحَدَائِقُ الْأَنِيقَةُ النَّفْسَانِيَّةُ الْمُشْتَمِلَةُ عَلَى الْجَوَابَاتِ الْبُرْهَانِيَّةِ, drawing from the answers of our master Daud ibn 'Ajab to his son in religion, Sulayman ibn Hasan, the following of his chosen words: as for your question about one who owes a make-up of the witr — is it permissible for him to pray voluntary prayers or not — and about one who owes a make-up of the sunnah prayers: it is permissible, though it is better that he give precedence to the witr make-up before everything else.

I turn now to the sunnah and the voluntary prayers, again from that book of questions and answers.

Question: What do you say concerning the prayer of opening, the prayer of glorification, and the prayer of night vigil? Has anything fixed come down regarding their recitation, and what ought to be recited in them of the surahs?

Answer: Nothing fixed has come down. They are like the sunnah and voluntary prayers: one recites in them whatever one wishes.

Question: What do you say concerning the witr prayer and the prayer of the two feasts? Is it permissible for one who has not memorized the appointed portion of the Quran to recite something else in them?

Answer: If he has not memorized it, he recites what is easy for him and does not abandon the prayer.

Question: What do you say concerning the supplications of the night vigil? Is it permissible for one who has not memorized them to intend a voluntary prayer?

Answer: He intends the prayer of the night vigil and supplicates with the supplication if he has memorized it; otherwise he supplicates with what is easy for him, while still intending the prayer of the night vigil. It is twelve bowings, and it has two times: one is between the sunset prayer and the evening prayer, in which a person prays using the short surahs of the detailed portion.

Question: What do you say concerning the tarawih prayer, when the believers pray behind the imam for the sake of protective concealment with the intention of a voluntary prayer, while the imam recites the Quran from its beginning to its end — a practice not followed in the obligatory prayers, and particularly not in the witr, the sunnah of dawn, and the greeting of the mosque?

Answer: There is no harm in it for the sake of protective concealment, but they do not follow him as a leader.

Question: It is mentioned in كِتَابُ الشَّوَاهِدِ وَالْبَيَانِ that the worshipper prays the noon prayer as ten bowings, the afternoon prayer as eight bowings, the sunset prayer as five bowings, and the evening prayer as ten bowings.

Answer: The reliance is upon الدعائم, which is the gathering place of the questions. If a person leaves anything of the sunnah for the sake of protective concealment, he makes it up.

Question: It has come down concerning the prayer of glorification that when he rises from the second prostration he glorifies ten times. O our master, how does he do this — seated upon his feet, or standing?

Answer: When one who prays the prayer of glorification wishes to do this, he sits poised and ready after raising his head from the second prostration, glorifies ten times, then rises for the second bowing. Likewise, after the second bowing, he sits poised and ready after the second prostration, glorifies ten times, then sits settled, makes the testimony, and gives the salutation. He does the same in the last two bowings. This practice, however, is irregular. As for that upon which practice rests in the prayer of glorification: he sits settled after raising his head from the second prostration in each bowing and glorifies ten times, then rises for the next — or, after the second, makes the testimony following the glorification, remaining settled in his sitting during both the glorification and the testimony.

Question: It has come down concerning the prayer of sufficiency that in the first bowing one recites the opening of the Book, the chapter of sincerity, the chapter of the disbelievers, and the verse of the Throne — once each; in the second, twice each; in the third, three times each; and in the fourth, four times each. O our master, does he repeat the opening of the Book in each bowing, or is it recited once in each bowing? Kindly give the answer.

Answer: He recites the opening of the Book once in each bowing, and God knows best.

It is said in مُخْتَصَرُ الْآثَارِ: whoever has not learned the Quran, let him recite what is easy of it. God, Exalted is He, said:

> فَٱقْرَءُوا۟ مَا تَيَسَّرَ مِنَ ٱلْقُرْآنِ

So recite what is easy of the Quran.

And whoever wishes may stand the whole night with a single surah, repeating it, or with part of a surah, or with two surahs or more than that.

It is said in تَأْوِيلُ الدَّعَائِمِ, concerning the voluntary prayer of the Messenger of God (ع) upon his mount while he was making his way toward Medina: he would gesture with his head in indication of the prostration, with the direction of prayer behind him.

It is said in the abridged discourse on jurisprudence — whose questions are from the most honorable Shaykh Hasan, son of the Mawla Adam al-Hindi, and whose answers are from the masters of Yemen, may God sanctify all their spirits — that whoever prays between the obligation of the later evening prayer and its voluntary prayer whatever he wishes, then prays the voluntary prayer, does not intend it as a make-up, for it is called the prayer of the night.

Our master and Mawlana Jafar, son of our master Sulayman, may God raise their sanctity in the Gardens, wrote in like manner to his servant, the compiler of this book, from the island of Yemen to the island of Hind, concerning the matter of the prayer of Umm Daud, which is known among all who are of the ranks. He had asked him about it so that the sources and the paths might be made clear by his sufficient answer. He mentioned what concerns the prayer of Umm Daud and the supplication of opening, and that Qasim ibn Fir — the summoner of the maimed and blinded Daudi faction — commanded his companions to pray it, to fast it, and to supplicate with it in the month of Rabi al-Awwal of the year 1042, when the fifteenth of it fell on a Friday and they were in prison, and likewise in the honored month of Ramadan of that year. You asked about that, and about its permissibility and its practice, and I answered the one who asked you about it that Mawlana al-Sadiq (ع) gave no text on the aforementioned prayer of Umm Daud except for the month of Rajab, and he commanded her to purify herself. And when I asked him about it, and whether that supplication may be recited in a month other than Rajab, he said:

> فِي يَوْمِ عَرَفَةَ وَإِذَا كَانَ يَوْمَ جُمُعَةٍ لَمْ يَفْرُغْ صَاحِبُهُ اِلَّا وَقَدْ غُفِرَ لَهُ

On the day of 'Arafah; and if it falls on a Friday, the one who performs it does not finish until he has been forgiven.

The matter stands as I have stated it. Nothing outward exists without a sign by which it is recognized, and no practice is valid without a supporting text. In the command given to Umm Daud — to seek forgiveness until the month of Rajab, along with the supplication of opening already mentioned — there is both sufficiency and satisfaction. And in the text that follows, specifying the day of 'Arafah when it is not a Friday, and when it is a Friday as he mentioned, there is likewise sufficiency and satisfaction: the practice applies only to that specified day. As for any occasion other than these, no one should proceed without a text. If a text exists in the matter, let it be followed; and if not, then little practice, as the Prophet (ع) said:

> عَمَلٌ قَلِيلٌ فِي سُنَّةٍ خَيْرٌ مِنْ عَمَلٍ كَثِيرٍ فِي بِدْعَةٍ

You asked whether, on the day of 'Arafah, one should supplicate with the mentioned supplication or perform the mentioned prayer on that day. The root of the matter is that the mentioned supplication has as its condition the mentioned prayer, for he said:

> فَأَيْنَ أَنْتَ مِنْ دُعَاءِ الْاِسْتِفْتَاحِ

Where are you with regard to the supplication of opening?

When I asked him about it, he made it known to her by that mentioned prayer, then by the mentioned supplication. So if you pray on that day, its prayer is upon that; and if one supplicates with it on that day, it is accepted for its excellence and its nobility. God knows best and judges best; from His friends we learn in order to know, and God grants success to what is right and guides to the best of means.

In the questions from some of the shaykhs of Hind (رض), with answers from some of our masters — the summoners of Yemen, may God raise their sanctity — it is said:

Question: Most of the believers do not keep the sunnah fasts as they pray the sunnah prayers.

Answer: It is permissible for them to omit the sunnah fast, and no make-up is required of them. As for the sunnah prayer, whoever has missed it ought to make it up. The Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) and their followers did so, and they have reward from God if they fast.

Our master and Mawlana Hibat Allah, son of our master Ibrahim, said in his answer to questions from his servant and the humblest of his slaves, Salih ibn Ahmad — answering him from the island of Yemen to the island of Hind in the year 1135: You asked concerning what has come in the books, that the prophets — such as Jesus and others — and the Imams, such as Mawlana 'Ali and Mawlana Zayn al-Abidin and others among the Imams, the blessings of God be upon them all, at the time of a great event would call for water, perform the ablution, and pray two bowings, and in some instances four bowings, for the relief of distress. It has not been made clear at what time they prayed. Is that prayer to be prayed at all times of trial, affliction, and calamity, or only at a time when the sunnah and voluntary prayers are permitted? May the master and owner graciously give the answer.

Answer: By the help of God, Exalted is He, and the favor of His friend upon His earth (ع), and by the instruction of my master Badr al-Din, the inspired and the helper: the prayer of the prophets and the prayer of the Imams, the blessings and peace and benedictions of God be upon them all, flows down to us from them at times of grave matters and events, and at the descent of calamities and disasters. Their prayer is praise of God and thanksgiving for what He disclosed to them of the secret of affairs, and for their knowledge that those things do not come to pass except by a measure decreed. You mentioned that they, the blessings of God be upon them, did not inform us at what times it is to be prayed, but only reported it in a summary way. Yes, it is permissible to pray it at all the times when the sunnah and voluntary prayers are permitted; prohibition has come only concerning the forbidden times, which are not hidden from one of clear mind and illumined form. When their servants and their followers wish to pray that mentioned prayer, and by it to repel all calamities and to be shielded by it from all evils and ruinous things, it is for them to be guided by their guidance and to follow their practice. May God, Mighty and Majestic, keep us firm upon obedience to them, grant us their accepted intercession, and supply us from their secret and their blessings. He is the Guardian of that and the One able to do it, by the right that is His and the secret that is with Him.

I turn now to the prostration of the Quran, and again from that book of questions and answers.

Question: What do you say concerning one who recites a supplication in which there is a verse of prostration? Is it obligatory upon him to prostrate or not?

Answer: If it is among the binding ones, he must prostrate.

Question: What do you say concerning the teacher of boys, when he is teaching them the Quran and passes over the places of prostration while instructing them? Does he prostrate every time he passes over them, and do those who hear him prostrate as well, or not?

Answer: He has a choice in that: if he wishes, he prostrates, and if he wishes, he leaves it.

Another case: a man says in the prostration of the Quran what he says in the prostration of prayer.

It is said in مُخْتَصَرُ الْمُصَنَّفِ: when the reciter recites one of the binding prostrations, whoever is present prostrates. If that same prostration is recited again, none of them is required to prostrate a second time so long as he remains in that place. As for the remaining prostrations, whoever wishes to prostrate may prostrate, and whoever wishes may leave it; though prostrating is better.

Mawlana, the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his four hundred points of conduct:

> أَطِيلُوا السُّجُودَ فَمَا مِنْ عَمَلٍ أَشَدُّ عَلَى إِبْلِيسَ مِنْ أَنْ يَرَى ابْنَ آدَمَ سَاجِدًا فَأَنَّهُ أُمِرَ بِالسُّجُودِ فَعَصَى وَأُمِرَ ابْنُ آدَمَ بِالسُّجُودِ فَأَطَاعَ وَنَجَا

Prolong the prostration, for nothing bears more heavily on Iblis than to see the son of Adam prostrating. He was himself commanded to prostrate and disobeyed, while the son of Adam was commanded to prostrate, obeyed, and was saved.

## 25. The Sickbed and the Sound of Mourning

I turn now to the book of funerals — to its teachings on the sick person, the visits made to him, and the state of dying itself. In the book of the interpretation of the pillars, I find that the dying state is the moment when death draws near and the passage from this world to the hereafter is at hand.

In the book of questions and answers — whose questions were put by the eminent Shaykh Yusuf ibn Ismail al-Samiti al-Hindi, and whose answers were given by our master Idris ibn Hasan, may Allah sanctify him — I read the following exchange.

Question: What do you say concerning people who move from one place to another out of fear of epidemic and plague, believing that it will not reach them? Is that permitted or not?

Answer:

> يَتَوَجَّهُونَ حَيْثُ يَشَاءُونَ وَالْأَمْرُ بِيَدِ اللَّهِ تَعَالَى

They may go wherever they wish. The matter is in the hand of Allah, exalted be He.

I turn next to what our master and mawlana Jafar ibn Sayyidina Sulayman — may Allah sanctify him and grant us his intercession and his nearness — set down in his noble epistle, which he named The Sharpest Sword in the Nullifying of Innovations. He wrote it to counsel his people and to rouse those among them who had grown heedless and slept too long — among the people of the island of Yemen — after he had settled and dwelt among them. In one section of it, he says that it has been reported of some of you that you are slow in visiting the sick, though the reward for it has its established place in the noble book of the pillars of Islam, where it is recorded on the authority of your mawla the Commander of the Faithful (ع) that he heard the Messenger of Allah say:

> مَا مِنْ عَبْدٍ مُسْلِمٍ يَعُودُ مَرِيضًا إِلَّا صَلَّى عَلَيْهِ سَبْعُونَ أَلْفِ مَلَكٍ مِنْ سَاعَتِهِ الَّتِي يَعُودُ فِيهَا إِنْ كَانَ نَهَارًا حَتَّى تَغْرُبَ الشَّمْسُ أَوْ لَيْلًا حَتَّى تَطْلُعَ

I also find recorded there, from the Commander of the Faithful (ع), that he said:

> نَهَى رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ أَنْ يَأْكُلَ الْعَائِدُ عِنْدَ الْعَلِيلِ فَيُحْبِطَ اللَّهُ عِيَادَتَهُ

The Messenger of Allah forbade the visitor from eating in the presence of the sick person, for when that happens, Allah renders his visit worthless.

In another narration, the wording is that Allah renders his reward worthless — and indeed casts it behind him. The reason is this: when the visitor eats and drinks in a sick man's house, he presses upon someone already pressed by illness, and harms the household as well. The sick man may be of slender means, so that he and his family are inwardly galled by it. Whoever knows this must pass it on to those who do not, for not all people are learned, and the learned among them are few and select. The one who gives the warning has discharged his duty. The truth has come and falsehood has perished.

Our mawlana the Commander of the Faithful 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred points of conduct:

> ولا ينامنّ أحدكم على الحجة ولا على وجهه فمن رأيتموه نام على وجهه فانتبهوه ولا تنام المرأة مستلقية على ظهرها

Let none of أحدكم sleep in that posture, nor upon his face. Whomever you see sleeping upon his face, wake him. And let المرأة not sleep stretched out upon her back.

And he said in it:

> أسأل الله العافية من جهد البلاء فان جهد البلاء ذهاب للدين

I ask Allah for well-being and refuge from severe affliction, for the gravest affliction of all is the loss of one's religion.

I turn now to the command to remember death and to what accompanies it. In this same book of questions and answers — arranged by its compiler following the chapter order of the book of the pillars of Islam — I read the following.

Question: What is your view on the lifespans of people? Are they fixed with Allah, glorified be He, admitting neither increase nor decrease? And if that is so, what is the meaning of what the books record — that a lifespan may be both increased and decreased?

Answer:

> أَنَّهَا أَعْمَارٌ مُؤَجَّلَةٌ لَا تَزِيدُ وَلَا تَنْقُصُ وَأَمَّا مَعْنَى الزِّيَادَةِ وَالنُّقْصَانِ فَهُوَ صَحِيحٌ بِمَعْنَى أَنَّ الَّذِي يَنْقُصُ فِي الدَّوْرِ الْمَاضِي يَزِيدُ فِي الْمُسْتَقْبَلِ فِيهِ

Question: What do you say concerning his saying (ع):

> الشَّقِيُّ كُلُّ الشَّقِيِّ مَنْ أَدْرَكَتْهُ السَّاعَةُ حَيًّا لَمْ يَمُتْ

The wretched — wholly wretched — is the one whom the Hour overtakes while he is alive and has not died.

Is what is meant here that the most wretched of the wretched is the one whose death comes upon him while he still lives the sensory, animal life, not yet having passed into the angelic life? Or is something else intended?

Answer:

> إِنَّ قَصْدَهُ إِذَا أَتَتْهُ السَّاعَةُ وَهُوَ فِي حَالِ الْبَهِيْمِيَّةِ

What is intended is that the Hour overtakes him while he is still in the animal condition.

Among the questions that the eminent Shaykh Hasan ibn Nuh addressed to our master Husayn ibn Idris — may Allah sanctify his soul — along with his answers to them, the tenth question is this: a woman dies while pregnant, carrying in her womb a fully formed and living child; should the child be left in her womb, or should it be taken out?

Answer:

> إن حضرت المرأة امرأة تحسن إخراجه فهو الواجب إذا قدر أو في ستة أشهر فما فوقها ويرجى له الحياة وان لم فلا تقبر أمه حتى يتحقق موته والله أعلم

If there is present with the woman another woman skilled in delivering the child, that is what must be done, if it is possible, or if the child is of six months or more and life is hoped for it. If not, then the mother is not to be buried until his death is confirmed. And Allah knows best.

Our mawlana the Commander of the Faithful 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his four hundred points of conduct:

> تَمَسَّكُوا بِمَا أَمَرَكُمُ اللَّهُ تَعَالَى فَمَا بَيْنَ أَحَدِكُمْ وَبَيْنَ أَنْ يَغْتَبِطَ وَيَرَى مَا يُحِبُّ إِلَّا أَنْ يَحْضُرَهُ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ وَمَا عِنْدَ اللَّهِ خَيْرٌ وَأَبْقَى وَتَأْتِيهِ الْبِشَارَةُ وَاللَّهِ لَتَقَرَّنَّ عَيْنُهُ وَلَيَحِينَ لِقَاءُ اللَّهِ

Hold fast to what Allah, exalted be He, has commanded you. There is nothing between one of you and his rejoicing and seeing what he loves except that the Messenger of Allah should be present with him, and what is with Allah is better and more lasting, and the glad tidings come to him. By Allah, his eye shall be gladdened, and the meeting with Allah shall surely draw near.

And he said:

> أَكْثِرُوا ذِكْرَ الْمَوْتِ يَوْمَ خُرُوجِكُمْ مِنْ عِنْدِ الْقُبُورِ وَيَوْمَ قِيَامِكُمْ بَيْنَ يَدَيِ اللَّهِ تَهْنْ عَلَيْكُمُ الْمَصَايِبُ

Remember death often, and the day of your coming forth from the graves, and the day of your standing before Allah, and calamities will sit lightly upon you.

And he said in it:

> اَلْحُمَّى رَايِدُ الْمَوْتِ وَسِجْنُ اللهِ فِي الْأَرْضِ يَحْبِسُ بِهِ مَنْ يَشَاءُ مِنْ عِبَادِهِ وَهُوَ يَحُتُّ الذُّنُوبَ كَمَا يَحُتُّ الْوَبَرُ مِنْ مَسَامِّ الْبَعِيرِ

Fever is the forerunner of death — Allah's prison on earth, through which He confines whomever He wills among His servants. It strips away sins as hair is stripped from the pores of the camel.

And he said in it:

> مَا مِنْ شِيعَتِنَا أَحَدٌ يُفَارِقُ أَمْرًا نَهَيْنَا عَنْهُ فَيَمُوتُ حَتَّىٰ يُبْتَلَىٰ بِبَلِيَّةٍ تُمَحِّصُ ذُنُوبَهُ أَمَّا فِي مَالِهِ أَوْ وَلَدِهِ أَوْ فِي نَفْسِهِ حَتَّىٰ يَلْقَى اللَّهَ بِمُحِبِّنَا وَمَا لَهُ مِنْ ذَنْبٍ وَأَنَّهُ لَا يَبْقَىٰ عَلَيْهِ شَيْءٌ مِنْ ذُنُوبِهِ فَيُشَدَّدُ عَلَيْهِ عِنْدَ مَوْتِهِ وَيُمَحَّصُ مِنْ ذُنُوبِهِ وَالْمَيِّتُ مِنْ شِيعَتِنَا صِدِّيقٌ شَهِيدٌ صَدَّقَ بِأَمْرِنَا وَأَحَبَّ فِينَا وَأَبْغَضَ فِينَا

I turn now to condolences and patience — to the weeping that is permitted and the wailing that is forbidden — and to everything that falls within these subjects.

The abridged compendium states that no hair is to be shaved, no lamp lit, and no nail cut on account of the dead. In the book of questions and answers already mentioned — arranged according to the chapters of the book of the pillars of Islam — I find the following:

**Question:** What do you say concerning some people who place weapons beside the dead when the body passes the night in the house with them, and others who hold that sitting beside the body is a binding obligation? How is that?

**Answer:**

> مِثْلُ هَذَا لَا مُعَوَّلَ عَلَيْهِ

There is no reliance to be placed upon anything of this kind.

Our master and mawlana Jafar ibn Sayyidina Sulayman, may Allah sanctify their souls in the chambers of the gardens, addressed this in his noble epistle known as *The Sharpest Sword in the Nullifying of Innovations*, through which he counseled his people and roused those among them who had grown heedless and slept too long — among the people of the island of Yemen, after he had settled and dwelt among them. In one section he writes that among these innovations is what has become known of some of you: the wailing over the dead, with all the innovations it carries. A company of women gathers from their various places to lament the dead through nights and days on end, as though it were a prescription from your Lord — glorified be He — appointed at fixed times. With it comes singing and all that follows: diversion and play that stretch past the limit within which they are permitted on the days of weddings and banquets, where their purpose is to distinguish fornication from lawful marriage — and stretch on into what is prohibited, forbidden, and altogether impermissible. Even if your adversaries who stand outside this noble creed have a full share of such things, you are not free of them either. They disgrace you before Allah, glorified be He, and before His friends (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), and they bring you — may Allah protect you — to destruction in your return to Him. And you are the cream of this whole world and its kernel. What then is the matter with you, that you do not restrain yourselves from this fault, nor guard yourselves against falling into the pit? May Allah shield you from it from this hour onward, and from returning to its like, if you are believers.

The one among you who reads has read in the noble book of the pillars of Islam the words of its author — may Allah sanctify his soul — in the narration whose soundness he intended and sought, from Jafar ibn Muhammad, the blessings of Allah be upon him, who made this bequest when death came upon him:

> لَا يُلْطَمَنَّ عَلَيَّ خَدٌّ وَلَا يُشَقَّنَّ عَلَيَّ جَيْبٌ فَمَا مِنِ امْرَأَةٍ تَشُقُّ جَيْبَهَا إِلَّا لَهَا فِي جَهَنَّمَ صَدْعٌ كُلَّمَا زَادَتْ زِيدَتْ
>
> "Let no cheek be struck for me, and let no collar be torn for me. There is no woman who tears her collar but that she has a rent in Hell, and whenever she adds to it, it is added for her."

He also records in it, from 'Ali, the blessings of Allah be upon him, that he wrote to Rifaah ibn Shaddad, his judge over al-Ahwaz:

> وَإِيَّاكَ وَالنَّوْحَ عَلَى الْمَيِّتِ بِبَلَدٍ يَكُونُ لَكَ بِهِ سُلْطَانٌ
>
> "Beware of wailing over the dead in a land where you hold authority."

I also record in it, in plain terms, from the Messenger of Allah, that he said:

> صَوْتَانِ مَلْعُونَانِ يُبْغِضُهُمَا اللَّهُ اِعْوَالٌ عِنْدَ مُصِيبَةٍ وَصَوْتٌ عِنْدَ نِعْمَةٍ

Two voices are accursed, hated by Allah: a howling at a calamity and a voice raised at a blessing.

By this I mean wailing and singing. Whoever wails for his dead calls down upon himself the very causes of affliction by which the soul is punished — as the Prophet has explained. This is precisely what the noble book I have already named recounts in the account of 'Abdullah ibn Rawahah. The Prophet visited him while he lay ill, finding him weighed down by pain and fallen unconscious, aware of nothing, while the women shrieked around him. The Messenger of Allah called to him three times and received no answer, so he prayed for him with the prayer he made to his Lord — glorified be He — and turned away from him. Afterward, Abdullah felt a lightness in himself and emerged from his unconsciousness; the Prophet was informed of it. He returned, stood over him, and said:

> يَا عَبْدَ اللَّهِ حَدِّثْ بِمَا رَأَيْتَ فَاِنَّكَ رَأَيْتَ عَجَبًا
>
> "O 'Abdullah, tell what you saw, for you have seen something wondrous."

He said:

> يَا رَسُولَ اللهِ رَأَيْتُ مَلَكًا مِنَ الْمَلَائِكَةِ بِيَدِهِ مِقْمَعَةٌ مِنْ حَدِيدٍ تَأَجَّجُ نَارًا وَكُلَّمَا صَرَخَتْ صَارِخَةٌ يَا جَبَلَاه أَهْوَى بِهَا لِهَامَتِي وَقَالَ أَنْتَ جَبَلُهَا فَأَقُولُ لَا بَلِ اللهُ فَيَكُفُّ بَعْدَ أَهْوَائِهَا فَإِذَا صَرَخَتْ صَارِخَةٌ يَا عِزَّاه أَهْوَى بِهَا لِهَامَتِي وَقَالَ أَنْتَ عِزُّهَا فَأَقُولُ لَا بَلِ اللهُ فَيَكُفُّ بَعْدَ أَهْوَائِهَا

The Messenger of Allah said:

> صَدَقَ عَبْدُ اللَّهِ فَمَا بَالُ مَوْتَاكُمْ يُبْتَلَوْنَ بِقَوْلِ أَحْيَائِكُمْ

'Abdullah has spoken the truth. What is the matter with your dead, that they should be made to suffer by the words of your living?

Is there not, in this — and in less than this — enough for your living? The learned among you has come to know it; its signs and landmarks have been made plain before him. And yet he engages in a forbidden matter, walks a path that earns displeasure, and leaves his own ground unguarded. As for whoever conceals it from the one in authority over him and keeps it within his house among his household — he has broken the covenant of Allah that he took upon himself and retreated from his knowledge back into his ignorance.

The noble book I have already named makes clear what is permitted in this matter and what is not, through its noble sayings — sayings that tell the one who conforms to them that he has attained. From 'Ali, the blessings of Allah be upon him, that he said:

> بَكَىٰ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّىٰ اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ عِنْدَ مَوْتِ بَعْضِ وَلَدِهِ فَقِيلَ لَهُ يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ تَبْكِي وَتَنْهَانَا عَنِ الْبُكَاءِ فَقَالَ لَمْ أَنْهَكُمْ عَنِ الْبُكَاءِ وَاِنَّمَا نَهَيْتُكُمْ عَنِ النَّوْحِ وَالْعَوِيلِ وَاِنَّمَا هَٰذِهِ رِقَّةٌ وَرَحْمَةٌ يَجْعَلُهَا اللَّهُ تَبَارَكَ وَتَعَالَىٰ فِي قَلْبِ مَنْ يَشَاءُ مِنْ خَلْقِهِ وَيَرْحَمُ اللَّهُ مَنْ يَشَاءُ وَإِنَّمَا يَرْحَمُ اللَّهُ مِنْ عِبَادِهِ الرُّحَمَاءَ

The Messenger of Allah wept at the death of one of his children. Someone said to him, "O Messenger of Allah, you weep, yet you forbid us to weep?" He replied, "I did not forbid you from weeping. What I forbade was wailing and lamentation. This is nothing but tenderness and mercy — a mercy that Allah, blessed and exalted, places in the heart of whomever He wills among His creation. He shows mercy to whom He wills, and He shows mercy only to the merciful among His servants."

And from him (ع), he said:

> رَخَّصَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ فِي الْبُكَاءِ عِنْدَ الْمُصِيبَةِ وَقَالَ النَّفْسُ مُصَابَةٌ وَالْعَيْنُ دَامِعَةٌ وَالْعَهْدُ قَرِيبٌ وَقُولُوا مَا أَرْضَى اللَّهَ وَلَا تَقُولُوا الْهُجْرَ

Let this, then, be your practice — in what you keep hidden and in what you make open alike. As for the accursed voice at a blessing — namely, singing — the noble book I have already named has likewise made clear what is permitted of it at banquets and weddings, so that lawful marriage may be distinguished from fornication and the permitted from the forbidden. These are the words of its author, whose pen has already dried upon them — may Allah sanctify his soul — narrating from the Messenger of Allah that he once passed by the Banu Zurayq, heard music, and said, "What is this?" They said, "O Messenger of Allah, so-and-so has married." He said:

> كَمَّلَ دِينَهُ هَذَا النِّكَاحُ لَا السِّفَاحُ وَلَا يَكُونُ نِكَاحٌ فِي السِّرِّ حَتَّى يُرَى دُخَانٌ أَوْ يُسْمَعَ حِسُّ دَفٍّ

His religion is complete. This is lawful marriage, not fornication. There is no marriage in secret, until smoke is seen or the sound of a tambourine is heard.

And he said:

> اَلْفَرْقُ مَا بَيْنَ النِّكَاحِ وَالسِّفَاحِ ضَرْبُ الدُّفِّ

What sets lawful marriage apart from fornication is the beating of the tambourine.

This, then, is what the sincere believer must hold fast to in each of these matters. Whoever turns away from it toward something else will find only ruin in all his affairs.

> وَأَنَّ فِي ذَلِكَ لَذِكْرَى لِمَنْ كَانَ لَهُ قَلْبٌ أَوْ أَلْقَى السَّمْعَ وَهُوَ شَهِيدٌ وَمَنْ عَمِلَ صَالِحًا فَلِنَفْسِهِ وَمَنْ أَسَاءَ فَعَلَيْهَا وَمَا رَبُّكَ بِظَلَّامٍ لِلْعَبِيدِ

In that is a reminder for whoever has a heart, or gives ear while present in mind. Whoever does righteousness does so for himself, and whoever does evil, it is against himself — and your Lord is never unjust to the servants.

And our mawlana, the Commander of the Faithful, 'Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said:

> وَأْمُرُوا أَهَالِيَكُمْ بِالْقَوْلِ الْحَسَنِ عِنْدَ مَوْتَاكُمْ فَاِنَّ فَاطِمَةَ بِنْتَ مُحَمَّدٍ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهَا لَمَّا قُبِضَ أَبُوهَا صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ أَشْعَرَهَا بَنَاتُ هَاشِمٍ فَقَالَتْ أَكْثِرُوا التِّحْدَادَ وَعَلَيْكُمْ بِالدُّعَاءِ

Command your households to speak well over your dead. When Fatimah, the daughter of Muhammad, lost her father, the daughters of Hashim mourned alongside her, and she said, "Grieve with restraint, and hold fast to supplication."

He also said in it:

> مَنْ ضَرَبَ عَلَىٰ فَخِذِهِ عِنْدَ الْمُصِيبَةِ فَقَدْ حَبِطَ أَجْرُهُ

Whoever strikes his thigh at a calamity forfeits his reward.

## 26. Washing, Shrouding, Carrying, Burying

I turn now to the washing of the dead and to what belongs with it.

In the book of questions and answers arranged according to the order of the chapters of *The Pillars of Islam*, the following was asked. Question: what do you say concerning a man who washes the dead? Must he perform the ablution upon them three times together with three washings, or not? Answer: yes, he performs the ablution upon them three times together with three washings; and if he performs their ablution at the last of the washings, that suffices for them, as comes in the Book of Reports. From another place: if a man is wounded and is not moved from his place, and lives an hour, or two hours, or a day, and speaks, he is likewise not washed, because he was not lifted from his place.

Question: concerning one who dies while traveling and no water is to be found; or a man who dies among women with no woman of his own; or a woman who dies with no husband and no woman to be found who may wash her — must the dead in such cases be given tayammum with earth, or not? Answer: it is mentioned that if a man in a state of janabah and a dead person are together, and there is not enough water except for one of them, the man in janabah performs the washing and the dead is given tayammum; and these cases are like that. From another place: if a man dies, the washer washes the dead even while in janabah; and if washing would harm him, he performs tayammum and then washes the dead, and this is permitted when he finds no one else. And if the man in janabah is the more excellent of them, his washing of the dead is permitted.

It is said in the Mukhtasar of al-Musannif that the nearest of those present to the dead has the best right to wash him. From the same book: if the martyr was in janabah, he is washed on account of the janabah. And if a man is found dead with no mark upon him, he is washed, treated with hanut, and shrouded.

It is said in the Mukhtasar al-Athar that the drowned and the burned are washed, and so is a limb — when there is found of a man such a part that it is known he could not have lived once it was parted from him. Whatever falls from the dead is washed and placed with him. If his limbs, or any of them, have come apart, they are washed and each limb is set in its own place, and he is shrouded in that condition and buried. It is said in the Mukhtasar of al-Musannif that no hair is to be shaved from the dead and no nail is to be trimmed.

Our master and lord Jafar, son of our master Sulayman, answered questions put to him by his servant, the author of this book, replying from the island of Yemen to the island of Hind. Among what I asked, I said: what do you say concerning a woman who dies? Is she wrapped in an إزار (izar) and washed as a man is wrapped, or is the end of her wrapper drawn behind her over her private parts and between her thighs until the other end comes round behind her, in the fashion used by the unbelievers and by others among the Muslims? For the Daudi group uses this and denounces whoever does not use it. Is that to be used when she is washed, or not? And is her wrapper to be so arranged in her shrouding, or not? For the Daudi group for the most part uses it in shrouding. They likewise use a ستارة (sitarah) when the dead is washed, screening the body from the women. Is nothing else permitted, or is there no harm in her being washed without a screen over her when she is at a distance from men?

The answer to that: nothing is mentioned in the book of the Pillars except that an إِزَار is placed upon the dead when he is washed, reaching from the navel to the knees, and the water is passed beneath it, and the washer wraps a cloth upon his hand and brings it in under the إِزَار and washes the private parts and the rest of the 'awrah that lies beneath the إِزَار. Practice rests upon that, and the manner you have described prevents its being done. The shrouding is likewise, and concerning it there has come in the book *The Pillars of Islam*, from Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع), that he said:

> نِعْمَ الْكَفَنُ ثَلَاثَةُ أَثْوَابٍ قَمِيصٌ غَيْرُ مَزْرُورٍ وَلَا مَكْفُوفٍ وَلِفَافَةٌ وَاِزَارٌ

The best shroud is three garments: a shirt neither buttoned nor hemmed, a wrapping sheet, and a waist wrapper. Know that. As for the matter of the screen in the washing of the dead — and above all of a woman — the better course is that it be done only within a covering.

And of what I asked, I said: what do you say concerning the meaning of the words of our master al-Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad in the Book of Purity, at the end of it, in the mention of the washing of the dead, where he says that the woman is bound up and is given the 'ajrah? What is the meaning of his word وتعجر? The answer to that: the عجرة (جُرْح) known in the lands of the Arabs, spelled with the undotted راء (ra'), is the gathering of the shirt, drawing together that part of it which faces the navel down to below the navel over the clothing, and tucking it into the fastening of the إِزَار. That is a mark of difference between men and women, like the head covering which is a mark of difference between men and women. Know that.

Our lord the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Counsels:

> مَنْ مَسَّ جَسَدَ الْمَيِّتِ بَعْدَ مَا بَرَدَ فَعَلَيْهِ الْغُسْلُ

Whoever touches the body of الميت after it has grown cold — برد جسد — must perform the washing.

And he said in it:

> مَنْ غَسَّلَ مَيِّتًا فَلْيَغْتَسِلْ بَعْدَ مَا يُلْبِسُهُ الْكَفَنَ وَلَا يَمَسَّهُ بَعْدَ ذَلِكَ فَيَجِبُ عَلَيْهِ الْغُسْلُ

In the book of questions and answers already mentioned, I find this saying explained. Question: what do you say concerning the meaning of the words of the Commander of the Faithful (ع) — that whoever touches the body of the dead after it grows cold is bound to perform the washing? What is the meaning of "after it grows cold"? Answer: it means after his end has come upon him and he has been washed according to the sunnah by whoever washes him, because the body of the dead has become impure; when its moisture reaches a living man it renders him impure, and that is the meaning of the washing being binding upon him.

In the book of questions sent by certain dignitaries of Hind, answered by certain of our lords the summoners in the Yemeni peninsula, I find it written: you asked about a woman who gave birth to two children joined to one another — what is to be done with them, and if one of them dies, what is to be done with him. God, mighty and exalted, creates what He wills. If their adhesion is such that separation is possible, what lies between them is cut without harm to them and without danger, when the matter is slight; but if it is grave and dangerous, they are left as they are. In that case their death is one death and their life is one life, and God knows best, and He does what He wills. If one of them dies and not the other, whatever adheres to the living one is cut from the dead one, as long as the living one feels no sensation or pain from the cutting, if that is possible; the dead one is buried and the living one is left. But if pain is felt in the body of the other, then their life is but one life and their death but one death, and God knows best.

As for the hanut and the shroud, I find it asked in the same book: what do you say concerning what comes in the Book of Reports — that the first thing taken from the estate of the dead is the shroud and his outfitting, then the debt, then the bequest? O our master, if the debt is greater than his estate, is his outfitting to be taken from it according to his station, even if it is costly? Answer: his shroud and his outfitting must be taken out according to his station.

From another place: the hanut is placed upon the places of prostration of the dead, and upon the private parts and the feet, and between the thumb and the forefinger, and upon the navel, the armpits, the breast, the mouth, the eyes, the nose, the forehead, the ears, and the hands at the palms, and the knees. A little oil, wheat, and perfume are put into the water on the third night, and the water is poured upon the grave on the third day. If the dead man is of the people of rank, the Quran is recited over his grave for seven days, and over the graves of others for three days; such has been the practice of our lords, may God lengthen their lives. It has come from our master Hasan ibn Idris that the Quran is recited for the people of rank seven times and for others three times, whether in the mosque, at the graves, in the house, or elsewhere.

In the Mukhtasar al-Athar, I find that the hanut is whatever is of perfume, but neither wars nor saffron is put into the hanut of men, while these are put into the hanut of women. In the Book of Purity, I find that the hanut and the camphor are placed upon the forehead and the nose, the insides of the palms, the knees, and the tops of the feet — these being the places of prostration. From the same book: cotton is placed upon the face, under the armpits, and between the knees, the shins, and the feet, if it is to be had.

In the Tawil al-Daaim, I find that the sunnah concerning the incensing of the dead is this: the dead man himself is not incensed, nor is his bier followed with incense; rather his shroud is incensed, and the place in which he is washed and shrouded — and nothing beyond that. And our lord the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Counsels:

> وَلَا تُجَمِّرُوا مَوْتَاكُمْ وَلَا تَمَسُّوهُمْ بِالطِّيبِ اِلَّا بِالْكَافُورِ فَاِنَّ الْمَيِّتَ بِمَنْزِلَةِ الْمُحْرِمِ

Do not perfume your dead with incense, and do not touch them with any fragrance except camphor, for the dead man is in the position of one consecrated for pilgrimage.

In a book of legal questions, all of them remarkable, I find another question: what do you say concerning a man who died, whose head and face were not covered and to whom nothing of the hanut was brought near when he died and was shrouded? Answer: that man died as a pilgrim in a state of consecration, so his head and his face are not covered, and nothing of perfume is brought near him, because he is consecrated during days that are numbered.

As for walking with the bier, I find it asked in the same book: what do you say concerning what comes in the law commanding that one walk behind the bier? O our master, does a man sin if he walks before it, or not? Answer: it is mentioned in the Book of Reports that walking is disliked only before the bier of an enemy of the family, because of the angels of wrath that come to it. As for the believers, it matters not where you walk in relation to the bier, since the angels of mercy encompass it. Yet walking behind it is the more excellent. I find it stated in the Book of Reports that they disliked the call to prayer at the bier, and permitted the informing of people of the death without a public call.

In the Tawil al-Daaim, I find that what is required is this: no one should rise for the bier when it passes except one who intends to follow it, and he should not sit down until it has been set at the edge of the grave. From the Mukhtasar al-Athar: when the dead is brought to the burial ground, the bier is set down crosswise, the dead lying upon his back upon it, so that his right side is toward the qibla and his left toward the one who prays.

From the Tawil al-Daaim, and of that is what is related of the Messenger of God (ع):

> نَظَرَ إِلَى النِّسَاءِ يَتْبَعْنَ جَنَازَةً فَوَقَفَ وَقَالَ لَهُنَّ تُصَلِّينَ عَلَيْهَا فِي مَنْ يُصَلِّي قُلْنَ لَا قَالَ فَتَحْمِلْنَهَا فِي مَنْ يَحْمِلُهَا قُلْنَ لَا قَالَ فَتَنْزِلْنَ لَهَا فِي الْقَبْرِ فِي مَنْ يَنْزِلُهَا قُلْنَ لَا قَالَ فَتُوَارِينَهَا فِي مَنْ يُوَارِيهَا قُلْنَ لَا قَالَ فَارْجِعْنَ مَعْذُورَاتٍ غَيْرَ مَأْجُورَاتٍ

The *Mukhtasar al-Athar* records that we have transmitted from the Imams of the household of the Messenger of God (ع) that they commanded the following: once the deceased has been shrouded, he is placed upon his bier and laid upon it, when a bier is to be had and this is possible; men carry him on their shoulders, when men can be found. If that is not possible, he is carried on a beast or by whatever other means are available. His shroud is covered from above with a cloth, if one is to be had. A canopy is erected over the bier and it is screened with a cloth; and the deceased is secured if there is fear he may fall from the bier.

On the prayer over the dead, the same book was asked: What do you say concerning a man who dies in a place where no authorized man is present, though an authorized woman is? May she lead the women in the funeral prayer, or should the men pray over him individually and he then be buried? Answer: The prayer of a man is more in keeping with the teaching of the People of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ).

Question: What of the prayer over the dead — is it permitted to pray over him after he has been carried from the place where he was prepared, or not until he reaches the burial ground? Answer: He may be prayed over wherever one wishes: in the burial ground, in his house, or anywhere else. Whoever wishes may recite the supplication between the takbirs of the funeral prayer; whoever wishes may leave it and be content with the imam. The funeral prayer is also permitted during the disliked hours and at the time of an eclipse.

The *Majalis al-Bayan* states that the funeral prayer is a communal obligation upon every Muslim who knows of it, until one of them has performed it, after which the rest participate as volunteers. The Reports record, on the authority of transmitters from the People of the House (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), that it is recommended to pray over the bier within the appointed prayer times, provided there is no risk of missing the prescribed prayer; if that risk exists, one begins with the prescribed prayer.

The *Tawil al-Daaim* likewise states: when the imam of the age is absent, whoever has been appointed judge by him, or given charge by him of some affair of the Muslims, has the better right to the prayer over the dead. If a company of those brought near by the imam's command is present, or of those whom the imam has advanced, the right belongs to the highest among them in station; if only one such person is present, he has a better right to the prayer than the deceased's guardian, being someone the imam has set up, or set up for some affair of the Muslims. Whoever is appointed to lead the people in prayer, if he is present at the bier, has the better right to pray over it. If none of these persons is present, then the most entitled of the guardians has the best right to pray over it.

The *Mukhtasar al-Athar* further states that the funeral prayer is offered over the martyr, over those who have committed grave sins and offenses, and over one killed in execution of a prescribed punishment or in retaliation. The *Mukhtasar* of al-Musannif adds that the supplication is recited quietly during a daytime funeral prayer and aloud during a nighttime one, and that the prayer is offered over one who has taken his own life. From the same book: the deceased's knots are loosened, his right hand is brought out from beneath his side, and his cheek is laid against the earth. If a woman dies carrying a child of six months or more in her womb, the child is taken out if that is possible.

Our master and lord Jafar ibn Sulayman — in his answers to questions put to him by his servant, the author of this book, replying from the island of Yemen to the island of Hind — answered what I asked when I said: What do you say concerning a bier at which one is present, when the imam has come forward to pray over it? Is his recitation of the supplication in the prayer sufficient, or ought the one who follows him to recite it himself as well, given that it is by day? The answer to that: it has come in the book of legal questions and answers that whoever wishes may recite the supplication between the takbirs of the funeral prayer, and whoever wishes may leave it and be content with the imam's recitation. Know that.

As for burial and the graves, it was asked in the same book: What do you say concerning what the law stipulates — that whatever falls from a man while he is alive is to be buried in the earth? O our master, may he bury it wherever he wishes, or only in the burial ground? And if he leaves it and does not bury it, does he sin in that or not? Answer: He buries it wherever he wishes, and there is no blame upon him if it is lost.

Question: What do you say concerning a man in whose land people have buried their dead without his leave, and who, when he came to know of it, wished to have them removed? Is that permitted to him or not? Answer: It is not permitted that burial be made in his land except with his leave; but since they did that without his consent, he has nothing but the value of the places of the graves, and it is not permitted to him to have them removed.

Question: What do you say concerning what the law commands — the burial of the dead where they fell and the prohibition of moving them? O our master, if the burial ground is far off, how is one to act? Answer: The deceased is buried in the place where he dies, in the burial ground of the people of that town, and he is not to be carried to another town. But if he dies in open wilderness, there is no harm in his being carried to the nearest burial ground.

Question: What do you say concerning a woman who dies when there is not to be found in the town any male relative who may lawfully look upon her? Who shall lower her into the grave? Answer: If a woman dies and no kinsman to whom looking upon her is lawful is present at her grave, then the person nearest to her undertakes the burial; and if such a one is lacking, upright men other than her kin bury her out of necessity.

Question: What do you say concerning what comes in the books of the rightly guiding summons — that mud bricks, wood, or whatever else one wishes are placed over the dead, and that if earth is heaped over him it is well? O our master, is it permitted to place over him baked brick, stone, and whatever else is to be found? For the people of the outward sense dislike baked brick. How does the matter stand — may God make us your ransom? Answer: It is permitted to place over him baked brick and whatever else is to be found. Rely upon this and pay no regard to anything else, save what may be done for prudent concealment.

And our lord the Commander of the Faithful, Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع), said in his Four Hundred Counsels:

> أَلِمُّوا بِالْقُبُورِ الَّتِي يَلْزَمُكُمْ حَقُّ سُكَّانِهَا وَزُورُوهَا وَاطْلُبُوا الرِّزْقَ عِنْدَهَا فَأِنَّهُمْ يَفْرَحُونَ بِزِيَارَتِكُمْ وَلْيَطْلُبِ الرَّجُلُ الْحَاجَةَ عِنْدَ قَبْرِ أَبِيهِ وَأُمِّهِ بَعْدَ مَا يَدْعُو لَهُمَا

Visit the graves of those who have a claim upon you. Go to them, and seek provision at their graves, for they rejoice at your visiting. Let a man ask for what he needs at the grave of his father and his mother, after he has prayed for them both.

## 27. The Wealth That Is Not Yours

I come now to the Book of Zakat and to what has been handed down concerning the merit of giving zakat and the matters bound up with it.

Our master Jafar ibn Mansur al-Yaman, may God sanctify him and grant us his intercession and his company, spoke in the book of inward nursing, citing the word of the Messenger of Allah:

> لا تحل الصدقة لي ولا لأهل بيتي

Charity is not lawful for me, nor for the people of my house.

And he said in it: As for one whose scope in the call, after what is incumbent upon him has been discharged, is that his excellence appear outwardly — in that he has abundance of wealth — he pays from his wealth what he wills, apart from the fitra, of the obligatory rights and of voluntary giving. And he said in it: Whoever pays the khums is thereby excused from paying the tithe, except as a voluntary act; but whoever pays the tithe alone is not thereby excused from paying the khums that is due from him, because the tithe does not encompass the khums as the khums encompasses the tithe. And the Prophet said:

> لَا صَلَاةَ لِمَنْ لَا يُؤَدِّي الزَّكَاةَ

There is no prayer for one who does not pay zakat.

Our master Zayd ibn Muhammad, may God sanctify him, said in the Hidden Epistle: God Almighty has imposed zakat upon ten things, and they are:

1. gold
2. silver
3. camels
4. cows
5. sheep
6. dates
7. raisins
8. wheat
9. barley
10. grains

And Mawlana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) said in his Four Hundred Counsels:

> دَاوُوا مَرْضَاكُمْ بِالصَّدَقَةِ وَحَصِّنُوا أَمْوَالَكُمْ بِالزَّكَاةِ

Treat your sick with charity, and fortify your wealth with zakat.

Our master and Mawlana, the author of the Mustansiriyya Councils, (ع), said in the twelfth council of them: You have heard what we recited to you in what came before, of the jurisprudence of guardianship and purity and prayer, by which the sincerity of the testimony is completed — that testimony which is the root of religion and worship, and the first of the causes of mercy and felicity. So listen now to what we recite to you concerning zakat, which is among its rights according to what has already been said. Its sincerity is the knowledge of its noble boundaries, and the fulfilling of its rights is the performance of the seven obligations that are the pillars of Islam, according to the order we have already set out. In zakat likewise there are seven obligations and twelve sunnahs, like the number of the clauses of the testimony and its letters. Zakat must be received by the Imam, and it is taken from seven things:

1. gold
2. silver
3. camels
4. cows
5. sheep
6. crops
7. minerals

And it is disbursed to eight who are named, who fall into seven divisions:

1. the poor and the needy, who are given charity from it
2. those who labor in its collection, who are given from it the wage of collection
3. those whose hearts are to be reconciled, who are paid from it as a bond of protection
4. and in the freeing of necks, by which those under bondage of service are set free from it
5. the debt-burdened, whose debts are discharged from it among the people of allegiance
6. and in the way of God, meaning the struggle that is an obligation of sufficiency
7. and the wayfarer, those cut off from the road of guidance

As for its twelve sunnahs:

1. that it not be taken until the year has run its course
2. that the nisab of gold is twenty dinars
3. that the nisab of silver is two hundred dirhams
4. that the nisab of camels is five grazing head
5. that the nisab of cows is thirty grazing head
6. that the nisab of sheep is forty head
8. that the zakat is taken from the middling of the livestock, which is what is below the best of them and above the poorest of them
9. that what is separate among them is not joined together, nor is what is joined together divided
10. that the stud animal is not taken from them
11. that the owner of crops gives charity from what has not reached the nisab, which is five awsaq, by the bundle and by the handful, at the time of his harvest, for the saying of God Almighty:

> ﴿وَآتُوا حَقَّهُ يَوْمَ حَصَادِهِ﴾

And give its due on the day of its harvest.

12. that there is no zakat upon dwellings, nor upon ornaments, nor upon anything that is for use.

Every word of this speech carries abundant meanings and traces of wisdom.

I set down next what has been said concerning the severity of the matter in the giving of zakat. It is stated in the book of questions and answers already mentioned, which one of its arrangers ordered according to the arrangement of the chapters of the book Daaim al-Islam.

Question: What do you say concerning what has come in the Sharia forbidding that a man conceal the zakat of his wealth from his imam? Our master, who is meant by it — the one who withholds zakat, or another? And how does a man conceal zakat from his imam if he is giving it to him openly? Answer: This report concerns one who brings out a part of the zakat. As for the one who withholds it, he has abandoned the obligation altogether.

Question: What do you say concerning what has come in the book of Daaim, that the Messenger of Allah said:

> إِنَّا أَهْلَ الْبَيْتِ لَا تَحِلُّ لَنَا الصَّدَقَةُ

We, the people of the House — charity is not lawful for us.

Our master, does this refer to the Imams, or to all his descendants through Fatima (ع)? Answer: It refers to the Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ).

Question: The book Daaim al-Islam states that charity is not lawful except for five, one of whom is a man who has undertaken a hamala. Our master, what does hamala mean here? Answer: It is either a penalty imposed by a tyrannical ruler, or the bearing of blood money on behalf of the 'aqila.

Question: From the book of Daaim, in the covenant that Amir al-Muminin wrote to Muhnaf ibn Sulaym: whoever belongs to the people of our obedience in the territory between the Jazira, Kufa, and the land of Syria, and claims to have paid his charity to the officials of Syria while residing within our domain — held back and encompassed by our horsemen and foot soldiers — that is not permitted to him. Even if the matter is as he claims, he may not dwell in our lands and pay the charity of his wealth to our enemy. What is the ruling here? And does the seizure of the land of India by a ruler through his horsemen and his men make the zakat due to him or not? Answer: The covenant that Amir al-Muminin (ع) wrote to Muhnaf ibn Sulaym — and his statement that whatever territory his horsemen and foot soldiers have not encompassed is not to be assessed for zakat — means that zakat is not demanded from one whom the enemy has seized who is not of the Shia who hold to the allegiance. For it has come down from the people of the House, God's blessings upon them, in the book of Daaim and elsewhere, that whoever hands over zakat to other than the one whom God — mighty and majestic — commanded it be handed to, it does not suffice him, even if it is later plundered or stolen, until he hands it to the guardian of the affair or to one who receives it by his command.

And it is said in the Mustansiriyya Councils: likewise, the one who upholds the outward form of the prayer and believes in its inner reality, yet does not spend from what God has provided him what He has made obligatory upon him — the zakat of his wealth, the khums of his earnings, the purchase of his own self, and the redemption of his pledge — falls short in merit of the one God described in this verse:

> ﴿وَمِمَّا رَزَقْنَاهُمْ يُنفِقُونَ﴾

And out of what We have provided them, they spend.

And the Prophet said that there is no prayer for one who does not pay zakat, and he said:

> مَانِعُ الزَّكَاةِ فِي النَّارِ

The one who withholds zakat is in the Fire.

## 28. Gold, Herds, and Harvest

I turn now to the zakat of gold, silver, and jewels.

### Mentioning Zakat on Gold, Silver, and Jewels

He said in the book of questions and answers, arranged according to the order of the chapters of the Pillars of Islam:

Question: What do you say about a man who died having appointed two guardians, and one of them — or another of those close to the deceased — feared that the second guardian would block payment of what was owed from the estate: the khums, zakat, Hajj, and the rest? He paid these obligations in secret, without informing the second guardian, out of fear of him. Is anything held against him for that? And what must be paid, given that the deceased fixed nothing and left the matter unspecified, while the state of the kinsfolk is as it is, and it was known of him that he had withheld zakat, had not paid the khums on all his wealth, and had not performed the Hajj?

Answer: If the guardian discharges what lies upon the deceased, there is nothing wrong with it. Zakat is a debt from which release must first be obtained, then the Hajj, then the khums. If the next of kin do not obtain that release, nothing falls upon anyone else, as long as they clear his neck — whether in secret or openly.

Question: What do you say about a man who does not bring out the zakat of his wealth and does not deliver it to the one appointed to receive it? Is it permissible to eat food in his house?

Answer: There is nothing wrong with that.

Question: What do you say about a man who possessed wealth, did not pay its khums, and then died? Must the khums be taken from his entire estate — including the houses, the servants, and the undivided holdings — or not?

Answer: Yes, the khums is taken from his entire estate.

Question: What do you say about a man who owned the nisab and did not pay its zakat, or who did not own the nisab until he died, and then when his estate was assessed — goods and houses together — it was found to have reached the nisab? Must zakat be taken from it? And likewise, what of the Hajj and the other obligations that rested upon his wealth?

Answer: If what he left behind includes trade goods held for selling, buying, and commerce, and those goods reach the nisab, then paying their zakat is obligatory, and the Hajj is discharged after the zakat.

Question: What do you say about a man who died, and when his entire estate was examined it proved sufficient to cover the Hajj and the other obligations — expiation, al-Shurwa, and khums — even though during his lifetime the deceased had owned little beyond his house and its furnishings? Are the Hajj and the other obligations to be discharged while the heirs take what remains?

Answer: If he leaves wealth, the Hajj takes precedence over the inheritance, because it is a debt, and debt comes before inheritance. The remaining obligatory duties are likewise binding if the wealth is sufficient for them and something is still left for the heir.

Question: The collector of the customs levy takes ten dirhams from every two hundred dirhams paid by polytheists, and five dirhams from every two hundred dirhams paid by Muslims. The collector remitted one or two dirhams for certain Muslims. Now the owner of cloth for whom the collector remitted nothing comes to the man for whom he did remit, so that the goods may be registered in that man's name, on the condition that whatever remains is divided equally between the two of them. Is it permissible to take that arrangement or not?

Answer: Whatever the partners have agreed upon and both know of is permissible.

Question: A man died and left two hundred dinars — or less, or more — and zakat, Hajj, khums, al-Shurwa, and expiation were all owed from his estate. Which of these claims is settled first from the capital?

Answer: He begins with the zakat, for it is a debt upon him and is not part of his wealth; then the Hajj, then the khums, then the expiation, then al-Shurwa.

Question: A man died leaving wealth and an heir, and made no bequest at all. Are the khums, al-Shurwa, and the expiation taken from the capital or not?

Answer: The khums must be brought out of the capital. If the heirs are God-fearing, it is their duty to discharge it on behalf of the one who has passed, and so to purify the wealth. If they refuse, then:

> لَآ إِكْرَاهَ فِى ٱلدِّينِ

There is no compulsion in religion.

From another source: whoever pays the zakat of two years to the one authorized to receive it before the second year falls due, it is discharged from him.

Question: A man has two hundred mithqals and brings out the zakat at the head of the hawl. He then leaves one hundred in his house — neither selling nor buying with it — while he trades with the remaining hundred. When the second hawl comes round, does he pay zakat on the two hundred or on the one hundred? And likewise, if he buys a commodity and the hawl passes over it while he has not sold it, and the price has fallen: is zakat due on it or not?

Answer: Zakat remains obligatory on whatever it fell due on for as long as it stays with its owner — whether he trades with it or hoards it — until what he owns falls below the nisab.

Question: It appears in al-Masail that al-Shurwa is one hundred and nineteen dirhams, and that those of means owe a dinar. O our master, what is the limit of means, and what is the weight of a dinar, and what is the weight of a dirham?

Answer: Our master said: the limit of means, for all, is ownership of wealth on which zakat falls due after the hawl — that is, either two hundred dirhams or twenty gold dinars. Each dirham is fourteen carats of silver and two fifths of a carat; by this reckoning, the weight of two hundred dirhams is eighteen ounces by the weights of the Arabs, which is ninety-five kadiyana and a quarter kadiyana, and a wal and a quarter wal of silver by the weights of Gujarat. One then looks at what this silver is worth in the coinage current at the time. Whoever owns this amount, or more, is counted among those of means.

As for gold: each dinar is twenty-four carats, and by this reckoning the weight of twenty dinars is three ounces of gold by the weights of the Arabs, which is sixteen kadiyana less a wal and half a wal of gold. Yet here there is a matter to consider, because the price of gold and silver differs in every age according to people's desire for the one and their lack of desire for the other, and the reverse — so that at times the price of twenty dinars is more than two hundred dirhams, and at other times the opposite. It is therefore fitting for the believer who holds to his religion to reckon by whichever of the two named coinages — gold and silver — is the lesser in price.

Question: A man has five hundred tankas and brings out the fifth — one hundred tankas — in the first year, leaving him four hundred tankas. He traded with them and gained three hundred tankas in profit, of which household expenses took two hundred tankas, leaving him one hundred of the profit. Does he bring the khums out of three hundred tankas or out of one hundred?

Answer: If he has already brought out the khums from the capital, then there is no khums on the profit except on what remains in his hand. Paying khums on the whole is a merit, not a binding obligation.

This is the answer of our master Idris.

He said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: zakat is obligatory when what is liable to it reaches its nisab unencumbered and the hawl has passed over it. He said in it: as for a deposit, if the one holding it alters it and introduces into it what takes it out of the ruling of a deposit, or consumes it and puts wealth of his own in its place, then zakat on all of that is obligatory upon him. And if the hawl passes over the estate of a dead man after he has died and his heirs have not divided it, zakat is not obligatory on the estate as a whole, but is obligatory upon each of the shares: whoever's share reaches a nisab must pay zakat on it, and whoever's share does not reach a nisab — and who has no other wealth besides — owes no zakat on it. If a man has wealth and also owes a debt, and the hawl passes over it, one looks at what he owes and what he has; if his wealth exceeds his debt by a nisab or more, the zakat on the excess over his debt is binding upon him. If a man sells trade goods for the trade goods of another — which he also buys for trade — the second goods are valued at the head of the hawl reckoned from the time he first owned the original goods; and whether he suffered a loss in that or not, he pays what is due from him in zakat.

He said in it: likewise, if he buys trade goods with coin on which zakat is due, the reckoning follows how long the money and the goods stood in his hands; and when the hawl passes over it, he pays the zakat on his wealth.

He said in al-Akhbar: they narrated that whoever sets aside wealth that is surplus to him and then finds it again pays zakat on it. And from it: the apostate begins his zakat anew and does not pay zakat for what was his during his apostasy. He said: there is no zakat in the wealth of an orphan, nor in the wealth of one who lacks his reason.

Tawil al-Daaim said: this means that there is no zakat on that wealth due from the owner who holds it for himself; but whatever of it is held for trade is valued at a price, and zakat is due on it.

He said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: in what comes out of a mine there is the khums, and the remainder is for the one who found it; in buried treasure there is the khums; and dinars found bearing the coinage of Islam fall under the ruling of found property.

Some of the friends of God (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) said: al-Shurwa due from the rich man of means is one hundred and nineteen mithqals of gold, and from the one without means, one hundred and nineteen pieces of silver.

He said in the questions put by our master Sulayman ibn Hasan in his early days, with the answers of our master Daud ibn 'Ajab: I also asked about one who has paid the khums of the lands of his estate — is the khums then due upon their seed or not?

Answer: Yes, may God prolong you; the khums is obligatory upon him in the seed of those lands, as God Almighty said:

> وَاعْلَمُوا أَنَّمَا غَنِمْتُم مِّن شَيْءٍ فَأَنَّ لِلَّهِ خُمُسَهُ

Know that whatever you take as spoil, a fifth of it belongs to God.

These seeds fall under the word "thing," as our master the Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad mentioned and made clear and plain in Kitab al-Himma.

He said in Tawil al-Sharia: Imam al-Muizz li-Din Allah (ع) was asked about zakat, and he said that it is five dirhams, and that the poor are of five classes, with a dirham for each class. Zakat, he said, is mutual consolation: all people and the whole of creation are as a single soul, and when one limb of the body suffers pain, all the limbs turn to that limb to treat it until its pain is gone. When God tests one of the children of the human kind, it is the duty of the kind to occupy itself with his healing. And a man was mentioned in his presence, and it was said, "There is no defect in him." He (ع) said:

> لَوْ كَانَ أَحَدٌ لَمْ يَمْسَسْهُ عَيْبٌ لَمَا أَدْرَكَهُ الْمَوْتُ

If there were anyone whom no defect had touched, death would never have reached him.

Our master, the most exalted summoner, Jafar son of our master Mansur al-Yaman, said at the beginning of the Book of the Inner Nursing: the five prayers are known at five times, and the zakat likewise is five:

1. The khums of every wealth a man earns through trade, sales, and gain.
2. The zakat of wealth whose khums has already been brought out, which is one dinar from every forty dinars; it is an obligation due to the Hujja, and the khums is for the Imam.
3. The zakat of crops, which is likewise its tenth.
4. The zakat of livestock, which is one head from every forty head.
5. The zakat of the soul, which is what the believer pays for himself: the shurwa for the redemption of his neck, and the fitra of his fast — after these four obligatory zakats.

Our master Zayd ibn Muhammad said in al-Risala al-Maknuna: whoever owns twenty mithqals of gold owes half a mithqal. Then he said: and upon whoever owns two hundred dirhams there are five dirhams, and the two hundred are two covenants. He said in it: there is no zakat upon one who does not own twenty mithqals, nor upon one who does not own two hundred dirhams. He said in it: whoever owns two hundred dirhams is forbidden to take zakat and charity. He said in it: the owner of gold and silver owes no zakat on his wealth so long as the hawl has not passed over it.

### Mentioning Zakat on Livestock

He said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: there is no zakat on livestock until they are free-grazing and the hawl has passed over them. If they graze during part of the hawl and are fed with fodder during another part, the ruling follows whichever is the greater part.

He said in it: if someone owns five camels and fails to pay their zakat for two consecutive hawls, he owes a sheep for the first hawl but nothing for the second, since that second sheep already stands against him as a debt. The same principle applies to all livestock other than camels. If a man's camels have reached the threshold where camels are themselves the due, and he also owns sheep, then the zakat is taken from his sheep — from animals fit to serve as sacrificial animals — and the same applies to the zakat on sheep. Buffaloes and cows are alike in their zakat, as are sheep and goats.

He said in Tawil al-Daaim: this applies specifically when zakat has actually fallen due; if nothing has yet become due on them, then no obligation rests on him.

Our master Jafar ibn Mansur al-Yaman stated at the opening of the Book of the Inner Nursing: the zakat on livestock is one head from every forty.

Our master Zayd ibn Muhammad said in al-Risala al-Maknuna: camels are slaughtered by the thrust to the throat and then by the cut. He said in it: no zakat is due on fewer than five camels. He said in it: until the five are complete; and once he owns them, a sheep of the flock is obligatory — one sheep for every five — until they reach twenty, when he pays four sheep. When they reach twenty-five, a bint makhad is due. He said in it: the bint makhad is one whose mother has carried a young one in her belly. He said in it: then with an increase of ten; when they reach thirty-five, a bint labun is due. He said in it: when they reach forty-five, he pays a hiqqa fit to be covered by the stallion. He said in it: then with an increase of fifteen; and when one is added above sixty, a jadha is due. He said in it: above the jadha, nothing further is taken in charity. He said in it: a hiqqa was set at sixty camels because she resembles the stallion and is able to bear what he bears. He said in it: when the camels reach one hundred and twenty, two hiqqas are due, because one hundred and twenty is twice sixty.

He said in it: when cattle reach thirty, a tabi is due. He said in it: when they reach forty, a musinna is due. He said in it: in sixty, two tabis are due. He said in it: in ninety, three yearlings are due. He said in it: when they reach one hundred and twenty, in every forty a musinna is due and in every thirty a tabi.

He said in it, concerning sheep: when they reach forty, one sheep is due as charity. He said in it: up to one hundred and twenty, one sheep remains due. He said in it: when they reach two hundred, two sheep are due. He said in it: then after that, one sheep for every hundred. He said in it: nothing defective is taken in charity. He said in it: the ram of the flock is not taken in charity unless the collector wishes it.

### Mentioning Zakat on Grains, Fruits, and Plants

He said in the book of questions and answers mentioned above:

Question: The nisab of gold is twenty dinars and of silver two hundred dirhams. How then is the nisab of grains reckoned?

Answer: It has come in the Pillars that zakat is due from everything the earth brings forth, and upon this rests the practice of our masters, so it is a duty to follow them. They brought out the tenth from little and from much, and in that is merit. And it has come in the Sharia that the nisab in grains is five awsaq, and the wasq is sixty sa.

He said in al-Akhbar: whatever is sold as a standing crop — that is, grain — has zakat due on it; but whatever is sold before it forms its kernel and is then harvested carries no zakat. The same holds for fruits. They also narrated that the owner of the land does not set off against it what he spent upon it.

He said in Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: this is obligatory upon the young and the old, the rich and the poor, and upon one who is in debt.

From al-Akhbar: they narrated that the land of the tenth is every tract of Arab land against which the Muslims did not advance with horse or riding beast, and every mountain land that its own people brought into cultivation and which no water from the great rivers overflowed. That is the land of the tenth. They narrated that the land of kharaj is the land of the Sudan and those mountain lands against which the Muslims advanced with horse and riding beast and which they brought under cultivation by force. When the people of war submit while still upon their own territory, that land becomes land of the tenth. They further narrated that if a Muslim buys land of kharaj from a protected non-Muslim and takes possession of it, its ruling is not changed by passing to the Muslim, and it remains land of kharaj as it was; and if a protected non-Muslim buys land of the tenth from a Muslim and takes possession of it, the tenth is still due on what it brings forth, because that obligation attaches to the land's produce and no account is taken of the owner.

He said in Maalim al-Din: zakat is obligatory in all grains that run in the course of wheat and dates, and nothing of that reaches the measure unless it reaches five awsaq.

He said in al-Muntakhaba: the nisab of the grains we have mentioned is five awsaq, and the wasq is sixty sa.

He said in al-Akhbar: if part of a crop is watered by one means and part of it by another, the ruling follows whichever portion is the greater; and if the two are equal, three quarters of the tenth are taken from him. He said in it: one half is not joined to another half among grains and fruits, and there is no zakat in any of them until the half reaches the measure at which zakat becomes obligatory.

He said in Mukhtasar al-Athar: the tenth becomes obligatory in grains only when they form their kernels, and at that point an estimate is made — which is when their soundness becomes apparent — and a just estimate is made upon their owners.

He said in the Book of the Inner Nursing: as for the tenth, it is an outward ruling by which the Messenger of God judged, taken from the believers and from the people of the outward. He said in it: likewise, in the outward, whoever pays the tenth of his earnings in truth has rightly discharged his outward obligation, so long as he has kept back only nine tenths; he has done what he was told to do, even if the ruler over his outward affairs had made no demand of him. And whoever withholds some of his earnings and keeps with himself more than nine tenths has not rightly fulfilled what he intended by paying the tenth that was commanded of him, and has not in truth rendered obedience to the ruler over his outward affairs.

Our master Zayd ibn Muhammad said in al-Risala al-Maknuna: these five — meaning dates, raisins, wheat, barley, and grain — when they reach five awsaq, half a wasq of charity is due on them. He said in it: the tenth is obligatory on what the sky waters and what the rivers water; and on what is watered by wheels and by water-carrying beasts, half the tenth is due. He said in it: God imposed the khums on spoils. God Almighty said:

> وَاعْلَمُوا أَنَّمَا غَنِمْتُم مِّن شَيْءٍ فَأَنَّ لِلَّهِ خُمُسَهُ وَلِلرَّسُولِ وَلِذِي الْقُرْبَىٰ وَالْيَتَامَىٰ وَالْمَسَاكِينِ وَابْنِ السَّبِيلِ

Know that whatever you take as spoil, a fifth of it belongs to God, and to the Messenger, and to the near of kin, and the orphans, and the needy, and the wayfarer.

These are five stations to which the khums is directed. He said in it: the khums is taken from mines, from treasures, and from spoils.

And from Jafar ibn Muhammad al-Sadiq (ع) it is transmitted that he said:

> كَانَ رَسُولُ اللَّهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَآلِهِ إِذَا أَتَاهُ الْمَغْنَمُ أَخَذَ نَصِيبَهُ ثُمَّ يَقْسِمُ مَا بَقِيَ بَيْنَ الْقُرْبَى وَالْيَتَامَى وَالْمَسَاكِينِ وَابْنِ السَّبِيلِ

When spoils came to the Messenger of God, he would take his share and then distribute what remained among the near of kin, the orphans, the needy, and the wayfarer.

## 29. The Charity of the Head

I turn now to zakat الْفِطْر.

In the first book of questions and answers — arranged according to the chapter order of the Book of the Pillars of Islam — the following exchange appears. Question: the custom among the Indians when collecting the fitra is to mix four kinds of grain — rice, maize, wheat, and salt — and then take their monetary value, whereas in the radiant Shariah the zakat of Fitr is a sa of dates, raisins, wheat, or barley. Does it suffice a person who gives a sa of barley? Answer: the reliance is on what is stated in the Pillars — that is, a sa of wheat, barley, dates, or raisins.

> وَالْفِطْرَةُ مِنْ أَطْيَبِ مَا تَأْكُلُونَ وَأَزْكَى مَا تَدَّخِرُونَ

The fitra is to be from the best of what you eat and the purest of what you store away.

Whoever mixes the fitra from everything he ate during Ramadan, that suffices him; and whoever ate nothing but barley, that too suffices him. Verify this well.

Question: what is the ruling for a man who wishes to pay his fitra before Ramadan begins, fearing that poverty or travel may prevent him from paying it at its proper time? What does the noble law say on this — does it suffice him or not? The question arises because obligatory duties may not be performed outside their appointed times, nor made up except after those times have passed. And if it does not suffice him, it should be noted that the Book of the Pillars states there is no harm in collecting zakat from those who owe it before its due time, when there is need for that. Please provide the answer on this, and also address the case of one who did this out of ignorance — has it sufficed him? Answer: if he delivers his fitra to someone who has the right to receive it, it has sufficed him.
