The prohibition in this matter is that a person should not break his fast before delivering his fitra. No fixed time was set for advancing or delaying it; the obligation is simply its delivery.

He stated in the book Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: a dhimmi who accepts Islam before the break of dawn on the day of Fitr owes the zakat of Fitr; if he accepts Islam after the break of dawn, nothing is due from him.

He stated in the reports: they narrated that the zakat of Fitr is paid on behalf of the mukatab. He stated in the Landmarks of Religion: if a child is born before the break of dawn on the day of Fitr, the zakat of Fitr is due on his behalf. He stated in the Book of the Pillars: it has likewise been said that it is paid on behalf of the child still in his mother's womb, before birth. He stated in the Selected Book: or a sa of dried curd, or half a sa of wheat. He stated in the Landmarks of Religion that the weight of the sa is four and a half ratls by the ratl of Mecca; he stated therein that its weight by the ratl of Medina is five ratls less a third of a ratl; and its weight by the Baghdadi ratl is nine ratls and a tenth of a ratl.

Our master and lord, the author of the Mustansiriyya sessions — peace of God be upon him — stated in the thirteenth session: since we mentioned in the session before this one the zakat that is obligatory upon those who possess wealth, we follow that with the mention of the zakat that is obligatory in every condition, and upon those of small means who give it in charity — and that is the zakat of Fitr. In it are seven obligations and twelve practices of the Sunnah. The obligations are:

1. that a man pay it on behalf of himself;
2. on behalf of the elder of his household;
3. and the younger of them;
4. and the free among them;
5. and the enslaved among them;
6. and the males among them;
7. and the females among them.

The practices of the Sunnah are that he bring it out from the middle sort of what he eats — a sa of wheat, or a sa of barley, or a sa of dates, or a sa of raisins; half a sa of wheat suffices him, and from anything other than wheat nothing suffices but a full sa; and the sa is four mudds. He should hand it to the Imam (ع), or to whomever the Imam has appointed; it should be brought out before the breaking of the fast; whoever delays bringing it out must make it up; and whoever lacks the food brings out its value in silver or in gold. As for the woman who has no husband with her, she brings it out on behalf of herself and on behalf of those she supports.

He stated in the book of the Inner Nursing, regarding the saying of the Messenger of God:

> اَلْفِطْرَةُ لَا تَحِلُّ لِي وَلَا لِأَهْلِ بَيْتِي

The fitra is not lawful for me, nor for the people of my house.

Our master Zayd ibn Muhammad — may God sanctify his spirit — stated in the Concealed Epistle: as for the charity of Fitr, the Messenger of God made it obligatory upon every head among the Muslims to pay his fitra — upon the males and the females, the small and the great, even the infant born on that very day — and it is paid on behalf of the child in his mother's womb. He made obligatory upon every head a sa of wheat, dates, raisins, barley, or other grains. He stated therein:

> وَسُمِّيَتْ صَدَقَةُ الْفِطْرِ صَدَقَةَ الرَّأْسِ

The charity of Fitr has been named the charity of the head.

He stated therein: it is paid before the prayer, and if it is paid before the prayer it is charity. He stated therein: if the child is born after the Fitr, the fitra is paid before the Fitr.

He stated in the book Mafatih al-Nima — which belongs to our master the Judge al-Numan ibn Muhammad, may God exalt his sanctity — concerning the fitra:

> فَاِنْ أُخْرِجَتْ قَبْلَ طُلُوعِ الشَّمْسِ كَانَتْ فِطْرَةً وَإِذَا أُخْرِجَتْ بَعْدَ طُلُوعِ الشَّمْسِ كَانَتْ صَدَقَةً

If it is brought out before sunrise, it counts as fitra; if it is brought out after sunrise, it is ordinary charity.

He stated, up to his words:

> وَمَعْنَى الْفِطْرَةِ إِخْرَاجُ الْوَاجِبِ لِلَّهِ وَلِوَلِيِّهِ مِنَ الْفِكَاكِ وَالْقُرْبَانِ

The meaning of fitra is the bringing out of what is owed to God and to His appointed guardian, as ransom and as offering.

I turn now to various rulings on zakat, on the one upon whom no zakat falls, and on other matters. In a book of legal questions — each one a marvel — he raised another question: what do you say concerning a man for whom the zakat on his wealth has been made lawful? The answer: in that case he is a man who owns a thousand dinars and owes a thousand dinars, so there is no zakat upon him on it. He stated therein another question: what do you say concerning a man for whom the zakat on his wealth is lawful while he himself is well off? The answer: in that case he is a man among the polytheists who has found no Muslim to whom to hand the zakat of his wealth, so he has more right to it than anyone else.

## 30. A Shield Against the Fire

I turn now to the Book of Fasting, and to the obligation of fasting the month of Ramadan.

He stated in the book Mukhtasar al-Musannaf: fasting is a shield from the Fire — a shield upon the tongues, the hearing, the eyes, and all the other limbs. It is not a matter of food and drink alone; every limb has its own portion in the fast, and whoever fulfills that portion is truly fasting. Whoever abandons any part of it diminishes the merit of his fast in proportion to what he has left. Fasting the month of Ramadan is an obligation upon every Muslim who is of age, sound of mind, healthy, and resident — and upon every woman likewise. He also stated therein: there is no fasting for one who is a hypocrite, disobedient to God.

He stated in the book al-Idah: what has come concerning fasting upon the sighting of the crescent, and what is mentioned in this chapter and in the two chapters before it, applies only to one who is absent from the presence of the Imam and does not know the measures of the crescents.

Our master and Mawlana Jafar ibn Sayyidina Sulayman, may God exalt his sanctity, stated in his noble epistle entitled The Sharpest Sword in Nullifying Innovations, in which he counseled his people and roused those among them who had grown heedless and whose sleep had lengthened — from the people of the island of Yemen, while he dwelt there and settled among them:

"Chapter: Among these matters also is what has been heard and known from most of you: that the honored month of Ramadan is not honored with the honor that God, glorified be He, and His Messenger (ع) have prescribed for it. It is the month of God, the Great, the Generous. That honor lies in placing the word 'month' before it, as God, glorified be He, said concerning it:

> شَهْرُ رَمَضَانَ

The month of Ramadan.

Yet you, when you name it, call it 'Ramadan' alone, and that is never seemly in the judgment of the intellect, nor does it guide in this matter toward right guidance — for a person does not address one who is dear to him among people except with a word he places before his name, according to what is known among you and whose glory and form are recognized, together with what is established by the aforementioned word of God, glorified be He:

> وَإِلَى ٱللَّهِ تُرْجَعُ ٱلْأُمُورُ

And to God all matters return.

Beyond this, it has come concerning it from our master al-Qadi al-Numan ibn Muhammad, may God exalt his sanctity, in the book Mukhtasar al-Athar al-Sharif, that your Mawla the Imam al-Baqir Abu Jafar Muhammad ibn Ali (ع) said:

> لَا تَقُولُونَ رَمَضَانَ وَلَكِنْ قُولُوا كَمَا قَالَ اللَّهُ تَعَالَى شَهْرَ رَمَضَانَ

Do not say Ramadan, but say as God Almighty said: the month of Ramadan.

So whoever has had God, glorified be He, cause his ear to hear this saying — how can it be permissible for him to oppose it and turn away from it, being of the people of piety and faith? The heedless one who turns away from it after having come to stand upon it makes light of God, glorified be He, and of His noble month mentioned above, and of the weighty saying of the Imam al-Baqir (ع) mentioned above. And the one who speaks by this noble book while turning away from acting upon what is in it enters under the censure contained in the words of God, glorified be He, in the verses of His noble and guarded Book, where He, glorified be He, says:

> يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا لِمَ تَقُولُونَ مَا لَا تَفْعَلُونَ
>
> كَبُرَ مَقْتًا عِندَ اللَّهِ أَن تَقُولُوا مَا لَا تَفْعَلُونَ

O you who believe, why do you say what you do not do? It is greatly hateful in the sight of God that you say what you do not do."

He stated in the book Tawil al-Shariah: fasting becomes obligatory upon a boy once he can tell his right hand from his left.

He stated in the fourteenth majlis of the Mustansiriyya councils, God's peace be upon its author: what follows is what we recite to you concerning the meaning of fasting, and that it is divided into categories, of which seven are obligatory and twelve are of the practice and commended. The obligatory ones are:

1. Fasting the month of Ramadan every year.
2. Fasting two consecutive months for one who deliberately breaks his fast on a day of it and cannot find a slave to free.
3. Fasting two consecutive months for one who kills a believer by mistake, if he likewise cannot find a slave to free, together with paying the blood money to the family.
4. In the expiation of zihar, fasting two consecutive months for one who cannot find a slave to free.
5. In the expiation for breaking an oath, fasting three days for one who can find neither the feeding nor the clothing nor the freeing of a slave.
6. The fast of one who performs tamattu with the lesser pilgrimage up to the pilgrimage, when he cannot find an offering, and it is ten days.
7. The fast of a vow.

As for the fasts of the practice and the commended:

1. The fast of itikaf.
2. The fast of Shaban.
3. The fast of the first Thursday of every month.
4. The fast of the middle Wednesday of that month.
5. The fast of the last Thursday of that month.
6. The fast of the three white days.
7. The fast of Dawud (ع), which is fasting one day and breaking the fast the next, and it is the most rigorous of fasts.
8. The fast of Friday joined to Thursday or to Saturday, without singling out Friday alone for fasting.
9. The fast of the day of Arafah for one who is not on pilgrimage.
10. The fast of the boy when he reaches seven years.
11. The fast of recompense for game taken in the sacred state, which varies according to what is ruled by the one to whom God has entrusted the ruling in it.
12. The fast for shaving the head on account of harm.

Our master Zaid ibn Muhammad, may God sanctify his soul, stated in the Concealed Epistle: God Almighty imposed upon His servants, after the performance of prayer and the giving of zakat, the fasting of the month of Ramadan, and He commanded that one month out of twelve be fasted. He stated therein: just as the fasting of the month of Ramadan is obligatory, it is thirty days.

Mawlana Amir al-Muminin Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) stated in his Four Hundred Manners:

> صَوْمُ ثَلَاثَةِ أَيَّامٍ مِنْ كُلِّ شَهْرٍ وَصَوْمُ شَعْبَانَ يُذْهِبُ بِوَسْوَاسِ الصَّدْرِ وَبَلَابِلِ الْقَلْبِ

Fasting three days of every month, and fasting Shaban, drive away the whisperings of the breast and the disquiet of the heart.

## 31. The Quarrel Over the Crescent

I turn now to the sighting of the crescent moon: what has been said about it, and what runs along the same course.

I find in the Book of Clarification that what has come down concerning fasting by the sighting of the crescent — together with what is mentioned in this chapter and in the two chapters before it — is directed to whoever is absent from the presence of the Imam and does not know the measures of the crescent moons. Our master and mawlana Sulayman ibn Hasan, may God exalt his sanctity in the chambers of the Gardens of Eden, treated this matter at the opening of his noble epistle known as the Ijadiyya, containing the Ahmadi secrets and the golden jewels drawn from the most glorious benefits. After he had spoken there of what is prior and more fitting, and after the restatement of both, he explained that when the moon has traveled twenty-eight stations after parting from the sun, it requires the traversal of the first two stations in order to overtake the sun once more, because during that period the sun has itself advanced two complete stations and a degree of the third. The moon therefore meets the sun at thirty stations, and the reckoners count this as one month.

Our master and mawlana, the most exalted summoner al-Muayyad fil-Din, may God exalt his sanctity, spoke on this in the forty-second session of the first hundred of his noble Muayyadi sessions — those known as the Wisdom Sessions — in refutation of those among the people who make fasting depend upon the sighting of the crescent.

The claimants have claimed that the month of Ramadan is sometimes complete and sometimes deficient, and that its fast is founded upon the sighting of the crescent. They have argued for this by the saying of the Prophet (ع):

> صُومُوا لِرُؤْيَتِهِ وَأَفْطِرُوا لِرُؤْيَتِهِ فَإِنْ غُمَّ عَلَيْكُمْ فَأَكْمِلُوا ثَلَاثِينَ

Fast when it is seen and break your fast when it is seen; and if it is hidden from you, then complete thirty.

This statement of theirs is unsound in several respects, which we shall set out, and we shall establish the proofs that the month of Ramadan is always complete and that no deficiency befalls it in any circumstance whatever.

We begin with the reply to whoever argues from the report "fast when it is seen and break your fast when it is seen." We say to them: you yourselves acknowledge that the sense of this report is that the Messenger of Allah (ع), when he wished to set out on one of the campaigns near the month of Ramadan, was approached by his companions, who gathered to him and said:

> يَا رَسُولَ اللَّهِ كُنَّا نَصُومُ بِصَوْمِكَ وَنُفْطِرُ بِإِفْطَارِكَ فَكَيْفَ يَجْرِي حَالُنَا فِي غَيْبَتِكَ

O Messenger of Allah, we used to fast by your fasting and break our fast by your breaking of the fast; how then shall our case run in your absence?

The Messenger of Allah (ع) said: fast when it is seen and break your fast when it is seen. The report thus indicates that fasting is obligatory by the fasting of the Messenger when he is present, or by the fasting of the one who stands in his place when he is absent, and that breaking the fast is obligatory by his breaking of the fast, and that fasting upon the sighting of the crescent belongs to the matters of necessity at the time when the Messenger and the Imam who stands in his place are not present. When the Messenger is present, or the Imam is present, the rule of the obligation is that one fasts by his fasting and breaks the fast by his breaking of it, just as the people said to the Prophet (ع): we used to fast by your fasting and break our fast by your breaking of the fast.

As for the saying of the one who claims that the Prophet (ع) used to fast by the sighting of the crescent, it is unsound in three respects, two of them legal and one rational.

As for the first of the two legal respects: it is well known that the Prophet (ع) used to say — and he is truthful — that the Trustworthy Spirit, Gabriel (ع), came upon him morning and evening with revelation and with the noble Quran. One to whom Gabriel (ع) comes morning and evening with the tidings of heaven has no need to turn his face about seeking the crescent when he has with him one who brings him certain news. Were he to go out toward the sky seeking a crescent, it would nullify his own saying that Gabriel descends upon him, and it would be said to him: why did you not ask him about it? So his independence of any searching is established.

The other respect is that it is handed down from him (ع) in the reports that he said:

> أَنَا بِطُرُقَاتِ السَّمَاءِ أَعْرَفُ مِنْكُمْ بِطُرُقَاتِ الْأَرْضِ

> أَنَا أَعْرِفُ مَنَازِلَ السَّمَاءِ أَحْسَنَ مِمَّا تَعْرِفُونَ مَنَازِلَ الْأَرْضِ.

Had he been observed going out to seek the crescent after saying this, people would rightly have asked him: what then becomes of your words of yesterday — that you know the roads of the sky better than we know the roads of the earth?

As for the rational ground: it is well established that the Prophet (ع) stands far above any ignorance of the differences in crescent risings and their degrees — a matter scarcely hidden even from an ordinary astronomer — and that earthly locations differ as well. Some are elevated, making the sighting of the crescent swifter there — such as mountain summits and what runs in their course; others are low-lying, making the sighting slower. Knowing that none of this was hidden from him — and that had it been hidden, it would have been the gravest deficiency, from which he is wholly free — how could reason possibly require that, with full knowledge of these visible differences, he should impose the obligation of fasting, which is bound up with the obligation of pilgrimage, upon all people by a single standard, while knowing that standard cannot hold? One people see the crescent on a given night; another people do not. Nor can it be sound that one and the same day should belong to Shaban where the crescent is not seen and to the month of Ramadan where it is seen. No reasonable person doubts this; only an ignorant man rejects it.

Beyond this, God, glory be to Him, has said in the firm text of His Book:

> كُتِبَ عَلَيْكُمُ ٱلصِّيَامُ كَمَا كُتِبَ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَتَّقُونَ

Fasting is prescribed for you as it was prescribed for those before you, so that you may be mindful of God.

"Those before you" refers to the Christians, whose fasting is governed not by sighting but by calculation. He then said, confirming that:

> أَيَّامًا مَعْدُودَاتٍ

for a counted number of days.

Counted days are days that remain always counted, requiring neither sighting nor observation. Had the month of Ramadan been capable of being thirty days at one time and twenty-nine at another, He would certainly not have said "a counted number of days." It is like saying: this is a reckoned reckoning, and this is a counted dirham.

A further argument is this: since the year is ordered so that six of its months are complete and six deficient, its root and structure must rest on completeness rather than deficiency. The sequence runs as follows:

- Muharram: complete
- Safar: deficient
- Rabi al-Awwal: complete
- Rabi al-Akhir: deficient
- Jumada al-Ula: complete
- Jumada al-Akhira: deficient
- Rajab: complete
- Shaban: deficient
- The month of Ramadan: complete

The Prophet (ع) said:

> مَا تَمَّ شَعْبَانُ وَلَا نَقَصَ رَمَضَانُ

Shaban is never complete, and Ramadan is never deficient.

The proof of Shaban's deficiency lies in the night that falls at its midpoint — a true middle night, with no equivalent in Rajab or in Ramadan. The fifteenth night of Shaban is its genuine midpoint: fourteen nights precede it and fourteen follow it, placing it exactly in the center. Ramadan admits no such midpoint. To call its fifteenth night the middle fails, because fifteen days of the month still remain after it. To call its sixteenth night the middle fails equally, because sixteen is not half of thirty.

A further sign of Ramadan's completeness is the structure of the expiations prescribed for anyone who willfully breaks his fast during it: he must fast two consecutive months as an act of repentance to God — that is, sixty days, like the month of Ramadan — and if he is not able, then the feeding of sixty poor persons. Were it possible for the month of Ramadan to be twenty-nine days, it would be possible for the expiation to be the feeding of sixty poor persons or of fifty-eight poor persons. Praise God, then, O believers, for what He has preserved you from of deficiency in your religion, and for the success He has granted you in fulfilling the conditions of faith by following your masters, the friends of the Merciful.

He, may God exalt his sanctity, spoke of the like of this in the book of his noble life. He relates that he attended upon King Abu Kalijar al-Daylami, lord of Shiraz and of Iraq al-Ajam and its king, and that a sharif from the notables of the Zaydi school was brought before him. Speech passed between the two of them on the matter of fasting and its obligation, and whether it rests upon sighting or upon something other than the known sighting. He says that the aforementioned sharif said to him in this connection: it is related of the Messenger of Allah (ع) that he gathered his five fingers and said:

> نَحْنُ أُمِّيُّونَ لَا نَحْسِبُ الْحِسَابَ الصَّوْمُ مَرَّةً هَكَذَا

We are unlettered; we do not reckon the reckoning. The fast is once like this.

He continued until he had completed the count of thirty in six turns; then he gathered his fingers a second time, and when he came to the last he withheld one finger and said: and once like this.

Our master al-Muayyad fil-Din, may God sanctify his soul, said to him: God forbid that the Prophet (ع) — whom God honored with the ascension and to whom He revealed what lies beyond the veil — should be charged with such halting speech and such inarticulacy, and should be so unlettered that he could not distinguish thirty from twenty-nine with his tongue, and instead had to gather his fingers and move his hand these many times, in the manner of one who herds cattle and sheep. Moreover, the Prophet (ع) said: I know the roads of the sky better than you know the roads of the earth. Had he — this mighty saying being taken as settled and confined to him — then risen to scan the sky in search of the crescent, the people would have risen to confront him, saying: where does this leave your claim of yesterday, that you know the roads of the sky better than we know the roads of the earth? And besides this, were the seeking of the crescent a thing whose obligation and necessity were established, it would be so for us in particular, while he (ع) would have no need of it, since Gabriel (ع) informs him that the crescent has appeared. His asking Gabriel about that would be more fitting for him than his scanning the sky on his own account, thereby rendering the descent of revelation upon him of no effect. Indeed, if one of us found a way to an angel among the angels to ask for a ruling and for tidings of hidden matters, he would not rely upon himself in searching and striving. This is a thing upon which no proof and no demonstration can stand.

The Alid was so shamed by this reply that he rose stumbling in his own robes, until those standing in the outer ranks and the masters began to laugh at him and mock him. Then each of us withdrew to his house.

Some of the summoners, may God sanctify their souls, spoke of the like of this in the argument against the opponents, for guidance along the paths, in the form of a question:

What do the eminent scholars among all the people of Islam say concerning the saying of the Lord of Majesty and Honor:

> يَا أَيُّهَا الَّذِينَ آمَنُوا كُتِبَ عَلَيْكُمُ الصِّيَامُ كَمَا كُتِبَ عَلَى الَّذِينَ مِنْ قَبْلِكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَتَّقُونَ أَيَّامًا مَعْدُودَاتٍ فَمَنْ كَانَ مِنْكُمْ مَرِيضًا أَوْ عَلَى سَفَرٍ فَعِدَّةٌ مِنْ أَيَّامٍ أُخَرَ

"O you who believe, fasting is prescribed for you as it was prescribed for those before you, so that you may be mindful of God, for a counted number of days; and whoever among you is sick or on a journey, then a number of other days" — up to His saying, mighty and majestic:

> فَمَن شَهِدَ مِنكُمُ ٱلشَّهْرَ فَلْيَصُمْهُ وَمَن كَانَ مَرِيضًا أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍۢ فَعِدَّةٌۭ مِّنْ أَيَّامٍ أُخَرَ يُرِيدُ ٱللَّهُ بِكُمُ ٱلْيُسْرَ وَلَا يُرِيدُ بِكُمُ ٱلْعُسْرَ وَلِتُكْمِلُوا۟ ٱلْعِدَّةَ وَلِتُكَبِّرُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ عَلَىٰ مَا هَدَىٰكُمْ وَلَعَلَّكُمْ تَشْكُرُونَ

Who, then, did God intend by His words "those before you, so that you may be mindful of God"? Who are "those before"? How many are the counted days? And what is the witnessing of the month?

The scholars of the Arabic language have said that الشهر (al-shahr) means the crescent. Others have said that the month is a counted number of days in its entirety, completed when it ends and the next month begins. In the words of God the Exalted:

> شَهْرُ رَمَضَانَ ٱلَّذِىٓ أُنزِلَ فِيهِ ٱلْقُرْآنُ

The month of Ramadan in which the Quran was sent down.

there is an indication that it was sent down in counted days, not in the crescent. God has declared that the number of months in His reckoning is twelve:

> إِنَّ عِدَّةَ ٱلشُّهُورِ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ ٱثْنَا عَشَرَ شَهْرًۭا فِى كِتَبِ ٱللَّهِ يَوْمَ خَلَقَ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضَ مِنْهَآ أَرْبَعَةٌ حُرُمٌۭ

The number of months with God is twelve months in the Book of God, from the day He created the heavens and the earth; four of them are sacred.

I note that only one crescent is known — one that waxes until it becomes a full moon, then wanes through the remaining days of the month — while the months themselves run from Muharram to Dhu al-Hijja.

Some have argued, with regard to His saying "so whoever among you witnesses the month, let him fast it," that it means: when one looks upon the crescent. They further take His saying "and whoever among you is sick or on a journey" to indicate that the witness in view is the resident, as opposed to the absent traveler; and it is the resident who fasts and is granted no dispensation to break it.

Then there is His saying "and that you complete the count and magnify God for having guided you." What count is meant here, and how much does it amount to? The magnification falls at the festival of breaking the fast, after the fasting is completed. Is the completed count of the month thirty days, or twenty-nine, or twenty-eight? And which of these is the fuller count and the longer span?

The Prophet (ع) said:

> مَا أَتَاكُمْ عَنِّي فَاعْرِضُوهُ عَلَى كِتَابِ اللَّهِ فَمَا وَافَقَ كِتَابَ اللَّهِ فَأَنَا قُلْتُهُ وَمَا خَالَفَهُ فَلَمْ أَقُلْهُ وَكَيْفَ أُخَالِفُ كِتَابَ اللَّهِ وَعَلَيَّ نَزَلَ
>
> "Whatever comes to you from me, set it against the Book of God. Whatever agrees with the Book of God, I said it; and whatever contradicts it, I did not say it. How should I contradict the Book of God when it was sent down upon me?"

Now, those who fast by the rising of the crescent may fall short of completing the count of thirty. They have related from the Prophet (ع) that he said: fast when it is seen and break your fast when it is seen. Yet this, by their own account, does not accord with the complete count that the noble Book has declared. Surely in that is a reminder for those possessed of understanding.

Then there has come down from the Prophet (ع), in what al-Sadiq Jafar ibn Muhammad (ع) related, that he said:

> مَا تَمَّ شَعْبَانُ وَلَا نَقَصَ شَهْرُ رَمَضَانَ

Shaban is never complete, and the month of Ramadan is never deficient.

This accords with the plain Book, and the completeness of the month's count at thirty days is thereby established.

We found the saying of the Prophet (ع), "fast when it is seen and break your fast when it is seen," to indicate the anticipation of its sighting. Examining what the scholars of the language have recorded about the uses of the letter ل (اَللَّام), we found this lam to be the lam of anticipation — as one of them recorded in verse:

> فَلِلْمَوْتِ تَغْذُوا الْوَالِدَاتُ عِيَالَهَا
> كَمَا لِخَرَابِ الدَّارِ تُبْنَى الْمَسَاكِنَا

Mothers nourish their children for death, just as dwellings are built for the ruin of the house.

And another said: they were born for death, and they built for ruin. Of this same kind is the saying of God the Exalted in the Book:

> هَذَا مَا تُوعَدُونَ لِيَوْمِ ٱلْحِسَابِ

This is what you are promised for the Day of Reckoning.

God stated the promise first and then said "for the Day of Reckoning," making the lam the lam of outcome (لَامُ الْعَاقِبَةِ); and this agrees with what we have said, and it is the most fitting and correct view. So the saying of the Prophet (ع), "fast when it is seen," means: let it rise while you are fasting; and "break your fast when it is seen" means: let it rise while you are breaking the fast. And God knows best.

If you say otherwise, then bring your proof concerning the completeness of the month's count that the Quran has declared. And why did the Messenger of Allah (ع) not say, fast after seeing it and break your fast after seeing it, so that the matter would be clearer to his community? For it is said: work for a day on which you shall be raised:

> اِعْمَلُوا لِيَوْمٍ فِيهِ تُبْعَثُونَ وَيَكُونُ لِلْآخِرَةِ مَا تَعْمَلُونَ

Work for a day on which you will be raised up, and let what you do be for the hereafter.

The revealed Book, then, agrees with the saying of the Prophet who was sent. Bring us the satisfying answer, for we do not walk the road of obstinacy or the rejection of what is plain to the eye. Praise be to God, the High, the Bestower, Lord of bounty, favor, and kindness. May God bless His Messenger to all mankind and jinn, and his family and his companions, who made clear the meanings of the Quran, for as long as night and day alternate and the two ages follow one another; and may He grant them peace, mercy, and honor. God suffices us, and He is the best guardian.

Our master Zayd ibn Muhammad, may God sanctify his soul, said in his epistle known as the Maknuna, concerning the interpretation of the eclipse prayer, that God — blessed and exalted — when He created creation, ordained among them a wondrous ordinance whose beginning confirms its end. He made all that He created in the first age a proof for the people of the last age. He made the eclipse of the moon a veiling for it, and ordained that this veiling should fall when fourteen nights of the month have passed, though it may occur at thirteen nights; yet what is relied upon, and what happens most often, is at fourteen nights. In all of this are causes and proofs of God that stand as witnesses. He further ordained that the moon's absence and concealment from the world should fall at twenty-eight nights in every month, so that in its concealment it is absent for two days and two nights — forty-eight hours in all — and then rises again after those forty-eight hours.

What follows is the blessed epistle on the month of Ramadan, known as al-Lazima, treating the fast of the month of Ramadan and its proper time. It is by our master and mawlana, the most exalted, unique, and brilliant summoner Ahmad Hamid al-Din ibn Abdallah al-Kirmani, may God exalt his sanctity and grant us his intercession and his companionship — the proof of our mawlana al-Hakim bi-Amr Allah, Commander of the Faithful, may God bless him.

## 32. Two Kinds of Sight

In the name of God, the Merciful, the Compassionate, and in Him I place my trust.

Praise be to Allah, through whose grace good deeds reach completion, and through obedience to His guardians blessings spread to all. May Allah bless His messenger and servant Muhammad — the finest of mankind and the glory of the noble messengers who came before him — and bless the righteous among his pure progeny, those beacons of guidance and moons of the dark night, and grant them abundant peace.

To proceed: my brother, you wrote to me noting that the people of division and disagreement — true to the habits they have long cultivated — have set themselves against the people of truth and concord. Their objections have multiplied in challenge, faulting them in community matters and reproaching them for beginning the fast before the general public. You asked me to clarify the question of entering the fast among the Shia of the Commander of the Faithful (ع), and in particular the fast of the year four hundred, in which commands issued from the Pure Presence made it obligatory to begin the fast two days before the crescent was seen with the eye. You asked me to set out the reason for this with statement, clarification, and proof.

I therefore resolved to compose this epistle, in which I would advance such healing proofs that the eyes of the discerning could graze at ease upon the order of its foundations, while the eyes of the opponents would smart at any attempt to take up its meanings. And so I say, seeking help from Allah and from His guardians upon His earth — (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) — and I have named it the necessary epistle on fasting the month of Ramadan and its time. For what I have placed within it are proofs binding upon the whole body of Muslims, from which they have no escape in the first principle of reason or of sense. Allah is the guardian of right success.

Know — may Allah preserve you from the disgrace of ignorance and guard you from the affliction of misguidance — that had the opposing party possessed discernment and insight, or had they any share in reason and understanding, the cunning of Satan would have been turned away from them and their aspirations directed toward the truth. They would have been among those whom Allah excepts in His Book when He says:

> وَلَا يَزَالُونَ مُخْتَلِفِينَ اِلَّا مَنْ رَحِمَ رَبُّكَ
>
> "They will not cease to differ, except those upon whom your Lord has mercy."

But they mounted the steed of caprice, and the causes of mere personal opinion carried them off. Satan made their conduct seem fair to them, and they followed him — all but a company of the believers. So the word of Allah came true of them:

> أَفَلَمْ يَسِيرُوا فِي الْأَرْضِ فَتَكُونَ لَهُمْ قُلُوبٌ يَعْمَلُونَ بِهَا أَوْ آذَانٌ يَسْمَعُونَ بِهَا فَأِنَّهَا لَا تَعْمَى الْأَبْصَارُ وَلَكِنَّ تَعْمَى الْقُلُوبُ الَّتِي فِي الصُّدُورِ
>
> "Have they not traveled through the land so that they might have hearts with which to understand, or ears with which to hear? For it is not the eyes that go blind, but the hearts within the breasts that go blind."

In such a state, they are like cattle — indeed, like the dead — and they do not perceive. As Allah Almighty said:

> أَمْوَتٌ غَيْرُ أَحْيَآءٍۢ وَمَا يَشْعُرُونَ
>
> "Dead, not living, and they do not perceive."

They have died to the knowledge of realities and grown blind to them — blinded by their arrogance toward the people of truth, their insolence, and their willful disagreement. They have become like beasts in their disputes: wavering back and forth, stumbling about in their blindness, devoid of discernment or insight. They fell into this disagreement and separation — which Allah has forbidden — as He declares:

> وَلَا تَكُونُوا۟ كَٱلَّذِينَ تَفَرَّقُوا۟ وَٱخْتَلَفُوا۟
>
> "Do not be like those who separated and fell into disagreement."

They fell into it solely through their insolence, their arrogance, their envy, and their disdain for obeying the one whose obedience Allah has made obligatory among those charged with the Command — those to whom Allah Almighty directed that all disputed matters be referred, as He says:

> فَإِن تَنَزَعْتُمْ فِى شَىْءٍۢ فَرُدُّوهُ إِلَى ٱللَّهِ وَٱلرَّسُولِ
>
> "If you dispute about anything, refer it to Allah and to the Messenger."

And He said:

> وَلَوْ رَدُّوهُ إِلَى ٱلرَّسُولِ وَإِلَىٰٓ أُو۟لِى ٱلْأَمْرِ مِنْهُمْ لَعَلِمَهُ ٱلَّذِينَ يَسْتَنۢبِطُونَهُۥ مِنْهُمْ
>
> "Had they referred it to the Messenger and to those charged with authority among them, those among them who draw out conclusions would have known it."

Whoever sets aside arrogance, insolence, and envy, and submits to the people of truth, comes to stand upon realities and leaves the company of the people of ignorance. Whoever opposes the people of truth, however, and persists in his current state — wandering in blindness and misguidance — is brought to ruin and to an evil saying; the word of punishment comes true against him, and he earns the sign of chastisement. We seek refuge in Allah from all of that — from division after concord, from tyranny after faith, and from disobedience toward His guardians, may (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ), after submission and certainty. We ask Him to place us among those who attain salvation through His mercy and who stand firm in obeying Him and obeying His guardians, for He is the best of those who are asked.

He then made fasting obligatory upon His servants in the explicit text of His Book, saying:

> كُتِبَ عَلَيْكُمُ ٱلصِّيَامُ كَمَا كُتِبَ عَلَى ٱلَّذِينَ مِن قَبْلِكُمْ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَتَّقُونَ
>
> "Fasting has been prescribed for you as it was prescribed for those before you, so that you may guard yourselves."

The Prophet (ع) said:

> شَعْبَانُ شَهْرِي وَرَمَضَانُ شَهْرُ اللَّهِ وَصَوْمُهُ فَرِيضَةٌ
>
> "Shaban is my month, and Ramadan is the month of Allah, and fasting it is an obligation."

The Imams (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ) said:

> صَوْمُ شَهْرِ رَمَضَانَ فَرِيضَةٌ عَلَى كُلِّ مُسْلِمٍ
>
> "The fast of the month of Ramadan is an obligation upon every Muslim."

When Allah Almighty imposed the fast of Ramadan upon His servants, the Prophet (ع) did not leave them blind to the knowledge of its time and season — just as he did not leave them in ignorance of the other obligations, such as the numbers of the prayers and their times when those were prescribed, and the amounts of alms due on various kinds of property when those were made obligatory. So he said:

> صُومُوا لِرُؤْيَتِهِ وَأَفْطِرُوا لِرُؤْيَتِهِ
>
> "Fast at its sighting and break the fast at its sighting."

Scarcely any disagreement arises over this narration among the sects of the Muslims. It is the pillar upon which the people of the outward rest their argument.

I say, then, that Allah Almighty created all things in pairs, so that the One alone in oneness should be He, and nothing should share with Him in it. Allah said:

> سُبْحَنَ ٱلَّذِى خَلَقَ ٱلْأَزْوَجَ كُلَّهَا
>
> "Glory be to the One who created all the pairs."

And He said:

> وَمِن كُلِّ شَىْءٍ خَلَقْنَا زَوْجَيْنِ لَعَلَّكُمْ تَذَكَّرُونَ
>
> "Of every thing We created two kinds, so that you might remember."

Since this is so — and since we see all things in pairs and counterparts, such as substance and accident, sky and earth, land and sea, plain and mountain, night and day, good and evil, body and spirit, outward and inward, this world and the next, male and female, and others too many to enumerate — and since we see that the Prophet (ع) made pairing the foundation and governing principle of his law, joining commands and obligations to their counterparts as an indication of what I have said concerning the pure affirmation of the oneness of Allah Almighty, as with:

- the prayer joined to the alms
- the call to prayer joined to the iqama
- Hajj joined to Umrah
- al-Safa joined to al-Marwa
- the obligatory act joined to the practice of the Sunnah
- the bowing joined to the prostration
- the testimony that there is no god but Allah joined to the testimony that Muhammad is the Messenger of Allah

and the like, which would take too long to enumerate — I say that sight too is of two kinds, each joined to the other: natural vision and vision of the soul.

Natural vision is that which occurs by means of the eye. It apprehends colors, such as white, black, red, yellow, and others, and shapes, such as the round, the triangular, the square, the five-sided, and so on. There is no need to argue for it, since it falls under the senses. Vision of the soul is that which occurs by way of knowledge, through the heart and the soul, and it apprehends what natural vision does not apprehend — those causes that are hidden from its perception. Allah said:

> مَا كَذَبَ ٱلْفُؤَادُ مَا رَأَىٰٓ
>
> "The heart did not lie about what it saw."

And He said:

> ألم ترى ربك كيف مد الظل
>
> "Have you not seen how your Lord extended the shadow?"

And He said:

> ألم ترى كيف فعل ربك بأصحاب الفيل
>
> "Have you not seen what your Lord did with the companions of the elephant?"

We know that the Prophet (ع) was not alive in the time of the companions of the elephant and could not have witnessed them with natural sight; what is meant here is the vision of the soul — sight that comes by way of the heart and of knowledge. The same sense underlies the common proverb that a certain person is blind of heart, meaning he is dull and backward in knowing things and in perceiving them when they lie beyond his eyes.

Since what I have set out is sound — that sight is of two kinds — and since all things are divided into two states and two aspects so that the oneness of Allah may stand pure and nothing may share with Him in that oneness, I say:

- Natural vision, which comes by way of the eye, apprehends of things only what is corporeal and falls under limits and time, such as colors and shapes.
- Vision of the soul, which comes by way of the heart and of knowledge, apprehends what natural vision does not apprehend and what it is too weak to grasp of all that is hidden from the eyes.

From this follows the judgment that vision of the soul is more exalted than natural vision, finer and more subtle in its apprehension. Its act is to know things as they truly are — unlike the eye. If a man were to take a single drop from a pool of water, vision of the soul would judge that the quantity of that water had decreased by the measure of the drop, and would see this with a sound seeing, while that decrease would not register for the sense that comes by way of the eye and of sight.

Since vision of the soul is the prior and the more subtle in apprehension, holding fast to it in fulfilling the obligations of Allah Almighty is more binding and more fitting than holding fast to anything else. To confine oneself to what is not the more excellent when the more excellent is present belongs to the judgments of ignorance, not to the judgment of reason. What truly binds people is to take hold of the more precise and the more excellent — by which I mean the vision of knowledge and of the soul.

Furthermore, if a man were to know in his heart that the time of a prayer had come, yet had not heard the sound of the call to prayer, that prayer would still be binding upon him. Likewise, if one taking the meal before dawn were to see with his heart and know that the dawn had broken, while his eyesight was barred from seeing it by some barrier that stood in the way, abstaining from food would be binding upon him even though his eye had not seen it. Since this is so, the fast and the abstaining from food are binding upon the one taking that meal because of his knowledge that the dawn has broken, even though his eyesight is barred from seeing it by whatever bars it.

By the same token, I say that entry into the fast is binding once knowledge is obtained that the cause of the fast — the moon's separation from the greatest luminary after their conjunction — has come to pass, even if sight is barred from seeing it by any condition whatever, whether light, cloud, dust, or a mountain. It is agreed that when the cause is realized, its effect becomes necessary by its necessity, so that neither precedes the other except in rank. The people of the call, therefore, in their entry into the fast, act by the judgment of the vision of the soul, in which error scarcely occurs, upon clear evidence and knowledge.

Then, since the Messenger (ع) made the fast obligatory in certain months of the year and informed us that Allah Almighty has made the sun and the moon a means to the knowledge of years and months, their times and their crescents, Allah said:

> هُوَ ٱلَّذِى جَعَلَ ٱلشَّمْسَ ضِيَآءًۭ وَٱلْقَمَرَ نُورًۭا وَقَدَّرَهُۥ مَنَازِلَ لِتَعْلَمُوا۟ عَدَدَ ٱلسِّنِينَ وَٱلْحِسَابَ
>
> "He is the One who made the sun a radiance and the moon a light, and determined phases for it so that you might know the number of the years and the reckoning."

He also said:

> ويسألونك عن الأهلة قل هي مواقيت للناس
>
> "They ask you about the crescents. Say: they are appointed times for the people."

The year is twelve months, as Allah said:

> إِنَّ عِدَّةَ ٱلشُّهُورِ عِندَ ٱللَّهِ ٱثْنَا عَشَرَ شَهْرًۭا فِى كِتَبِ ٱللَّهِ يَوْمَ خَلَقَ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضَ
>
> "The number of the months with Allah is twelve months in the Book of Allah, from the day He created the heavens and the earth."

The year is complete only when that full count has passed. A month comes into being — and its days are completed — only through the moon's travel: from the point where the moon and sun share the same degree and minute of arc, which the people of the stars call the conjunction, all the way around the sphere and back to that same alignment. From this we understand that when the moon's degree and minute within a zodiacal sign match the degree and minute of the sun within it, the moon has completed its course for the past month; and when it parts from the sun, its course belongs to the coming month. As the moon travels toward conjunction and separation, it passes through states in which the light on the half of its body facing the center — which is the earth — decreases, until at the moment of conjunction the upper half of its body, on the side facing the encompassing sphere, is illuminated, just as the whole of the half facing the center is illuminated on the night of its opposition. Then light appears in half its body at the separation and increases in proportion to its distance, through causes that the eye is too weak to fully apprehend.

I say that the eye's inability to apprehend all these states, and to see the illuminated portion of the moon's body as it travels toward conjunction at the month's end and at its separation at the month's beginning, arises from nothing other than the condition of the sun's light, which bars the eye from apprehension. In barring the eye from seeing the moon at that time, the sun's light acts just like the other causes that intervene and prevent the eye from seeing it: cloud, dust, a wall, or a mountain.

Do you not see that the stars are invisible by day because of the sun's radiance and overpowering brightness, and that when the sun is eclipsed the eye perceives them again, because the eclipse reduces the light? Since it is established that the illuminated measure of the moon's body at its separation from the sun would be seen by the eye were it not for the sun's light and rays, then that light is the barrier and the preventer — just like cloud and the turbidity of the air. And since the sun's light bars the eye from seeing the crescent after its separation from it, the soul, through knowledge, is not barred from seeing it as it truly is. The fast therefore becomes binding by the vision of knowledge, which is the more exalted, as the proofs I have established make clear. By these testimonies, the people of the guiding call are upon truth and right when they enter the fast by the soul's vision. And if they are upon the right, then whoever opposes them is in error.

Then I say to the hostile party and the rest of the opponents: from where have you made it obligatory that seeing means the seeing of the eye alone, excluding the seeing of the heart and of knowledge, when the proofs stand that seeing is of two kinds — a point none can deny except one who is openly obstinate? If you say that a man knows nothing until his sight falls upon it, and that when he has beheld it the judgment of the vision of knowledge concerning it is established for him, I say that this argument of yours is corrupt and does not hold, from several sides:

1. The first: if I ask you, have you seen Allah Almighty, then upon this principle of yours you are bound to say no. So I say: then why have you affirmed Him, when you have laid it down that a man knows nothing until his sight falls upon it, and your sight has not fallen upon Him, exalted and magnified as He is? You are therefore bound to retreat from the doctrine of the eye to the matter of knowledge, and to say: we knew, and it was established for us by proofs, and so we affirmed it. And that is precisely my position and my foundation.

2. The second: the matter of the world and its origination in time. If I were to question you about it, you would be bound to rule upon its origination by the judgment of the vision of knowledge and proof, not by the judgment of the vision of the eye. And that is my position.

3. The third: I say, what do you say concerning a man who broke his fast without having seen the crescent with his eye, because a barrier intervened, when thirty days of the month of Ramadan had passed? Was his breaking of the fast valid or not? If you say it was valid, I say: how so, when he did not behold the crescent with the eye, and the foundation of your school is that fasting and breaking the fast are not permitted except after seeing the crescent with the eye? You are then bound to say: we knew that after thirty days the crescent is seen, and that a barrier merely stood in the way of seeing it. And I say to you: you have abandoned the judgment of the vision of the eye and returned of necessity to the matter of the vision of knowledge — and that is my position. And if you say it was not valid, then what binds you binds you still.

Beyond this, whatever is known of things only by way of the senses, which include sight and the rest, is no more than the substance of corporeal things in their form and their quality alone. Once the soul has drawn upon the senses in coming to know a thing for the first time, it has no need to draw upon them a second time. The outcome of the senses and their yield is that they become dispensable, since the soul stands by its own essence in their place. For the soul in man is one of the instruments by which he apprehends.

Within man the soul stands alongside hearing, sight, smell, taste, and touch as an instrument of perception, and it possesses a capacity that none of the others possesses: after the images of their objects have come to it, it rises above them all and perceives what hearing, sight, smell, taste, and touch do not perceive. Just as hearing perceives what sight, smell, taste, and touch do not perceive, and smell perceives what hearing, sight, taste, and touch do not perceive, so the soul perceives what lies beyond all of them. In the senses there is a wisdom from Allah Almighty: that man be not deprived of anything he cannot otherwise apprehend of what Allah has brought into being in the worlds, and that what one instrument apprehends not be lost to him because another cannot reach it.

The soul's perception is therefore of two kinds:

1. The first is perception by means of the five senses — such as its perception of colors and shapes by way of sight, of sounds that signify and sounds that do not signify by way of hearing, and whatever else belongs to the remaining senses; and it is from this that the soul draws its inference toward what the senses do not perceive.

2. The second is perception by the soul's own essence along the path of inference, which stands in place of all that it apprehends by way of the senses — as its perception that the world is originated, after it had first apprehended the world by way of the senses. To what the soul perceives by its own essence along the path of inference, the senses have no road at all.

What the soul perceives by way of the senses it needs them for only in the first instance; it does not need a second perception from their side when it has occasion to know that thing again, for it has received the image of the sensed thing upon its own form at the first apprehension, and that image is present to it within itself whenever it returns to it, without any need to call upon the senses once more. So it is with the image of the planets in themselves and in their quality: once the soul has apprehended them by way of sight, that seeing makes a second seeing unnecessary, since the image is held in the soul and preserved by its having received it. Thereafter the soul stands in the place of the senses by its own essence, as the soul of one whose eyes have gathered the forms of visible things has no need of a second apprehension by the eyes, the image remaining in what belongs properly to the soul, standing upon its form without alteration.

What is meant in seeking the crescent and sighting it with the eye every month and every year is not that one should learn its figure in its form, its thinness and its thickness, for the knowledge of that would indeed require sight, which is the instrument for apprehending such things. What is meant in seeking it is to know the fact of its being in the west before the sun after it had been in the east behind her. And the apprehension of this fact of its being in the west belongs to the soul, not to sight, for sight, even when every obstruction is lifted, apprehends nothing but shapes and colors.

Since sight apprehends nothing but shapes and colors, and the seeking of the crescent is not for knowledge of its color and its shape, the matter is settled. For if it were sought in order to know its color and its shape, it would be absurd to seek it at all, since knowledge of the quality of its form, its figure, and its color is already possessed from earlier seeing by the eye, together with the certainty that it will not fail so long as the heavens and the earth endure, and that its image as apprehended by sight will not change from roundness of shape and the lighting of the edge of its body into squareness of shape and the lighting of the whole of its body. Nor is it sought because the obligation of fasting depends upon a condition of thinness or thickness in the luminous body. For if it were sought in order to know its thinness and its thickness, it would be absurd that the one who sees it should fast when it is thin while the condition is thickness, or fast when it is thick while the condition is thinness. No such condition of shape, color, thinness, or thickness exists in the obligation of fasting. It is therefore void that the obligation of fasting should be attached to apprehending the crescent by sight; and once that is void, it is established that it is attached to the soul's apprehension of it as being before the sun. For the proof has been given that man knows by way of inference, from what he has apprehended of bodily substances through his senses, what he does not apprehend through them. So the knowledge does not come about by sight, and fasting is attached to the vision of the soul and to nothing else; and the people of the dawah, in taking what the vision of the soul requires when they enter the fast, are upon a sound way and a straight path.

Further, the seeing of the eye sometimes falls into error concerning things as they truly are, by reason of what befalls it, so that it sees the large as small and the small as large, the thin as thick and the thick as thin; and sometimes a thing is seen only from near, or only from far. But the seeing of knowledge scarcely errs in what it encompasses. To take, in the discharge of the obligatory duties, that which does not err is worthier in the judgment of those endowed with understanding than to take that which errs. The people of the true call, then, in entering the fast, have taken hold of that which does not err, upon clarity and upon truth.

As for the saying of the Prophet,

> "صُومُوا لِرُؤْيَتِهِ وَأَفْطِرُوا لِرُؤْيَتِهِ"
>
> "Fast for its sighting and break the fast for its sighting,"

outwardly it does not escape two faces. Either he intended by it that the fasting of the community should begin from the moment the crescent is seen with the eye, as the opponents say, or that the crescent is seen with the eye while the community is already fasting, as we say. If he intended that the fasting of the community begin from the moment of the crescent's being seen by the eye, then what is the meaning of his saying لرؤيته, and what is the reason for his restricting himself, out of all the modes of expression, to the letter اللام (al-lam) rather than to another of the letters used in expression, he who took pride in his speech and said,

> "أَنَا أَفْصَحُ الْعَرَبِ وَلَا فَخْرَ"
>
> "I am the most eloquent of the Arabs, and this is no boast"?

Was his use of this lam here — saying لرؤيته rather than omitting it — for any purpose other than that the community should already be fasting when the crescent is seen by the eye? For the sense of lam, as it is used and as it is intended, is that the act must precede that for which it is done. Consider the following:

- When one says to a company, "Set such a thing in order for the coming of so-and-so," the meaning is that the thing is to be already in order at the time of his arrival.
- When one says, "Compose for the meeting of so-and-so a poem that you may recite to him," the meaning is that they meet him with the poem already composed and spoken — not that they begin composing it from the moment they meet him.
- When one says, "Work for the Hereafter," the meaning is that they are to put the work forward before the time of the Hereafter, for it is known that in the Hereafter there is no work.

By these witnesses it is sound that the letter lam was used for nothing other than that the people should already be fasting when the crescent is seen with the eye. And if the intention of the Prophet was that the crescent be seen while the community is fasting, that is what we have said and for which we have established the proof.

If an opponent objects and says, "What do you say against the lam in the saying of the Prophet لرؤيته being like the lam in the saying of Allah Almighty,

> 'أَقِمِ ٱلصَّلَوٰةَ لِدُلُوكِ ٱلشَّمْسِ

'Establish the prayer at the declining of the sun,'

so that the fast need not precede the sighting, just as the prayer need not precede the declining of the sun?" we say: the account of the lam in the saying of Allah Almighty is the same as the account of the lam in the saying of the Prophet. For the declining of the sun is its departure from the dome of the sky, as it has been explained, and the apprehension of that departure is of two kinds: an apprehension belonging to the soul and an apprehension belonging to nature. The apprehension belonging to the soul precedes the apprehension belonging to nature, because the sun's departure in reality from the dome of the sky is perceived only by the vision of the soul, by way of knowledge. Natural vision is too narrow to apprehend it until the sun has parted from the point of the zenith in descent and has come clear of that region in its descending, at the intersection of the line of the circle with the straight line, as we have set forth in the figure: the earth at the center, the east and the west, the meridian line, the circle of elevation, the straight line that cuts across, and the tangent of the circle upon the straight line — with the place of the vision of the soul of the declining marked at the point ب and the place of the sensible vision of the declining marked at the point ج.

Only then does sense apprehend it. The movement of the sun and of its shadow falls under sense by its rising in the circle when it comes up from the east and by its descending in the circle toward its setting; where there is rising and descending, sense apprehends the shortening and lengthening of the shadow. But when the sun has come to where it has no elevation — while it is at the point of declining and descending — sense grows weary of apprehending its course and its movement, because it has reached the zenith in such a way that its motion is concealed from sense and hidden from it, and one who has no knowledge supposes that it has halted. It is hidden from sense only because the sun has come to stand over the place from which it is at its farthest remove in height, and its motion in its passage through the zenith has become, from the line of the circle, a motion upon a level line, where the plane touches the straight line of the circle. That is from the place of the line أ — the point where the line of the circle and the level line intersect — from the eastern side to the line on the western side. Sense does not discern the shortening and lengthening of the shadow, since that comes about only by elevation in height and by descent, and there the sun has neither elevation nor descent but moves upon a level line.

This being so, the true declining by which the prayer becomes obligatory is at the arrival of the sun's disk from the line of the circle at the line ب, which is seen by the vision of the soul and is the sign of the line of the half of what is cut off by the day — not at its arrival at the line ج, which is seen by natural vision and is more than the half. For at the time it reaches the line ب it has begun to descend, even if sense does not apprehend its shadow or its movement in its place, since it is upon a level line, and this is not made plain to sense until after it has reached the intersection of the line of the circle and the level line. The account of the lam in "at the declining of the sun," then, is the same as the account of the lam in "for its sighting." It follows that when the shadow turns, the one who prays is already within his prayer — just as when the crescent appears and sense apprehends it, the one who fasts is already within his fast — and this is the more careful course in the performance of the obligatory duties. By these witnesses the objection is void.

If he then says, "If the matter is as you have described, and fasting is binding by the vision of the soul according to what you have brought forward, what is the meaning of the saying of the Prophet,

> 'فَإِنْ غُمَّ عَلَيْكُمْ فَعُدُّوا ثَلَاثِينَ يَوْماً

'If it is obscured from you, then count thirty days,'

and what is that but a requiring of the seeing of the eye?" we say: we have established the vision of the soul by the witnesses we have brought forward, and that it is the nobler of the two.

## 33. The Sun, the Moon, and the Order of Obedience

It is the nobler of the two, and the more fitting to be relied upon in the performance of obligatory duties. In saying this, we have not set aside the ruling of the sighting of the eye (رُؤْيَةُ الْعَيْنِ), for these two sightings are both foundations in the performance of obligatory duties — one simply more excellent than the other. The more excellent belongs to the select, those who go before others by their knowledge: they are the possessors of knowledge (أُولُوا الْعِلْم). The other belongs to the generality, whose ignorance obliges them to follow the people of knowledge — those who are bare of understanding. The relation between the two sightings is like the relation between the two times of a prayer, the first of which is more excellent than the second.

So it is with all matters: they come in two kinds, hidden and apparent. The hidden is known only by the select among the excellent, while the apparent is known by both the select and the generality alike. The select are obliged to carry out what follows from what they have come to know, just as the common people are obliged to carry out what follows from what they have come to know. This being so, what is there to deny in the following: that a learned man, by the excellence of his knowledge, is bound to fast by the hidden vision of the soul (الرُّؤْيَةُ النَّفْسَانِيَّةُ), and that an ignorant man is bound to follow the learned man in that? It is precisely as with the one taking the pre-dawn meal who knows, by his hidden knowledge, that dawn has broken — for whom food, drink, and marital relations become forbidden, and fasting becomes binding — while the one taking the pre-dawn meal who is ignorant of the dawn must follow the learned man in that.

As for the saying of the Prophet (ع),

> فَإِنْ غُمَّ عَلَيْكُمْ فَعُدُّوا ثَلَاثِينَ يَوْماً

"If it is obscured from you, then count thirty days" — upon consideration, it can scarcely be held that what he intended by it was the sighting of the eye. For fasting by the sight of the eye, when the impediments of cloud and the like are lifted, can hardly ever yield months of thirty days; a month may well be twenty-nine days. And if that is so, it is not permissible that the Prophet (ع) should bind his community, in one of the commands of Allah Almighty, to a rule in which doubt may arise — making this the cause of an addition to an obligation or a subtraction from it, and leaving his community to perform it as though walking in the dark. Nor is it permissible that he should command his community to fast an added day in one of the deficient months on account of cloud, when, had the cloud not been there, it would have been known that fasting on that day is forbidden and that breaking the fast is required. For fasting is worship of Allah Almighty, and it belongs to the religion by which Allah is served; His servants are commanded to sincerity in it, by the saying of Allah Almighty:

> وَمَآ أُمِرُوٓا۟ إِلَّا لِيَعْبُدُوا۟ ٱللَّهَ مُخْلِصِينَ لَهُ ٱلدِّينَ
>
> "They were commanded only to worship Allah, sincere to Him in religion."

Sincerity is other than doubt and confusion. And since sincerity is other than doubt and confusion, and since doubt and confusion have entered into this rule and disturbed it, we say that the Prophet (ع) did not call people to doubt and confusion in the worship of Allah Almighty. And since the Prophet (ع) did not call to doubt and confusion, his restricting the days of the month to the fullest and most complete number — without deficiency and without the lesser count — when by the requirement of the sight of the eye the days of a month may be deficient and doubt may fall upon it, can only have been an indication pointing toward the vision of the soul, whose numbers neither increase nor decrease. It was an urging to take hold of what Allah Almighty called for in the completing of the count, in His saying:

> وَلِتُكْمِلُوا۟ ٱلْعِدَّةَ
>
> "And that you may complete the count."

Then we say: from where comes the assurance of a people whose fast, when cloud and impediment occur, rests upon that rule — the assurance that the day they counted as belonging to Ramadan or to Shaban, under a clouded sky, is indeed as they counted and reckoned it, when that very same day, for another people of their own school in taking the sight of the eye, is otherwise, because the impediments of cloud and the like have been lifted for them? So, for example, there is cloud in Kirman, and their fast differs from the fast of Shiraz, where, say, there is no cloud. And if it were permissible for the time of the fast to differ among the people of a single region — so that fasting becomes obligatory upon one people on a given day and upon another people on the following day — then it would likewise be permissible for the time of prayer to differ among the people of a single town, so that one and the same prayer becomes binding upon one man at one time and upon another man at another time. Since that is not permissible, and since, when the time of a prayer has come, the community is bound to establish it whether a man establishes it or abandons it, so likewise, when the time of the fast has come, the fast is binding upon the community, whether a man fasts or breaks it.

This being so, the saying of the Prophet (ع) is an indication given by him — knowing that error may fall in the sighting of the eye — pointing to that in which error does not fall: namely, the vision of the soul, which is knowledge, and its count, which belongs among the matters he commended, that is, the completing of the count. Further, his saying, "If it is obscured from you, then count thirty," requires, for entering the fast, that the counting be from the days of Shaban, and, for leaving it by breaking the fast, that the counting be from the days of Ramadan. Now it is known that counting is not possible except by having something to count and knowing its first day. It is also known that in most cases the crescent is not seen at the beginnings of the months, on account of impediments that prevent the sight from seeing it — especially in places whose air is turbid, such as Tabaristan, the mountain regions, the coasts, and the islands of the sea. And if it is possible that the crescent is not seen by the eye at the beginning of Shaban, and is not seen because of some obstacle that occurs, and its first day is not known, then how is one to count from it? And how is one to count from Ramadan when its first day has not been verified? To carry this report upon the meaning of the sighting of the eye requires that the Prophet (ع) cast his community into disagreement and called them to what lies beyond their capacity — namely, the counting of the days of a month whose beginning is not known and in which the crescent is not seen. Yet Allah Almighty says:

> لَا يُكَلِّفُ ٱللَّهُ نَفْسًا إِلَّا وُسْعَهَا
>
> "Allah does not burden a soul beyond its capacity."

Since such a thing cannot be imagined of the Prophet (ع) — who stands far removed and sanctified above burdening Allah's servants with what lies beyond them or casting them into disagreement — it must follow that either the report "If it is obscured from you, then count thirty days" is not sound, or that if the Prophet (ع) did say it, it must be taken in the one sense that is sound and that removes what is objectionable, and in no other sense. And since this is necessary, and the testimonies have established that what is sound — and what creation is called to — is the vision of the soul, which is knowledge, we say: the discussion has now made clear that what is intended is other than the sighting of the eye, that the common people, who are limited in knowledge, are bound to follow, and that the objection raised in this matter has been overturned.

Then we say: which of the pillars of Islam that Muhammad (ع) brought has ever had its affair entrusted to the community — a community that is not preserved from error and that falls into slip and mistake? Prayer? Fasting? Zakat? Hajj? Jihad? That fasting alone should forever be committed to them, in the seeking of the crescent and the knowing of its times? Is not prayer the pillar of religion and the face of Islam, and are not all its acts — the opening takbir, the recitation, the bowing, the prostration, and the salutation — committed to the Imam who stands in the place of the Messenger (ع) in leading the people in prayer, and not to those who follow him? Upon those who follow lies the following of the Imam in these acts, without their own preference being followed in advancing any of its acts or in delaying any of its limits; and whoever among them does this — advancing or delaying — his prayer is deficient (خِدَاج). Is not the collecting of zakat committed to the Imam who stands in the place of the Messenger (ع), so that setting it aside or turning it away from him is not permissible, and the distributing of what comes of it belongs to him among those who respond to him, and not to the community, as it was in the days of the Prophet (ع)? Whoever gives his zakat to one other than him who stands in the place of the Messenger (ع) must render it a second time, just as one who, in the days of the Prophet (ع), gave his zakat to another than him would have been bound to render it to him a second time, because he placed it where it did not belong. Is not the Hajj incomplete except with the Imam who goes before the assembly, so that they are bound to follow him in the rites he performs, and whoever sets himself apart by advancing one rite before another or by delaying it, his Hajj is deficient? Is not the ordering of jihad in the way of Allah committed to the one entrusted with the affair of the Muslims who fight — in preserving their order and all their condition, in leading them forward in battle and in withdrawal, and in seeking what is best in truce and in fighting? Upon the community that fights lies the following of the one who commands among them by the command of Allah, and not the following of their own desires in peace and in war; and whoever does anything of that fighting without his command and sheds blood without his permission is held answerable for it.

This being so, and the foundations of Islam being committed to one who preserves them, the community being obliged to follow him in them, from where has it become permissible for these people to follow their own opinions in the entering of the fast — which is one of those foundations — without following in it the one who stands in the place of the Messenger (ع)? Is this severance of theirs, this entering of the fast by their own ijtihad — which brings them to make obligatory what is not an obligation, to abandon an obligation, or to count it a Sunnah — anything other than presumption against Allah, or a departure from the safeguarding of His command? We seek refuge in Allah from that.

If he says: "On the principle you have laid down for the entering of the fast, namely conjunction and separation, the day on which conjunction and separation fall must be partly of Shaban and partly of Ramadan, and the fasting of that part becomes binding; and this is a contradiction, which the Prophet (ع) did not command," we say: the Prophet (ع) imposed the obligations on behalf of Allah, and appointed for each obligation two causes by which it becomes due and binding. The first is a preceding cause, by which the second cause is made valid; the second is the cause by which one obligation is distinguished from another. The first is general — it is a cause for this obligation and for others besides it, so that in performing the obligation one does not take hold of it alone but of the second. The second follows upon the general one and is specific — it belongs to that obligation alone, and by it the obligation is set apart from all else.

- **Prayer:** Since this prayer is not obligatory except by day, the day is its preceding general cause — a cause for this prayer and for the other prayers performed by day alike. Then, since this prayer is not obligatory by the mere occurrence of that preceding cause, which is the day, except when the sun declines from the zenith, the declining of the sun is a second cause by which the prayer becomes obligatory and by which one takes hold in performing it; and it is specific.

- **Zakat:** Since zakat is not obligatory except upon wealth, wealth is the cause of the obligation of zakat, and it is a preceding general cause. Then, since zakat is not obligatory by the mere occurrence of that preceding cause, which is wealth, except when a year has passed over it, the year is a second cause by which it becomes due and by which one takes hold in performing it; and it is specific.

- **Hajj:** Since the Hajj is not obligatory except with ability, ability is its preceding general cause. Then, since a man does not become obligated to perform the Hajj by the mere occurrence of ability — the preceding cause — except on the greatest day at Mecca, that day is a second cause by which one takes hold in fulfilling it; and it is specific.

- **Jihad:** Since jihad is not obligatory except with the occurrence of opposition, the raising of the standard of disobedience, and the refusal of obedience, opposition is the preceding general cause. Then, since jihad is not obligatory by the mere existence of opposition and refusal of obedience except through the standing of the one who holds the place of the Messenger (ع) and a head who leads the community into battle, that is a second cause; and it is specific.

Since that is so, we say that fasting too has two causes. The first is preceding and general: the separation of the moon from the sun. The second is specific: the time of dawn at which entering into the fast becomes obligatory. And just as prayer is not obligatory — though it be day — except at the declining of the sun, and that is its time; nor zakat — though there be wealth — except when a year has passed over it, and that is its time; nor Hajj — though there be ability — except at Mecca on the greatest day, and that is its time; nor jihad — though there be opposition — except through the ordering head who stands in the place of the Lord of the worlds, and he is its support; so likewise fasting is not obligatory — though the moon has separated from the sun — except at the dawn that follows upon that separation, and that is its time. His saying in objection — that part of the day is of Shaban and part of it is of Ramadan and that the fasting of that part is consequently binding — is thus overturned.

What adds to the certainty of this, along with the proofs already established from the apparent, is the greatest testimony, which speaks to the truthfulness of the Messenger of Allah (ع) in what he brought from Allah Almighty: the balance between what he laid down of the Law and the religious ordinances and what Allah Almighty created and formed in the horizons and in the souls, as stated in the saying of Allah:

> سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ
>
> "We shall show them Our signs in the horizons and in their own souls, until it becomes clear to them that it is the truth."

No human being has the power to imitate him in such a work, save one whose lineage is in the holy precinct (حَظِيرَةُ الْقُدْسِ) and whose soul has been given readiness and endowment for receiving the revelation of Allah Almighty, when one returns to the interpretation, which is one of the two divisions to which Allah commanded His Prophet (ع) to call His servants in His worship, in His saying:

> ٱدْعُ إِلَىٰ سَبِيلِ رَبِّكَ بِٱلْحِكْمَةِ
>
> "Call to the way of your Lord with wisdom."

This wisdom has two divisions:

- The work: the outward, natural worship suited to natural bodies, carried out through the obligations and practices the Prophet (ع) brought, and requiring interpretation — that is, knowledge.
- The knowledge: the greatest form of worship, the hidden worship of the soul, suited to particular souls. It is attained by holding to the truths concerning how to purify the affirmation of Allah's oneness, and by so doing it confirms the outward natural worship, which is the work.

In the interpretive call, the sun — in that it is the noblest and most luminous of bodies, the source of light for all others, the cause of growth among offspring in the world of nature, and the cause to which all the causes of the world are attached — has been made an indication pointing to knowledge of the rank of the Prophet (ع). Just as the sun is the noblest and most luminous of bodies, so the soul of the Speaker is the noblest of souls and the most knowing of them. Just as the sun is the source of light for the other stars, so the soul of the Speaker (ع) is the source for the souls of men in the divine sciences that lead to the pure affirmation of oneness. Just as the sun is the cause of growth among offspring in the world of nature, so the Prophet (ع) is the cause of growth among spiritual offspring in the world of the soul. And just as the sun is the cause to which the causes of the bodily world are attached, so the Prophet (ع) is the cause to which the causes of the world of religion are attached, which is the worship of Allah Almighty. The one is in the body, and the other is in the soul.

As for the moon: its rank falls below the sun; it receives more of the sun's light than the other stars receive; it gives light in the darkness of the night; and it stands as an intermediary in the growth of offspring between the earth and the sun, both receiving and bestowing. For these reasons, the moon has been made an indication pointing to the rank of the Foundation (الْأَسَاس). Just as the moon's rank in light falls below the sun, so the rank of the Foundation in receiving the radiance of the world of origination falls below the Prophet (ع).

I have said that the moon receives more of the sun's light than any other of the wandering bodies; and in just the same way, the Foundation (الْأَسَاس) receives from the Speaker (اَلنَّاطِق) more of the blessings of the affirmation of Allah's oneness than any other recipient does.
