Just as the moon gives light in the darkness of the night, so the Foundation makes plain what the Prophet (ع) brought of the sealed symbols, the law set out in its breadth, and the parables that were struck. And just as the moon is the intermediary between the sun and the other bodies in the growth of offspring, so the Foundation is the intermediary between the Speaker and the community in the growth of spiritual offspring in the world of the soul, both conferring benefit and receiving it.

Now that the states of the bodily sun and moon in the world of bodies have been set beside the states of the spiritual sun and moon in the world of religion, I say that the moon has several states:

- When it comes into conjunction with the sun, the upper half of its body — the half that faces the sun — is illumined. This is what the astronomers mean when they say that a power extends to it, by which everything that is to come into being of events and existing things is brought into being until the second conjunction, by a measure ordained from the Mighty, the All-Knowing. It is like a minister who comes into the presence of the king: the king charges him concerning the affairs of the kingdom with what he is to carry out once he has left the royal presence. So it is with the Prophet (ع) teaching the Foundation (ع) what he wished to teach him, that he might stand in the Prophet's place in guidance and instruction — the Foundation receiving from him in his days, conferring nothing before that designation. It is for this reason that the Prophet (ع) pointed to Ali ibn Abi Talib (ع) and said:

> "أَنَا مَدِينَةُ الْعِلْمِ وَعَلِيٌّ بَابُهَا فَمَنْ أَرَادَ الْعِلْمَ فَلْيَأْتِ الْبَابَ"
>
> "I am the city of knowledge and Ali is its gate. Whoever desires knowledge, let him come to the gate."

- After the parting, the moon falls into hiddenness, lying beneath the light of the sun, concealed from the sight of the generality of people and seen only by those possessed of knowledge. This is like the hiddenness of the affair of the Foundation — his rank and the obligation of obedience to him, established by means of the indications made toward him — hidden from the people of ignorance and from the common people, and disclosed only to those of merit and special standing.

- When the sun has reached its setting place, the moon appears as a crescent so that the great and the small alike behold it. This is like the manifestation of the Foundation's affair through what came from the Speaker (ع) in the explicit text concerning him and the imposing of obedience to him, when the time of his own departure from the world drew near, so that the small and the great came to know it.

- The moon has no light during the day while the disc of the sun stands above the earth, and has light only after the sun has set. This is like the Foundation, who has no affair during the lifetime of the Speaker (ع), and only after his departure from the world.

- At the beginning of its appearance it is faint, seen only by one whose sight is strong, and people fall into disagreement over the sighting of it. This is like the weakness of the affair of the Foundation at the beginning of its season, the arising of disagreement concerning his Imamate, and the shrinking back of the community from binding themselves to obedience to him, except for one who possessed sincerity and knowledge.

- Its light increases with the passing of the days. So too the affair of his summons rises and his Imamate spreads among the people with the passing of the years.

- When fourteen days of it have passed, its light is complete, so that the small and the great see it and no one doubts the sighting of it. This is like the light of his Imamate reaching all the nations at the passing of the seven repetitions (اَلْمَثَانِي السَّبْعَةُ) at the end of the cycle, so that at that time no one doubts his Imamate and his precedence.

Then there is the fact that the people of knowledge and faith assign the day that falls within the concealment after the parting to the coming month — just as one of sound faith held himself bound, by what he knew of the indications of the Prophet (ع) given to a few in particular, to obedience to the Commander of the Faithful before the explicit text was given outwardly for all. The people of ignorance and the common people, by contrast, assign that day — whose fast is obligatory — to the month now past, whose fast is not obligatory, through the scantness of their knowledge, and so they fail in fasting it until they see the crescent with the eye. In the same way, they assign to the Foundation — obedience to whom is obligatory — the same standing as any other to whom obedience is not made obligatory, neither by indication nor by explicit text, and they fail in obedience to him out of ignorance of its being binding upon them, until the truth of his Imamate becomes plain to them.

The month of Ramadan is, among the months of the year, the noblest of them and the greatest in weight. It stands for the Foundation, who is placed ahead of the rest of the companions, whose rank was ennobled by exposition and interpretation — which is knowledge — who was pointed to by the indications, and who is the gathering place of the knowledge of all that the Prophet (ع) brought from Allah the Exalted. It is for this reason that Allah the Exalted said:

> "شَهْرُ رَمَضَانَ الَّذِي أُنْزِلَ فِيهِ الْقُرْآنُ هُدًى لِلنَّاسِ وَالْبَيِّنَاتِ مِنَ الْهُدَى وَالْفُرْقَانِ"
>
> "The month of Ramadan, in which the Quran was sent down as guidance for the people, and as clear proofs of guidance and of the criterion."

This verse was intended as an indication pointing to him. We know, after all, that the Quran was revealed over twenty-three years — not all at once in the month of Ramadan, but day by day, month by month, and year by year. To read this verse according to its outward sense leads directly to contradiction. Interpretation is therefore necessary, so that the word of Allah the Exalted and the word of the Messenger (ع) may each stand firm, together with the binding character of the fast and its obligation upon the sighting of the crescent: first, on the side of the soul, for the people of knowledge, at the very moment of their sighting; and last, on the side of the senses, for the people of ignorance. The same principle governs the obligation of obedience to the Foundation (ع) — when the Prophet (ع) raised him to this station and pointed to him, he did so first through indications understood by those with knowledge and discernment, and last through explicit text and formal declaration for the common people. The same principle also governs the obligation upon whoever is present to hold back from opening discourse and from teaching, even while the Foundation stands ahead of the assembly. It is for this that Allah the Exalted said:

> "لَا تَرْفَعُوٓا۟ أَصْوَتَكُمْ فَوْقَ صَوْتِ ٱلنَّبِىِّ"
>
> "Do not raise your voices above the voice of the Prophet."

This forbids the community from overstepping in a way that would drive out the ruling of Allah the Exalted and narrow it rather than widen it — by going ahead of the Messenger in his time, and ahead of the Foundation and whoever stands in their place in their own time — so that the Imam may be distinguished from those who follow him, and the head from those under him. Then He said:

> "من شهد منكم الشهر فليصمه"
>
> "Whoever among you witnesses the month, let him fast it."

This is an indication from the Speaker to the community that follows him: they are to follow the Foundation and to listen in silence to his speech. The meaning is that whoever attends the assembly of the Foundation in the knowledge of the outward and the inward, let him keep silent — for it is the Foundation who has been honored, who is placed ahead, and to whom the affair of religion is entrusted, and no one else. This is evidence that before obedience to him was made obligatory, the people used to discourse with one another in matters of religion and knowledge however they wished, and were then forbidden from it — just as before the fast and its obligation they used to eat however they liked, and were then forbidden from it by the imposing of the fast. It is like the command of the Prophet (ع) in the matter of marriage, when he said:

> "لَا نِكَاحَ إِلَّا بِوَلِيٍّ وَشَاهِدَيْنِ"
>
> "There is no marriage except with a guardian and two witnesses."

This shows that marriage had previously been contracted without a guardian or two witnesses — that is, temporary marriage (اَلْمُتْعَة) — however people wished, in the early days of Islam, and that they were then forbidden from this practice by this ruling.

Then His word:

> "وَمَن كَانَ مَرِيضًا أَوْ عَلَىٰ سَفَرٍۢ فَعِدَّةٌۭ مِّنْ أَيَّامٍ أُخَرَ"
>
> "And whoever is sick or on a journey, then a number of other days."

This is an exception that applies to all who witness the month. The meaning is that silence has been made obligatory upon all people when they attend the assembly of interpretation ascribed to the Foundation — except for one whose speech is for the sake of receiving benefit: a man in doubt, or a learner. In such a case, silence again becomes binding upon him afterward according to the rule. For attendance at that assembly is the prayer of the soul and of knowledge, and what is required in the prayer of works must equally be observed within it: the etiquettes, the customary practices, and the upholding of the rulings. I have also set down in the comprehensive treatise a portion concerning the fast, with explanation and clarification, to the measure of the benefits that have reached me from the side of the guardian of Allah (ع).

As for the month of Ramadan of the year four hundred — and the agreement of the people of truth to enter its fast two days before the crescent was sighted with the eye — this is a matter that, upon my life, is confusing to all but the people of understanding, given that no more than a single day of difference had ever been known to pass between the fast of the people of truth and the fast of the people of disagreement.

The reason is this: the conjunction that occurred in the month of Ramadan fell on the night of Monday, making Monday its first day. The crescent was not seen on Tuesday night by naked-eye sighting, because the sun's light overpowered its body. At the time of sunset, the moon stood at six degrees and some minutes from the sun — a distance equal to half the moon's body — and at such a distance the sight cannot reach it, since it falls beneath the ray that prevents sight from doing its work. It was therefore not permissible, out of propriety, to break the fast on that day, for the moon stood from the sun at the end of the month at a like number of degrees. Had one broken the fast then, what could have been fasted of the month of Ramadan would have amounted to only twenty-nine days — because the conjunction of the sun and the moon for Shawwal fell on the night of Wednesday, at two hours and some minutes into it, making Wednesday the day of the festival, on which fasting is forbidden. A failure in one of the obligatory duties would thus have occurred, since the month of Ramadan is thirty days without deficiency, by the word of our masters (عَلَيْهِمُ السَّلَامُ):

> "مَا تَمَّ شَعْبَانُ وَلَا نَقَصَ رَمَضَانُ"

The strongest proof of the soundness of our entering the fast from Monday is the word of the Prophet (ع):

> "يَوْمُ صَوْمِكُمْ يَوْمُ نَحْرِكُمْ"
>
> "The day of your fasting is the day of your sacrifice."

The sacrifice in the Sacred Precinct fell on Monday, the tenth of Dhu al-Hijjah of the year four hundred — according to what the sighting by knowledge requires — which is the ninth of it according to what the sighting by the eye requires. And the matter holds in this way every year, as he said (ع), without any disagreement arising.

If someone should say: what you have brought forward concerning the sun and the moon, the degrees, the distances, and the hours, and on which you have built your argument and your proof, belongs to the science of the stars, and the Prophet (ع) forbade taking it up and looking into it — I say: how is it that inference concerning the establishing of the time of the fast, and knowledge of it by way of the sun and the moon, which are the cause of the months and the years and which cannot be attained except through their motion, their course, their conjunction, and their separation, is forbidden to us, while inference concerning the establishing of the direction of prayer and the times of prayer, and the knowledge of them — which is like the fast in being among the pillars of Islam — by way of the mansions of the moon and the elevation of the sun with the measures we have set up for you, is commanded? Is this anything other than an unjust division (قِسْمَةٌ ضِيزَى)?

The Prophet (ع) forbade it only in a particular respect. It is that whoever begins the pursuit of the sciences by learning the science of the stars, without the divine and revealed sciences preceding it for him, is led by that path to what the followers of Ptolemy hold, and those who ascribe divinity to the heavenly bodies. But when the religious and revealed sciences have preceded it, then knowledge of the manner of the spheres and the stars, and of that for which they were composed, increases the soul in certainty concerning the oneness of Allah the Exalted, and gains for its conception of His unity a firmer faith. So too Allah the Exalted said:

> "لَخَلْقُ ٱلسَّمَوَتِ وَٱلْأَرْضِ أَكْبَرُ مِنْ خَلْقِ ٱلنَّاسِ وَلَكِنَّ أَكْثَرَ ٱلنَّاسِ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ"
>
> "The creation of the heavens and the earth is greater than the creation of mankind, but most people do not know."

And He said:

> "سَنُرِيهِمْ آيَتِنَا فِى ٱلأٓفَاقِ وَفِىٓ أَنفُسِهِمْ حَتَّىٰ يَتَبَيَّنَ لَهُمْ أَنَّهُ ٱلْحَقُّ"
>
> "We shall show them Our signs on the horizons and within themselves until it becomes clear to them that it is the truth."

The horizons are nothing other than the heavens and all they contain. Moreover, had the matter been as that objector claimed, Allah the Exalted would not have sworn by the stars, nor would He have described them as indicating His unity in His Book, where He says:

> "تَبَارَكَ ٱلَّذِى جَعَلَ فِى ٱلسَّمَآءِ بُرُوجًۭا وَجَعَلَ فِيهَا سِرَجًۭا وَقَمَرًۭا مُّنِيرًۭا"
>
> "Blessed is He who placed constellations in the sky and placed in it a lamp and an illuminating moon."

Nor would He have sworn, saying:

> "فلا أقسم بمواقع النجوم وأنه قسم لو تعلمون عظيم"
>
> "So I swear by the settings of the stars, and it is indeed a great oath, if you only knew."

And He said:

> "فَلَا أُقْسِمُ بِالْخُنَّسِ الْجَوَارِ الْكُنَّسِ"
>
> "So I swear by the receding stars, that run their course and hide."

And He said:

> "فَالْمُدَبِّرَاتِ أَمْرًا"
>
> "And those that arrange the affair."

And He said:

> "وَٱلسَّمَآءِ ذَاتِ ٱلْبُرُوجِ"
>
> "By the sky containing the constellations."

And He said:

> "وَالشَّمْسِ وَضُحَاهَا وَالْقَمَرِ إِذَا تَلَاهَا"

And He said:

> "هُوَ ٱلَّذِى جَعَلَ ٱلشَّمْسَ ضِيَآءًۭ وَٱلْقَمَرَ نُورًۭا وَقَدَّرَهُۥ مَنَازِلَ لِتَعْلَمُوا۟ عَدَدَ ٱلسِّنِينَ وَٱلْحِسَابَ"
>
> "It is He who made the sun a radiance and the moon a light, and determined for it stages, that you might know the number of the years and the reckoning."

What merit, then, is nobler than knowledge of the luminous stars in their circles — their positions at their highest and their lowest — which testify to the soundness of what Allah the Exalted has established of His religion, and bear witness to the truth of what the Prophet (ع) brought from Allah? And what knowledge stands higher, after the affirmation of oneness and the ranks of the hudud (الْحُدُود), than knowledge of what arises through the passage of the stars through their constellations — southward and northward — and through the conjunctions of their lights with one another, in the world of generation and corruption, in the appearance of offspring and in other events that point to the ranks of the friends of Allah?

> "فَهَلْ يَسْتَوِي الظُّلُمَاتُ وَالنُّورُ أَمْ هَلْ يَسْتَوِي الْأَعْمَى وَالْبَصِيرُ أَمْ هَلْ يَسْتَوِي الَّذِينَ يَعْلَمُونَ وَالَّذِينَ لَا يَعْلَمُونَ إِنَّمَا يَتَذَكَّرُ أُولُوا الْأَلْبَابِ"

Are darkness and light equal? Or are the blind and the seeing equal? Or are those who know and those who do not know equal? Only those of understanding take heed.

By the testimonies I have brought forward — outward and inward alike — it is now established that sighting is of two kinds; that choosing the more excellent of the two in fulfilling one's obligatory duties is the better course; and that when the Prophet (ع) used the letter ل (اَللَّام) in his words لرؤيته, he intended by it the sighting of knowledge — which requires that the people already be fasting when the crescent is sighted with the eye, and not otherwise.

Know this, then — and may Allah grant you success in obeying Him and obeying His friends — and take hold of the meanings of what I have set forth. Seek Allah's help in all your affairs, and draw near to Him through sincerity of intention and of inward purpose, both in your worship of Him and in your obedience to His friends, for He is the best helper toward whatever one seeks to know of the realities. And give thanks to Allah the Exalted for every blessing that reaches you from the side of His trustees.

Here I bring the treatise to its close, and I close it as it began — with praise belonging to Allah and with prayers upon those through whom His guidance reaches us. Nothing I have set down was mine to originate; it was only an ordering of what has been received. The fitting seal upon such a work is thanks, not claim.

> وَبَعْدُ فَقَدْ خَتَمْتُ الرِّسَالَةَ بِالْحَمْدِ لِلَّهِ الْعَلِيِّ الْأَعْلَى وَبِالصَّلَاةِ عَلَى النَّبِيِّ مُحَمَّدٍ الْمُصْطَفَى وَالْوَلِيِّ عَلِيٍّ الْمُرْتَضَى وَالْأَئِمَّةِ الطَّاهِرِينَ أَقْمَارِ الدُّجَى

I have sealed this treatise with praise of Allah, the High, the Most High, and with prayer upon the Prophet Muhammad the Chosen, upon the guardian Ali the Well-Pleasing, and upon the pure Imams, the moons amid the darkness.

That likeness is not an ornament. Just as the moon in the night sky carries a light it has received and gives it to those who travel while others sleep, so the pure Imams stand in the darkness of the age, luminous by what has been entrusted to them and not by what they claim for themselves. The same order continues unbroken in the Imam of our own time.

> وَإِمَامِ الزَّمَانِ مَوْلَانَا وَهَادِينَا وَوَلِيِّ نِعَمِنَا الْإِمَامِ الْحَاكِمِ بِأَمْرِ اللَّهِ أَمِيرِ الْمُؤْمِنِينَ صَلَوَاتُ اللَّهِ عَلَيْهِ وَعَلَى آبَائِهِ الطَّاهِرِينَ وَأَبْنَائِهِ الْأَكْرَمِينَ الْمُنْتَظَرِينَ إِلَى يَوْمِ الدِّينِ أَفْضَلُ التَّحِيَّاتِ وَالتَّسْلِيمِ

Upon the Imam of the age — our master, our guide, and the guardian of our blessings — the Imam who rules by the command of Allah, the Commander of the Faithful: may Allah's prayers be upon him, upon his pure forefathers, and upon his noble sons who are awaited until the Day of Judgment, the finest of greetings and salutations.

This is the order I have been tracing throughout: knowledge descends, obedience ascends, and the two meet in the one whom Allah has appointed. Whoever holds to that order is never left groping in the dark, for the light he walks by is not of his own making. And so the only request that remains is the request for steadfastness and for illumination, since neither lies within our power to produce.

> وَنَسْأَلُ اللَّهَ أَنْ يُثَبِّتَنَا عَلَى طَاعَتِهِ وَطَاعَةِ أَوْلِيَائِهِ وَيُنَوِّرَ عُقُولَنَا وَبَصَائِرَنَا بِمَنِّهِ وَكَرَمِهِ وَقُدْرَتِهِ إِنَّهُ خَيْرُ مَسْئُولٍ وَأَكْرَمُ مَأْمُولٍ وَحَسْبُنَا اللَّهُ وَنِعْمَ الْوَكِيلُ وَنِعْمَ الْمَوْلَى وَنِعْمَ النَّصِيرُ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَى سَيِّدِنَا مُحَمَّدٍ وَآلِهِ وَسَلَّمَ

We ask Allah to keep us steadfast in obedience to Him and to His friends, and to illumine our minds and our inner sight through His favor, His generosity, and His power. He is the finest of all who are asked and the most generous of all in whom hope is placed. Allah is our sufficiency — the best of guardians, the best of masters, and the best of helpers. May Allah bless our master Muhammad and his family, and grant them peace.
